### E9: PH6 Psi by sigma-way distribution (2026-11-16)

The prototype that section 5 defines as E9 (the owner authorised it on 2026-11-16, section 6c: "E4-E9, then 1b.7"; gates G1-G4 below were fixed by the orchestrator before the work; the abandon rule of the row E9 and of 4.6 applied). Code `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto/Ph6` (`PsiStream`, `BwtPass`, `IsaByDist`, `PsiFromIsa`, `PsiFiles`, `LinkSmoke`; commands `e9-bwt`, `e9-psi`, `e9-isa`, `e9-psi-isa`, `e9-oracle`, `e9-smoke`), tests `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/Ph6`, raw rows, design choices and all tables: [RESULTS-E9.md](../analysis/em/RESULTS-E9.md). What was built is PH6 as section 3.3 describes it: Psi from the BWT by one scan with one output cursor per symbol (`Psi[C[c] + k]` = row of the k-th occurrence of c), each cursor writing its own contiguous range strictly sequentially through its own mapped window, at most 64 cursors per pass and ceil(sigma / 64) passes above that; the row of the suffix 0 (the sentinel) skipped and recorded out of band, `PSI[0]` = 0. The BWT comes from **route (ii), a separate pass over the SA file and the packed text** (no change to the SA sink or to any existing code, so there is nothing to prove "flag off unchanged"; the "for free in PH3" route (i) is **not built and not measured**). The comparison is ISA by DIST (the one-level radix distribution of 3.3) and Psi = ISA[SA[i] + 1] from it. The oracle is the in-RAM ISA / Psi of the suffix-link prototype P1 (`LinkTables`, linked unchanged); the smoke test runs the P1 kernels. Measured on the prototype machine (A5), cold page cache, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit` = 96 MiB, medians of 3 runs, binaries of commits `8d79fc47` (timed rows) and `a7c3b52a` (identity and smoke rows; `apps` differs only in the smoke driver).

| gate | measured | result |
|---|---|---|
| G1 Psi byte-identical to the in-RAM Psi (P1 `LinkTables`, PSI[0] = 0) on dna_10m, prot_1m, rep_10m, a^n / Fibonacci / Thue-Morse 10^6, property tests for sigma = 1 .. 256 and windows of a few bytes; ISA from DIST equal to the in-RAM ISA | **40 of 40 `cmp` equal** over 8 inputs (the six, a random 256-symbol text, dna_100m) x 5 comparisons (default; 1 KB windows and 2 symbols per pass; cyclic variant; ISA by DIST; Psi from that ISA), 90 of 90 timed outputs equal as well; the BWT file checked by an independent Python program on all 8; 51 tests (all nine alphabets, the multi-pass case sigma > 64, windows of 1 byte to 64 KB, sentinel row at row 1 / inner / row n, exhaustive texts, adversarial families), default and checked build | **met** |
| G2 I/O of the Psi pass (BWT 1 B/char read, Psi 4 B/char written) <= 6 B/char, program counters and rchar + wchar and block-layer sectors; ISA by DIST next to it | program counters **5.000 B/char**; rchar + wchar **1.000** (+ the 4.000 of mapped writes, which are not syscalls); block layer (net of the runtime start) **5.19 B/char with 1 MiB windows (dna_100m: 1.02 read + 4.17 written)** and **7.16 with the 64 KB windows of the task statement (1.04 + 6.12)**; ISA by DIST **23.73 B/char counted** (about 24), + Psi from the ISA 8.00: 31.7 against 5.0; the BWT pass 5.000 B/char (SA read 4 + BWT written 1; 2.43 s at 10^8), so Psi pass + BWT = 10.0 B/char | **met** on the program counters and rchar + wchar at every window size and on the block layer with windows of 1 MiB; **the block-layer counter is not met with 64 KB windows (7.16)**: the .NET view accessor flushes (`msync`) every window on disposal (strace: 613 calls for 612 windows), so small windows cost 2 B/char of device writes and 2.4x the time (16 KB: 12.9 B/char, 256 KB: 4.65, 4 MiB: 4.04); the window size is a parameter, no redesign |
| G3 P1 link kernels x sources with the streamed Psi and the DIST ISA equal to the in-RAM tables on the P1 samples (200,000 internal nodes, seed 1; 145,561 for prot_1m) and to the definition on the small adversarial texts | **0 mismatches** in 7 inputs x 2 Psi routes x 8 cells (success flag, target and step count of every link): dna_10m 6,229,745 internal nodes, rep_10m 8,568,247, prot_1m 295,123 (sample **145,561**), a 256-symbol text, a^n / Fibonacci / Thue-Morse 10^6 (5,000 links, 20,000-step budget); equal to the definition on 1,725 exhaustive texts and the adversarial families at 9 lengths; the test fails when two entries of Psi or ISA are swapped | **met** |
| G4 RssAnon <= 256 MB incl. runtime, arena independent of n, time at dna_100m (cold cache), ESTIMATE for 3x10^9 | RssAnon (independent 20 ms sampler, whole process) **10.9 MB** (64 KB windows) / **10.0 MB** (1 MiB) at dna_100m, 4.4 MB without the in-process samplers; arena peak 1.31 MB (64 KB) / 5.24 MB (1 MiB) at both 10^7 and 10^8 (1 MiB read buffer + sigma x window); time **2.66 s [2.61-2.68]** (64 KB) / **1.10 s [1.08-1.13]** (1 MiB), the BWT pass 2.43 s, ISA by DIST 1.94 s, Psi from the ISA 4.48 s; **ESTIMATE for 3x10^9 (x30, linear, not measured): Psi pass 33-80 s, BWT pass 73 s, ISA route 192 s** | **met** (80 s against the 5-minute abandon line) |

Reading. (1) **Psi without the inverse permutation is exact and cheap**: 5 B/char of program-counted I/O against 31.7 for the ISA route, one pass for sigma <= 64, 10 MB of RssAnon whatever n is, 1.1 s per 10^8 symbols with 1 MiB windows (10 ns per entry); the P1 kernels cannot tell its tables from the in-RAM ones, and a Psi or an ISA with two swapped entries is caught. (2) **The BWT is the larger half of the bill** as a separate pass (5.0 B/char, 2.4 s per 10^8), 1.0 B/char if PH3 wrote it; that route is the open question of 1b.7d. (3) **Windows below 1 MiB are not free** (the finding above); the ADR's "sigma windows of 64 KB" in 3.3 should read 1 MiB, as the PH6 row of 4.2 already does. (4) More than 64 symbols: a byte alphabet takes 4 passes (8.0 B/char, measured on 10^7 random bytes) unless all 256 cursors are open in one pass (256 x 64-256 KB of windows, 5.0 B/char). (5) `PSI[0]` = 0 in the file, -1 in P1's RAM table, mapped on load; no kernel reads it (lb >= 1). Not verified: anything at 3x10^9 (ESTIMATES), the BWT written by PH3, the pass fused with PH4 / PH5 or run inside the pipeline process, a PSI section of the container (offset, CRC), alphabets above 256 symbols, 40-bit positions, the navigation pack beyond the link kernels; the audit of the new code: 70 mutants of the new code, 64 killed, 4 equivalent (argued), 2 survivors in the smoke driver (RESULTS-E9.md section 8). Defects: one in the smoke driver (a text without internal nodes under P1 sampling), found by a new test and fixed; one measuring-script defect (heap limit on the smoke test). EmProto tests 341 -> 392 (341 + 51; default build 392 of 392, Ph6 in the checked build 51 of 51).

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-16, E9, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `33582600`, tree clean; the diff since `25818bc1` outside `analysis/**` is only `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` (new `Ph6/*.cs` and `Experiments/E9.cs`, dispatch and usage lines in `Cli.cs`, six `Compile Include` lines in the project file), `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (new `Ph6/*`, two `Compile Include` lines), ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; `src/**`, `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` (the P1 sources are linked, not changed) and the e6 pipeline files are untouched, so there is no flag-off claim to prove. **Tests:** EmProto default build **392 of 392** (12 m 30 s); the checked build was not run by me (the implementer reports the Ph6 tests 51 of 51 in it; nothing in `src` changed). **Own measurement** with my binary published from this head (`/tmp/stbench/bin_e9_orch`) and my own sampler (RssAnon of `/proc/<pid>/status` every 20 ms, last `/proc/<pid>/io`, `/sys/block/vda/stat` read and write sectors from before the start until after `sync`; the script is in my scratchpad, not committed), cold cache, 96 MiB GC limit, `--no-sampler`, 2 runs per cell: my own `e6` run of `dna_100m` gives `.pk` and `.sa` with SHA-256 equal to the E7 reference hashes (24.6 s); BWT pass 1.54 / 2.08 s, RssAnon 9.0 MB, rchar + wchar 4.00 B/char, device read 4.30 and write 1.005 B/char; **Psi pass with 64 KB windows 2.55 / 2.27 s, RssAnon 5.1 MB, device read 1.05 and write 6.12 B/char (7.17 in all: the G2 block-layer figure of the implementer, 7.16, reproduces and is not met); with 1 MiB windows 1.01 / 1.02 s, RssAnon 4.2 MB, device read 1.05 and write 4.17 B/char (5.22: met)**; rchar + wchar 1.00 at both. My BWT file and Psi file are byte-identical (`cmp`) to the implementer's. **Independent identity:** a short pure-Python program of mine (no code of the implementer: ISA from the SA file, `Psi[i] = ISA[SA[i] + 1]`, `Psi[0] = 0`) equals the Psi file on `dna_100m` (100,000,001 entries) and on `dna_10m`; on `dna_10m` the streamed Psi and Psi from the ISA file are `cmp`-equal. **Smoke:** `e9-smoke` on `dna_10m` with my files (sample 100,000, seed 7): 0 mismatches in 2 Psi routes x 8 kernel cells, ISA and Psi equal to the in-RAM tables of P1. With two entries of Psi swapped (rows 1,000 and 2,000,000) the whole-table comparison says NO and the command exits 1, **but all eight kernel cells still report 0 mismatches, because the 100,000 sampled nodes never read the swapped rows**: the detector of a corrupt table is the whole-table equality, the kernel cells see only the corruption that the sample reaches (the implementer's test with swapped entries is among the 392; I did not check which rows it swaps). **Mutation audit:** the table of RESULTS-E9 section 11 has 70 rows, 64 killed, 4 equivalent (argued), 2 survivors (S02, S04, both in the smoke driver), as reported; the raw round files show 65 mutants run in round 1 (55 killed, 10 survived; 4 analyzer errors and 1 stale pattern not run) and 12 rows in rounds 2 and 3, which I reconciled with the table in total, not mutant by mutant. **Not re-measured:** the 40-comparison identity matrix on the small texts (a^n, Fibonacci, Thue-Morse, `rep_10m`, `prot_1m`, the 256-symbol text), the multi-pass case sigma > 64, ISA by DIST (23.7 B/char), the window sweep apart from 64 KB and 1 MiB, the 10.9 MB RssAnon with the in-process samplers, anything at 3x10^9 (the 33-80 s, 73 s and 192 s are ESTIMATES by x30). **Verdict:** E9 is accepted with one condition for 1b.7: every mapped writer uses windows of at least 1 MiB (flush-on-dispose of the .NET view accessor); the BWT source (a separate pass, 5 B/char counted, or PH3) stays open for 1b.7d.

### E8: hand-off package (2026-11-17)

E8 (section 5, row E8: the whole B-DCS pipeline at 1, 2.2 and 3 Gbp) cannot run in the session that prepares it (4 vCPU, 15 GB RAM, about 13 GB free disk, x86-64, no Docker daemon); the owner decided on 2026-11-16 (section 6c) that it runs on their Mac under Docker Desktop. What was built is therefore a **hand-off package**, and the **image is UNBUILT AND UNTESTED**: it was never built, never started, never run on macOS, on Docker Desktop or on arm64; its scripts ran only on the author's Linux x86-64 machine outside a container. Code `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` (new: `Gen/GenomeStream.cs`, `Verify/*`, `Experiments/E8.cs`, commands `e8-gen`, `e8-verify`, `e8-env`, usage lines in `Cli.cs`; **no change to the e6 pipeline, none to `src/**`**), tests `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/E8` (tests first: the generator 20 of 20 and the verifier 29 of 29 red against throwing stubs, `e8-env` red against a stub), package, scripts and rows `docs/refactoring/suffix-tree-upgrade/analysis/e8/` ([E8-RUNBOOK.md](../analysis/e8/E8-RUNBOOK.md) is what the owner follows): `Dockerfile` (SDK stage on the build platform, framework-dependent publish, arch-neutral; runtime stage per target architecture plus bash, python3, procps, coreutils, bc, util-linux, findutils), `Dockerfile.dockerignore` (an allow-list: 159 files, 1.1 MB), `docker/Directory.Build.props` (replaces the root props inside the build stage so that no NuGet package is restored), `e8-docker.sh` (host wrapper for macOS and Linux shells, bash 3.2, named volume, `--memory M --memory-swap M`, `--privileged` only with `--cold`, platform = the daemon's architecture, `--dry-run`), `e8.sh` / `preflight.sh` / `run_e8.sh` / `e8lib.sh` (entry point, preflight, restart-safe runner, helpers), `sample_e8.py` (independent sampler: RssAnon at 20 ms, `/proc/<pid>/io`, mincore residency of the packed text and the rank file, cgroup v1 and v2 counters, disk, phase markers, memory pressure), `tables_e8.py` (rows to the tables of RESULTS-E8 and the gates), `inject_faults.py`, `selftest_e8.sh`, `check_docker_context.py`, `mutation/`. The settings are those of E6 / E7 (layout A, codec Packed, PH4 base mode, no chain encoding, 4 threads, 96 MiB GC limit, `--scale-n 3000000000`: reference block sizes at 3 Gbp, the series-B ratios below).

**Pre-registered gates for the E8 run (fixed by the orchestrator; judged by `tables_e8.py` as pass / fail / not run):** **G1** the verifier passes at 1, 2.2 and 3 Gbp and the output SHA-256 are identical between the generous and every limited-cache run of the same text and between thread counts; **G2** independently sampled RssAnon of the dotnet process at most 256 MB at every size (E7: 124 MB at 500 Mbp); **G3** peak scratch at most 12.5 B/char and peak total disk at most 70 GB at 3 Gbp; **G4** reported, not gated: time per phase and total (ESTIMATE from E7 for 3 Gbp: 11-15 min on the prototype machine, linear), I/O in B/char, and the cache table of (d) with the answer to the open question of 6c (3); abandon rule: a run that takes more than 6 times the generous run of the same text is stopped and reported as "does not finish in practice at this memory". Protocol, cheap first: (a) 1 Gbp generous, (b) 2.2 Gbp (crosses 2^31), (c) 3 Gbp, (e) 1 Gbp with 1 and 2 threads, (d) the sweep of the same text at container memory 16 / 6 / 4 / 3 / 2.2 / 1.6 / 1.25 / 1.0 times (text + rank file) + 160 MiB (1 Gbp: 7792 ... 640 MiB), with the effective cache fraction (mincore) and the verdict "smallest memory with slowdown at most 3x".

**Measured here (nothing of it is the E8 run; all on the prototype machine, cgroup v1, cached files):** (1) **Generator** `e8-gen` (the genome-like family of E3-E7 streamed with a bounded buffer, SHA-256 and letter counts in a `.sums` file): byte-equal to `Families.ToAscii(Families.GenomeLike(n, seed))` for n = 1 .. 300 and nine sizes up to 3 M with three seeds, identical for nine buffer sizes (1 byte to 16 MiB), allocations below 24 MB for 40 M symbols (test); at 200 Mbp **byte-identical to the E7 input** `genome_200m.txt` (SHA-256 `8411266f...4923`); speed **7.1-7.2 ns per symbol** (200 Mbp in 1.44 s including the file write and the SHA-256), ESTIMATE for 3 Gbp (x15, linear, not measured) 21-25 s of CPU plus the write of 3 GB. (2) **Verifier** `e8-verify` (eleven checks: files, meta, text, input, SA permutation by a mapped bitmap, strict order of adjacent suffixes, stored LCP against the directly computed common prefix, LCP width rule, statistics recomputed by a stack scan, the child table cell by cell against the lcp-interval definition with no other cell set, SHA-256 of all files; no in-RAM reference; positions below 2^32 - 3): 100 Mbp genome-like **pass 11 of 11 in 5.8 s** (4 threads), **200 Mbp pass in 11.8 s = 59 ns per symbol** (rank pass 7.0 s, scan 3.5 s, input 0.6 s, hashes 0.6 s), RssAnon **24 MB** (16 MB of it the scan stack of the 1 Mi-entry N run; the permutation bitmap is a mapped temporary file); the SHA-256 of `.pk .sa .lcp .ovf .cld` at 200 Mbp **equal the reference hashes that E7 made from the in-RAM kernels** (`genome_200m.refs.sha`) and the statistics equal (NODE_COUNT 332,114,983, 132,114,981 internal nodes, LRS 999,999 at rank 150,096,427). Stated cost: rank pass about 2 random text reads per rank plus the common prefix up to the cap, scan one LCP pass plus the expected child cells, hashes one pass per file; **ESTIMATE for 3 Gbp (x15 of the 200 Mbp figure, not measured): 3 min with every file cached, 5-15 min when they are not; RssAnon about 240 MB for a 15 Mbp N run (16 B per open interval) plus a 375 MB bitmap file**. The common prefix of an adjacent pair is computed directly up to 65,536 symbols; longer pairs (N runs, satellites, a^n: 503,398 at 100 Mbp, 1,306,797 at 200 Mbp) are checked up to the cap, at the mismatch (both symbols, hence the order) and at 16 probes (the last 8 symbols of the claimed match, 8 spread): a stored value too large by up to 8 is always caught, by more it is missed with probability about 4^-8 per fault (**a bound of the design, reported in `long_pairs`**; uncapped is a switch). (3) **Injected faults, real outputs:** 33 faults at 100 Mbp and again at 200 Mbp, **33 of 33 detected each** (swap of two SA entries far apart and adjacent, a duplicated entry, an entry above n, SA[0] changed, LCP value +1 and -1, overflow value +1 / -1 and rank +1, child cell xor 1 / zeroed / an empty cell set / all ones / the root cell, one bit of the packed text, `.sa .lcp .cld .pk` truncated and extended by one byte, `.ovf` truncated by a pair, `.lcp` deleted, metadata n + 1, statistics n / nodecount / lrsrank / internal / max LCP + 1, one input symbol), the failing checks named per fault; after restoring every file the set verifies again and the SA hash is the original. Unit tests: **every single-byte flip (masks 01, 20, 80) of all seven files of five small texts** and more than 300 flips in a text with an overflow list are detected, 2,409 exhaustive strings (binary to 10, ternary to 5) pass, every LCP width (U8, U8 + overflow, U16 + overflow, U32) passes, swapped / duplicated / out-of-range SA entries, changed LCP values (every overflow pair by +-1 ... 8), corrupt child cells (every cell of "mississippi" five ways), truncated / extended / missing files, off-by-one in n (meta, stats, both), tampered metadata and statistics key by key, a changed input are caught by the check that owns them. (4) **Mutation audit of the verifier** (`analysis/e8/mutation`, four rounds, hand-made single-site mutants, watchdog): **90 mutants, 83 killed, 6 equivalent (argued), 1 survivor** (the spread probes of long pairs: no input found where they are the only check that fails); the first single-byte-flip test of the changed code found **one defect of the verifier itself** (a stored LCP of a long pair too large by 2 or more passed with probability about 9 %), fixed with the tail probes and tests. (5) **Scripts:** the runner, sampler, preflight, tables and wrapper ran for real at 20 and 200 Mbp (generous run with verification, thread runs 1 and 2 identical, a cell in a cgroup-v1 sub-group at 160 MiB with 61,012 limit hits and identical outputs, the report, the gates on synthetic rows); the cgroup readers against synthetic v1 and v2 trees (17 checks) and the real v1 hierarchy; the program built from exactly the files that the allow-list lets in, **offline, with an empty NuGet cache and an empty package source** (publish OK, no package downloaded, outputs byte-identical to the normal build on `dna_10m`).

**Boundary audit (static, 32-bit limits above 2^31 symbols; the design limit is n <= 2^32 - 4: positions are `uint`, `EndToEnd` refuses n >= 2^32 - 3, `ChildScan` n >= 2^32 - 2, `LcpGeometry` n >= 2^32 - 1, the Tagged codec ends at 2^30 - 1): no defect found, no change to the pipeline.** Safe by construction (long arithmetic, or an int that is bounded by a block, a bucket, an arena cap or a `checked` cast): `Prepare.cs:50,79,87` (block casts), `EmBuilder.cs:141,188-215` (K, fan-out, sample count; `(int)(hi - lo)` is about m / sampleCount, at most m / 2 < 2^31 for any m below 2^32), `PosStore.cs:44` (`MemoryPositions` holds an `int` count), `:56-74,186` (`start * 4` is `long`), `SaSink.cs:35` (`checked`), `:53,71` (`entries * 4`, view offsets `long`), `LeafSorter.cs:32` (`checked`, leaf <= K), `MappedWindowWriter.cs:47-74`, `AlignedWindowWriter.cs:74` (`index << 2` on a `long`), `SampleGeometry.cs:62-68`, `SampleNamer.cs:246,260,548` (names and slots below the sample count 1.4x10^8 at 3x10^9, asserted < 2^32), `ExternalDcs.cs:270-289`, `NameSinks.cs:105-119` (`checked`, reduced string <= N_base), `SaInverter.cs:23-79` (`checked`, arena-bounded), `Runs.cs:50-115`, `LcpBuild.cs:94-423` (chunk-local casts <= R, `* 4` offsets `long`), `Spool.cs:69,117,149` (`checked`), `LcpOutput.cs:67-137` (`(n + 1) * width` long, ranks `(uint)` < 2^32), `PlcpScan.cs:44`, `ChildScan.cs` (`IntervalEntry` uint fields, `None` = 2^32 - 1 above every rank), `PackedText.cs:129-135` (`long bit = i * Bits`), `SuffixOrder.cs:103-105` (`(uint)p` with p <= n, `(long)block * m`), `EndToEndOptions.cs:237,248` (bounded), `stackalloc` only with fixed sizes (`LeafSorter.cs:136`, `PosStore.cs:73`, `SampleNamer.cs:624`, `LcpBuild.cs:101`). Needed a test and got one (`BoundaryTests`, synthetic values; 16 tests, all green): the scale rule at 1, 2.147, 2.2, 3 and 4.29x10^9 (reference values at 3x10^9, P = 375 chunks, decimal arithmetic of K), the width rule and the chunk geometry at 3x10^9, `FastMod32` for numerators up to 2^32 - 1 and nine divisors, `SampleGeometry` slot and position inverse above 2^31, `DcsRankTable.Rank` above 2^31 against the slot formula, `PackedText` symbols, windows, keys and the vector and scalar common prefix at positions above 2^31 (bit offsets above 2^32; 2, 3 and 8 bits), `MappedWindowWriter`, `AlignedWindowWriter`, `MappedSaSink` and `MappedFile` at file offsets above 2^32 bytes (sparse files), the length limits of `EndToEnd` (refused from 2^32 - 3, passes the check at 2^32 - 4) and `ChildScan`. **Latent, not related to 2^31:** `EmBuilder.cs:239` doubles `oversample` (an `int`) on every degenerate retry, which overflows after 26 retries of one bucket (64 attempts are allowed); the two limits `EndToEnd` (2^32 - 3) and `ChildScan` (2^32 - 2) differ by one. **Remain unverified above 2^31 until the owner's run:** every stage end to end on a text of more than 2^31 symbols (PH1 recursion on 1.4x10^8 samples, the 2^31 / 2^32 crossing of the sort positions, PH4 over 375 chunks, PH5 above 2^31 ranks with a 15 Mbp stack, the mapped CHILD windows, the real 64-bit offsets of every output), and everything that needs real data above 500 Mbp.

**Not verified / limits.** The image was never built (the apt step, BuildKit's reading of `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, the Debian release behind the `10.0` tag), the scripts were never run in a container, on macOS, on Docker Desktop or on arm64; **cgroup v2 was exercised only against synthetic files** (this machine has v1), `drop_caches` / `--privileged` / `--memory` as Docker Desktop's VM presents them, mincore on its disk, are untested; arm64 uses the scalar common-prefix path and no prefetch (guarded `IsSupported`, tested scalar fallback; no P/Invoke anywhere: every native call is a .NET framework API, the `StructLayout.Sequential` structs of PH4 / PH5 are scratch-file records of 3 / 4 `uint`s); everything above 500 Mbp; the verifier above 200 Mbp; the sweep cells at the real block sizes; one machine. **Open points for the owner and 1b.7:** (a) the numbers of E8 are "this Mac under Docker Desktop", not comparable with the prototype machine, and arm64 does not transfer to x86; (b) the answer to the open question of 6c (3) is the table of step (d); (c) 1b.7 should keep `e8-verify` as the acceptance check of the container writer (it reads the separate files of e6, the section container needs an adapter); (d) the verifier's cap, probes and the 64 Mi-entry stack are parameters, not proofs; (e) a text with a single symbol repeated for hundreds of millions of positions makes the verifier's stack (and PH5's spill) as deep as the run (a^n: skipped, never passed). **Cost of E8 (wall clock between the first and the last commit; tokens are an estimate):** about 1.5 hours in one session (23:15 on 2026-11-17 to 00:45 UTC), about 0.5-0.6 M tokens; the cap of the brief (2.5 h, 0.8 M) was not reached. EmProto tests 392 -> 469 (392 + 77; default build 469 of 469, 11 m 53 s).

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-18, E8 package, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `9adcbe09`, tree clean; the diff since `6fcfbf4c` outside `analysis/e8/**` is `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` (new `Experiments/E8.cs`, `Gen/GenomeStream.cs`, `Verify/*`; of existing files only the usage and dispatch lines of `Cli.cs`), `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/E8`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; `src/**`, the e6 pipeline files and `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` are untouched; a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names found nothing, and the image labels carry only a title, a description and the git hash. **Tests:** EmProto default build **469 of 469** (12 m 8 s); the checked build was not run. **Own measurement** with my binary published from this head (`/tmp/stbench/bin_e8_orch`): `e8-gen --n 200m` takes 1.6 s and its file is `cmp`-equal to the E7 input `genome_200m.txt`; `e8-verify` on my own `dna_100m` outputs (made by my own `e6` run on 2026-11-16, SHA-256 equal to the E7 references) passes 11 of 11 in 12.0 s. **My own fault injection** (a separate Python program, not the implementer's `inject_faults.py`; each fault applied in place, the verifier run, the fault undone): SA swap of far rows, SA swap of adjacent rows, an SA duplicate, an SA entry above n, SA[0], LCP + 1, LCP - 1, one child cell, one bit of the packed text, the LCP file one byte short, NODE_COUNT + 1 in the statistics: **11 of 11 end in an explicit `verify_all = FAIL` with the expected checks named (not a crash); the baseline and the restored files pass.** Mutation audit of the verifier: the final table recounted, 90 mutants, 83 killed, 6 equivalent (argued), 1 survivor (C19d, the spread probes of long pairs; no input where they alone fail), as reported. Static review without a daemon: `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, `e8-docker.sh` and the runbook read, `bash -n` clean for all six shell scripts, `--dry-run` prints the expected `docker build` and `docker run --memory M --memory-swap M` commands (for this machine's amd64); the Dockerfile and the context closure are the implementer's own check (`check_docker_context.py`, which applies the ignore rules with its own matcher, not BuildKit's). **Not verified by me:** that the image builds (apt step, BuildKit's reading of `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, the Debian release behind the `10.0` tag); anything inside a container, on macOS or on arm64; cgroup v2 on a real kernel; the preflight and the runner in a container; the verifier above 200 Mbp (my files had 100 Mbp, my faults sit at small LCP values: a corruption in the middle of a long match is missed by design, limit 1 of the runbook); anything at 1-3 Gbp; positions above 2^31 end to end. **Cost:** the implementer reports about 1.5 h and 0.5-0.6 M tokens (an estimate); the session counter says **1.34 h and 0.71 M tokens**: the time is inside the forecast of 1-1.5 h, the tokens above the forecast of 0.3-0.5 M and below the cap of 0.8 M. **Verdict:** the package is accepted as a hand-off; the owner's first build and preflight are the first test of the image.

E8 run on the owner's Mac, part 1 (2026-11-18; the owner's rows in `analysis/e8/results/mac-20261118/`, my reading): Apple silicon (aarch64, 18 cores), Docker Desktop VM of 8.3 GB, cgroup v2, the image built and run natively on the first try (the preflight OK with the three expected warnings; the scalar compare path on arm64). Steps a (1 Gbp, warm and cold), b (**2.2 Gbp, above 2^31 symbols**), e (1 and 2 threads) and the limited-cache sweep at 1 Gbp finished. **Step c (3 Gbp), run 1, ran all phases in 407 s but ended with exit code 1: the program's own RssAnon cap of 256 MB (`rss-cap-mb` of `e6`) was exceeded (309.7 MB), so the runner skipped the verifier and removed the outputs. Run 2 with `--rss-cap-mb 400` (cold, 411.5 s, RssAnon 309.2 MB) passed the verifier 11 of 11 in 125 s (25,106,794 long pairs, child stack peak 3,000,002 entries): the 3 Gbp outputs are verified.** Numbers: 123 / 132 / 136 ns per symbol at 1 / 2.2 / 3 Gbp (123.3, 291.3, 406.8 s; the prototype machine: 205-300 ns); the verifier passes 11 of 11 at 1 Gbp (twice, 43 s), at 2.2 Gbp (102 s) and at 3 Gbp (125 s); output identity 9 of 9 SHA-256 comparisons (1 and 2 threads, seven cache limits); scratch 12.00 B/char, outputs 9.50-9.54 B/char, counted I/O 75.4-75.6 B/char, peak disk 55.6 GB at 3 Gbp (G3 PASS: scratch 12.00 B/char against 12.5, 55.6 GB against 70); RssAnon peak 172 / 226 / 310 MB at 1 / 2.2 / 3 Gbp with 4 threads, and 88 / 151 / 170 MB with 1 / 2 / 4 threads at 1 Gbp (times 247 / 151 / 123 s). **G1 PASS, G2 FAIL at 3 Gbp (309.7 and 309.2 MB in two runs: a reproducible plateau), G3 PASS** (`tables_e8.py` judged G1 and G3 on run 1 alone and printed NOT RUN; the orchestrator changed it to use the first finished run). Reading (the orchestrator's, from the rows and the sampler series): the peak is in the fused PH4 + PH5 phase (PH1 168 MB, PH2/PH3 180 MB at 3 Gbp), and the series is a **flat plateau of about 295-310 MB** from the start of that phase to its end, not growth: `arena_peak` (the larger stage peak) is 69 / 121 / 155 MB at 1 / 2.2 / 3 Gbp against the 160 MiB cap of PH4, PH5's child arena (cap 64 MiB) lives in the same phase (its own peak is not in the rows), plus the managed heap (limit 96 MiB) and the runtime. The E7 slope of 10 MB per doubling did not carry over: the parameters of the scale rule grow with n until the caps, and the caps as set (160 + 64 MiB arenas, 96 MiB heap, 30-60 MB runtime) add up to more than the 256 MB of assumption A1: the two are inconsistent by design. Above 3 Gbp the arenas stay at their caps, so the plateau is expected near 310 MB (ESTIMATE, not measured above 3 Gbp). **Cache sweep at 1 Gbp (text + rank file 481 MiB; cells 3024 ... 640 MiB): no knee reached:** slowdown 1.04-1.08x down to 768 MiB, 1.19x at 640 MiB (effective cache 0.858; PH4 + PH5 78 -> 87 s), no cell near the 3x criterion; the cells stop at 1.0x + 160 MiB, so the sweep is an upper bound for the cache PH4 needs, not the knee; the difference from E7 (no finish below an effective 0.7 at 100 Mbp, x86, scale-down parameters) is unexplained; cells below 640 MiB (512, 448, 384, 320) are the next step. The warm and the cold run of step a differ by 1 % (123.3 and 124.5 s). **Update, the sweep below 640 MiB (the owner's console, same day; the rows are not yet in the repository): the knee is found.** The cells of 512 MiB (effective cache 0.81) and 448 MiB (0.68) were stopped by the 6x watchdog (12 min against 123 s; exit code 137) and the sweep stopped itself after the two; the RssAnon peaks of 88 and 86 MB are the level of PH2/PH3, so the runs were still before the fused PH4 + PH5 phase (inferred from the RssAnon level, not from the phase markers). With 1.19x at 640 MiB (effective 0.86) the knee lies between an effective 0.86 and 0.81: **a cliff, not a slope**, the behaviour that E3 found for tie-rich text (11-14x at 0.74-0.84). Consequence: the floor of section 6b stands (about 1x of text + rank file plus the anonymous memory), the page cache that stopped the build is that of the random reads of text and ranks in PH3, **not PH4's scratch** (the cells never reached PH4 + PH5 below 640 MiB, so PH4's own need under a cache that small is still unmeasured); at 1 Gbp text + rank is 481 MiB and the smallest working container is 640 MiB (1.33x). ESTIMATE for 3 Gbp from the same ratios (not measured): text + rank 1.41 GiB (measured sizes 1,125,000,064 + 386,707,072 bytes) plus the anonymous plateau of about 0.3 GiB, i.e. a container of about 1.7-1.8 GiB.

### 1b.7a: Dist, SpillStack, counters (2026-11-18)

The first of the packages 1b.7a-e of section 4.5 (authorised 2026-11-18, section 6c; forecast 3-5 hours and 0.8-1.2 M tokens, +-50 %, stated before the launch; gates G1-G5 and the abandon rule were fixed by the orchestrator before the work). Production versions, with their own tests, of the three building blocks that the external-memory builder uses; the prototypes of `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` stay where they are and are **not rewired** (the persistent builder replaces them at 1b.7e, no line of `apps/` changed). Code `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (namespace `SuffixTree.Esa.External`, 10 new files, every type `internal`, **no existing file of the project changed**, no csproj changed): `Dist.cs` (the primitive: `Dist.Scatter`, `BuildIsa`, `BuildPhi`, `DistOptions`, `DistTarget`, `IDistMapper`, `DistEntry`, `ChunkDivider`), `DistScratch.cs` (the generalised scratch side of the tuple: `DistScratch<T>`, `DistWriter<T>`, `DistReader<T>`, the design of the prototype's `Spool` of PH4), `SpillStack.cs`, `ArenaBudget.cs` (`ArenaBudget`, `BudgetedArena`), `IoCounters.cs`, `ProcIo.cs` (`IProcSource`, `ProcText`, `ProcIoSnapshot`, `IoMeasurement`), `RssReader.cs` (`RssReader`, `RssSampler`), `CountedFile.cs`, `MappedWindowWriter.cs` (the minimal writer that `Dist` needs; 1b.7b extends it), `Atomic.cs`. Tests: `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.Esa.Tests/External` (145 tests, the contract of every block) and `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/External` (the differential tests of G2: 10, and two `[Explicit]` cost tests of G3); scripts, raw rows, mutant lists and results: `analysis/em/{run_1b7a_g3.sh, sample_rss_1b7a.py, tables_1b7a_g3.py, rows_1b7a_g3.tsv}` and `analysis/mutation/*1b7a*`.

Choices made (small; none is a public API, a format or a behaviour verified in E4-E9): (1) **`ArenaBudget` lives next to `NativeArena` as a decorator (`BudgetedArena` wraps one), not as an optional parameter of the arena.** Reasons: no existing production class changes (the in-memory kernels allocate through `NativeArena` exactly as they were verified; a parameter would add a branch to every allocation and a release to the finalizer path); the budget is a policy of the builder that spans one arena per task, not a property of an arena. The cost: nothing stops a builder task from calling `NativeArena` directly and bypassing the cap; 1b.7b-e must construct `BudgetedArena` only (a review rule, not an analyzer). The production budget also refuses a release of more than is live, cannot wrap around at a cap of `long.MaxValue`, and gives the bytes back when the arena throws on a damaged guard band. (2) **Generalisation of tuple and target:** pass 1 reads a file of 32-bit values and a struct mapper turns entry `i` into `(key, value)` or skips it (ISA, Phi, the rank file of PH1 are three mappers: `IsaMapper`, `PhiMapper`, a test-local mapper over the prototype's `SampleGeometry`); pass 2 scatters into an array of R entries of any unmanaged value type with a fill value and an optional exactly-once check; PH4's own tuples (`PhiTuple`, `RankTuple`) use `DistScratch<T>` / `DistWriter<T>` / `DistReader<T>` directly (any unmanaged tuple, caller-chosen chunk, thread-safe appends). A key outside the target, a damaged chunk file, a repeat and a miss are `InvalidDataException`, never a write into a wrong buffer. (3) **The counters are instances, not process-wide statics** (`IoCounters` is passed through the options; scratch live / peak bytes live in it), so tests and concurrent builds do not share state. (4) **The `/proc` readers take an injectable `IProcSource`** and return "unavailable" (`null`, `RssSnapshot.Available == false`, `RssStats.Available == false`, no sampler thread) where the file, a field or the whole `/proc` is missing or the source misbehaves; they never throw. (5) **Mapped windows default to 1 MiB** (`MappedWindowWriter.DefaultWindowBytes`, `DistOptions.WindowBytes`), the E9 condition. (6) **`SpillStack`: same semantics and spill format as the prototype** (block j at offset `j x B x sizeof(T)`, raw entries, two blocks resident, the clean-block rule), with one hardening: a failed spill write or read-back leaves the stack as it was (`Pop` no longer lowers the height before the read-back that may fail), so a retry fails the same way instead of returning garbage. (7) **Hot loop:** pass 1 is a method of its own marked `AggressiveOptimization`, and the division by R is Lemire's two multiplications (`ChunkDivider`): see the reading. (8) Keys, positions and values of the files are 32-bit as in the prototypes (`n < 2^32 - 2` for ISA and Phi, `NotSupportedException` above); 40-bit positions are not part of the package.

| gate | measured | result |
|---|---|---|
| G1 contract: property tests, exhaustive small cases, oracles, boundaries, failure modes | **145 tests** (123 written first and red against a throwing stub, 22 added by the audit and the review of the first measurements). `Dist`: every permutation of 1..5 elements x 7 option sets (1,071 runs) and 60 random permutations (n <= 3,000, random R / buffer / read / window) equal the plain scatter with the statistics, the explicit and mapped bytes, the scratch peak and the arena peak equal to a closed formula; chunk boundaries (rows = 1, 2, R-1, R, R+1, 2R-1, 2R, 2R+1 for R = 1, 4, 64 and buffer capacities 1, 2, 3, 5); the suffix arrays of a^n, Fibonacci, Thue-Morse, random DNA and periodic text; Phi for 263 texts (all 255 binary strings to length 7 and 8 more) and equal to the LCP array of the kernel through the PLCP definition; generic values (byte, ushort, ulong; tuple sizes 5, 6, 12); `ChunkDivider` equal to `/` for 21 divisors and the edges of the key range; the arena peak equals the documented blocks at the cap and is refused one byte below. `SpillStack`: model test against `Stack<T>` for every block size 2-50 (3,000 steps, updates in place), **all 2^12 push/pop sequences for blocks 2, 3, 4**, the spilled / read-back block counts, the file bytes, hysteresis at every boundary, the clean-block rule, flush interval, a chain of 300,000 entries (spill = 16 B x (height - about 2 blocks)), 1-byte and 8-byte entries. Budget: random sequences against a model, 8 threads, the cap at `long.MaxValue`; counters: known sequences, 8 threads; parsers: line anchoring (`cancelled_write_bytes` is not `write_bytes`), tabs, overflow. **Failure modes:** budget exceeded (and given back), truncated spill file and truncated chunk file (`EndOfStreamException`, stack unchanged, retry fails the same way), disk write error (`/dev/full` through a symlink: `IOException`, push does not happen), missing directory, scratch directory that is a file, damaged source (wrong length, entry beyond n, repeat, miss, key outside the target), mapper exception; after every failure no chunk file, no budget bytes and no scratch bytes remain | **PASS** |
| G2 equality with the prototype (differential tests in `EmProto.Tests`, prototype code unchanged) | **ISA files byte-identical** to `IsaByDist` with equal `DistStats`, equal explicit read / write bytes, equal mapped bytes and equal peak scratch: 180 option combinations on 9 small texts, a^n / Fibonacci / Thue-Morse 10^6 (R = 266,667 and 8,000,000), `dna_10m`, `prot_1m`, `rep_10m` (P = 375 and R = 8,000,000), and **all 50 outputs of the G3 protocol on `dna_100m` (30 production, 20 prototype) equal to the in-RAM ISA of the E9 setup (`cmp`)**; **rank file** equal to `SaInverter` in 40 cases (X = 7, 13; n = 0..400; 4 chunk / buffer sets); **`SpillStack` identical** to the prototype's on 21 random push / pop / update sequences of 2,500 steps (blocks 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 16, 33 x flush 0, 100, 1000): height, peak, top and all seven statistics after every step, the spill file byte for byte every 500 steps, and on a chain of 300,000 entries. **The prototype has no Phi file** (PH4 holds Phi per chunk in RAM), so Phi is compared with the in-RAM scatter on the families and data files and with the kernel's LCP, not with the prototype | **PASS** (the Phi clause of the brief is met against the oracle, not against the prototype) |
| G3 cost parity: ISA of `dna_100m`, medians of 5 runs per cell (the brief said 3), cold and warm, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit` 96 MiB, `sync` before every run, variants alternate, time of the `Build` call | **production / prototype, parameters of E9 (R = 266,667, P = 375, 64 KB buffers): cold 1.965 / 1.838 s = +6.9 %, warm 1.928 / 1.929 s = -0.1 %; the real R = 8,000,000 (P = 13): cold 2.276 / 2.189 s = +4.0 %, warm 2.280 / 2.269 s = +0.5 %**. I/O identical to the byte (11.86 B/char read, 7.86 written, 4.00 through windows, scratch peak 7.86). RssAnon (production sampler, 10 ms; the python sampler of `/proc/<testhost>` at 20 ms reads the same maxima; MB = 10^6 bytes from the KiB of the kernel): maximum 54.4 MB (A) and 62.3 MB (B) for the whole test host, which holds 13.7 MB before the call: **the call adds 40.8 MB (A) and 48.7 MB (B) against an arena peak of 25.7 / 32.9 MB** (the prototype 41.2-41.9 / 49.0-49.9 MB, arena 26.8 / 34.0 MB; the difference to the arena is the runtime's growth during the call); with R = 8 M, P = 375 at 3x10^9 the arena is 24.6 + 32 + 0.06 MB = 56.6 MB (D). Counters: 8.04 ns per update, about 50,000 updates in a run = 0.4 ms = **0.02 %**; the run-to-run comparison with and without counters gives 1.987 / 1.954 s = +1.7 %, inside the spread (+-5 %) of single runs | **PASS** (all four cells inside +-10 %; the measurement before the tuning read +6.8 / +10.6 / +19.4 / +12.0 % and the one after it with three runs and no `sync` +9.5 / +14.4 / -0.3 / +2.5 %, see the reading) |

| G5 no regression (test policy of PROGRESS-S1, level 2) | the affected set of `src/SuffixTree.Esa` (the only project that changed; the new code reaches memory through `Buf<T>`, which the checked build compiles differently): **`SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default 2,914 passed + 8 skipped** (2,777 before + 145 new; 1 m 47 s), **Exhaustive 71 of 71** (9 m 9 s) and **Fuzz 30 of 30** (8 m 35 s) because `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed, **checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) 2,915 passed + 7 skipped** (1 m 48 s); **`SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` 672 of 672** (always for 1b.7); **`SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` 479 of 479** (469 + the 10 differential tests; it references Esa through EmProto and holds the new tests; 14 m 7 s) and its External subset without the data files in the checked build 6 of 6; `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` (references Esa, has no tests) builds. **Skipped:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed: the diff of `src` is ten new files), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props are unchanged), the checked build of Persistent and of the whole EmProto project (nothing they compile differently changed); no widening trigger fired (no contract type of Core, container format, section table, shared props, shared test helper or base class, data file read by tests, reflection or child process) | **PASS** (nothing red, nothing skipped that the policy requires) |

Reading. (1) **The production blocks reproduce the prototypes** to the byte (files, statistics, I/O counters, spill bytes) and cost the same within the noise of the machine: the spread of single runs of the same code on this virtio disk is about +-8 % (1.64-1.96 s for the prototype in the first protocol), which is why the second protocol syncs before every run and uses five runs. (2) **A performance defect of the first production version, found by G3 and fixed:** with the pass-1 loop inside the large `Scatter` method the runtime compiles it as an on-stack replacement of an unoptimised frame (the JIT listing shows the loop variables in memory), and a 64-bit hardware division per entry; the first measurement (three runs per cell) read +6.8 % (A, cold), +10.6 % (A, warm), +19.4 % (B, cold) and +12.0 % (B, warm). A separate method marked `AggressiveOptimization` and the Lemire division (the technique of the prototype's `LcpGeometry`, exact for all 32-bit keys, tested against `/`) brought it to -0.1 .. +6.9 %. No behaviour changed. (3) **Defects found in the prototypes (not fixed there, the prototypes are not touched):** `ArenaBudget.Reserve` wraps around at a cap of `long.MaxValue` (unreachable in practice); `SpillStack.Pop` lowers the height before a read-back that can fail, so a truncated spill or an I/O error leaves a stack whose next `Top` is garbage (E5 never damaged a spill); `BudgetedArena.ReleaseTo` and `Dispose` keep the bytes when the arena throws a canary violation; `RssSampler.Stop` waits up to a whole interval; `ProcIoSnapshot.Read` throws on a malformed file; `MappedWindowWriter.WriteAt` can overflow its range test for an offset near `long.MaxValue` (latent: the view accessor then refuses it). (4) **Mutation audit** of the new code (hand-made, watchdog, in a separate worktree, analyzers off for the mutant builds; `analysis/mutation/make_1b7a_mutants.py`, `make_1b7a_round2.py`, `make_1b7a_round3.py`, `run_1b7a_mutants.py`, `1b7a_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b7a.py`, `summary_1b7a.tsv`): four rounds, **233 mutants, 217 killed (3 of them by the watchdog on a hang, 5 by a crash of the test host), 16 argued equivalent one by one (`1b7a_equivalent.py`), 0 open.** Round 1 listed 191 (160 killed, 20 survived, 11 did not compile); the survivors were 12 equivalents and **8 real gaps of the tests** (the read counter at the end of a file, `SA[0]` of the Phi mapper, the message of a key outside the target, deletion of a chunk file before pass 2 ends, a missing field of `/proc/self/io`, a `/proc` that vanishes at the start of a measurement, a source opened exclusively, a sampler that is not woken by `Stop`); round 2 re-ran them with 9 replacements of the mutants that did not compile (16 of 17 killed; the scratch-release test could not tell deletion per chunk from deletion at the dispose, it now watches the scratch fall while the target is still written); round 3 added 42 fresh mutants (36 killed incl. that one, 5 survivors: 2 equivalent and 3 test gaps, 2 did not compile); round 4 re-ran the five (5 of 5 killed). The audit found no defect of the production code, only gaps of the tests. (5) **What is deliberately not here:** the mapped writer is created sparse, so a full disk shows as a fault on a mapped page, not as an exception (the `/dev/full` test covers the explicit writes of the spill and the chunk files and the creation of the target); `posix_fallocate` through `FileStreamOptions.PreallocationSize` would raise ENOSPC at creation but changes the write pattern that E9 measured, so it is an open point of 1b.7b, not a change made here.

Not verified: any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the simulated platform and the Linux file source only; `MemoryMappedFile` flush-on-dispose and sparse `SetLength` are Linux facts here); anything above 10^8 entries (3x10^9: P = 375 chunks, arena 56.6 MB, ISA time about 60 s and 24 B/char of I/O are ESTIMATES by x30); a full disk under a mapped window; `DistScratch` appended from several threads under the real PH4 load (tested with 8 threads on small data); 40-bit keys; a legal value equal to the `Fill` marker under the exactly-once check is not detected (documented on `DistTarget`); the scratch-release test samples the counters from a polling thread (timing-based, it passed in every run, a very loaded machine could make it flaky); the counter overhead is bounded by count x cost (0.02 %), not isolated by a run (the run comparison is inside its own noise).

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-18, 1b.7a, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `71ddfbb8`, tree clean; the diff since `f4b3b27f` is **only new files** in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (10 files, 1,454 lines; no existing production file, project file or public API changed, `apps/` untouched), tests under `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests/External` and `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/External`, `analysis/**`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names found nothing. `MappedWindowWriter.cs` is in the set although the ADR split puts the writer in 1b.7b: it is the minimal port that `Dist` needs, 1b.7b extends it. **Tests (the policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me in Release, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` in the default build):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **2,914 passed + 8 skipped** (2,777 before), Exhaustive **71 of 71** (9 m 9 s), Fuzz **30 of 30** (8 m 39 s), `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **479 of 479** (469 + 10 differential tests; 14 m 53 s). **Skipped by me, with the rule:** the checked build (the new code has no `EsaBounds` conditional; the implementer reports Esa 2,915 + 7 skipped and the External subset of EmProto 6 of 6 in it), Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), Ukkonen and Mcp.Core (their projects, Core and the shared props unchanged); no widening trigger fired. **Own measurement of G3** with the implementer's harness (`run_1b7a_g3.sh`, the test class `DistCostTests`: the production `Dist` is internal, so it cannot be driven from outside) but my own input: the suffix array of my own `e6` run of `dna_100m` (SHA-256 equal to the E7 reference), the reference ISA of E9, 3 runs per cell, cold and warm cache, 96 MiB GC limit, medians of the Build call in seconds, production against prototype: **A (R = 266,667, P = 375) cold 1.94 against 1.77 (+9.6 %), warm 1.81 against 1.78 (+1.7 %); B (R = 8,000,000) cold 2.46 against 2.43 (+1.2 %), warm 2.23 against 2.06 (+8.3 %)**; the implementer's medians of 5 were +6.9 / -0.1 / +4.0 / +0.5 %. All four cells are inside the gate of +-10 %, production is never faster and the noise between runs is about 5 %, so the true cost is 1-10 % above the prototype, not zero. I/O equal to the byte in both variants (read 1,186,432,004 and written 786,432,000 bytes in cell A), RssAnon 51-59 MB with the test host, **30 of 30 outputs `cmp`-equal to the E9 reference ISA, 0 different**. The counters' overhead of at most 1 % cannot be resolved by wall time (mine: +3.2 % warm, inside the noise); it rests on the implementer's count of 8.04 ns per update (0.02 % of the run), which I did not re-measure. **Mutation audit:** round 1 listed 191 mutants (recounted from the result files: 191 rows), later rounds re-ran survivors; the figures 233 mutants, 217 killed, 16 equivalent (argued), 0 open are the implementer's, reconciled by me only for round 1. **Not re-measured:** the differential tests as such beyond their passing (the 10 in EmProto.Tests), the cold start on other platforms (simulated only), anything above 10^8 entries (the 3x10^9 figures are ESTIMATES), a full disk under a mapped window, `DistScratch` under real PH4 load with several threads. **Cost:** the implementer reports about 2.5 h and about 0.9 M tokens (an estimate); the session counter says **2.45 h and 0.67 M tokens**, inside the forecast of 3-5 h and 0.8-1.2 M. **Verdict:** 1b.7a is accepted; the two conditions for 1b.7b-e are those of the implementer's open points: construct only `BudgetedArena` (a task can bypass the budget through a bare `NativeArena`), and measure `posix_fallocate` before deciding how a full disk under a mapped window is reported.

### 1b.7b: MappedTextReader, codec 4, MappedWindowWriter (2026-11-18)

The second of the packages 1b.7a-e of section 4.5 (authorised 2026-11-18, section 6c; forecast 2-4 hours and 0.6-1.0 M tokens, +-50 %, economy cap 5 hours or 1.4 M tokens, stated before the launch; gates G1-G5 and the abandon rule were fixed by the orchestrator before the work). Production versions of what PH0 and the later stages need to read and write the text and the output files: the packed text of TEXT codec 4 (layout, writer, reader) and the extension of the mapped writer (ranges of a shared file, completion, the reservation of the disk space). The prototypes of `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` are **not rewired and not touched** (no line of `apps/` changed). Code `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (namespace `SuffixTree.Esa.External`, every type `internal`, no csproj and no file outside `External/` changed): new `PackedTextFormat.cs` (`PackedTextFormat`: `CodecId` 4, `PadBytes` 64, `MinBits` 1, `MaxBits` 8, `MaxSymbols` 2^32 - 4, `BitsFor`, `DataBytes`, `TotalBytes`, `PackInto`; `PackedTextInfo`: length, width, alphabet, counts, SHA-256), `MappedTextReader.cs`, `PackedTextWriter.cs` (`Scan`, `Pack`, `WriteFile`, `PackedTextWriterOptions`), `MappedOutputFile.cs` (`MappedOutputFile`, `MappedFileOptions`, `ReservationMode`, `ReservationOutcome`, `IReservationSource`, `SystemReservationSource`); edited `MappedWindowWriter.cs` (range of a shared file, `FileOffset`, `Flush`; the public constructor, `Append`, `WriteAt`, `Position`, `Length`, `Dispose` and the 1 MiB default unchanged). Tests: `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.Esa.Tests/External` (105 new tests: `PackedTextFormatTests`, `MappedTextReaderTests`, `PackedTextWriterTests`, `MappedOutputFileTests`, the bit-level oracle `TextOracle`) and `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/External` (`TextDifferentialTests`: 18 differential tests; `TextCostTests` and `WriterCostTests`: three `[Explicit]` cost tests); scripts, raw rows and mutant lists: `analysis/em/{run_1b7b_g3.sh, tables_1b7b_g3.py, rows_1b7b_g3.tsv, rows_1b7b_g3_reader_v1.tsv}` and `analysis/mutation/*1b7b*`.

Choices made (small; none is a public API; the layout is the prototype's and no format was invented, the gaps are the open points 1-4 below): (1) **The reader is the prototype's `PackedText` with the checks that make it safe on a damaged file**: the same window arithmetic (a big-endian bit stream, 64 bits loaded unaligned and byte-reversed, the carry from the ninth byte), `Key` = window >> (64 - (64 / b) b), `Symbol`, the vector common prefix (AVX2, 256 / b symbols per step, guarded by `Avx2.IsSupported`: `UseVectors` cannot be switched on where the hardware has none, which the prototype's setter allowed) and the scalar one (`64 / b` symbols per step; this is the path of arm64); added: `ReadSymbols` (bulk unpack of a range, a byte copy for b = 8), `Verify`, and the checks. Opening is O(1) (the region has exactly `TotalBytes` bytes, the unused bits of the last data byte and the 64 padding bytes are zero) and reports damage as `InvalidDataException` (values that come from a file are never an `ArgumentException`); a position below 0 is an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; a position at or beyond n reads the zero padding (one unsigned comparison more than the prototype on the hot path); the common-prefix limit is clamped to the shorter suffix, so the padding is never compared and nothing reads outside the mapping whatever the caller passes (the prototype's precondition "`max <= n - max(i, j)`" is now enforced); `CompareSuffixes` of the same position is 0 (the prototype returns 1 there). A read after `Dispose` throws `ObjectDisposedException`: `Dispose` sets the limit to 0, so the hot path needs no extra check; a read that races the disposal is undefined (documented). (2) **`Verify` is the O(n) check**: every symbol below sigma, the counts, and the SHA-256 recomputed from the symbols through the alphabet (the prototype's hash is of the input bytes, so it can be checked without the input). (3) **The writer is the prototype's two passes** (alphabet scan, counts and SHA-256; then pack block by block into a mapped writer), split into `Scan` and `Pack` so that the container writer (1b.7e) can aim `Pack` at the TEXT section of its own file, and `WriteFile` for a file of its own: `<target>.tmp`, filled, `fsync`ed, renamed over the target; a failure removes the temporary file and leaves the target as it was (the prototype wrote the target in place). The 64 padding bytes are written explicitly (a section that held an older build is overwritten), the length of the input is compared again when the second pass starts (the prototype noticed a changed input only through the counts, so an input that grew was not noticed), and `MinBits` can force a wider b (open point 4). (4) **`MappedOutputFile` is a file of known final length with one mapping and several range writers** (the shape PH3 needs: four tasks writing disjoint slices of the SA; PH6: one window per symbol of a Psi file; the prototype could not share a file between its writers). A writer's offsets are relative to its range and checked against it; the creation of the views is serialised; `Complete` (every writer must be disposed; `fsync`) ends the file; a failed creation leaves no file at the path. `MappedWindowWriter`'s public constructor keeps creating its file **sparse**, so `Dist` is unchanged in behaviour and in cost (nothing of 1b.7a was re-measured or needed to be). (5) **The reservation of the disk space is a mode with an injectable platform source** (`ReservationMode`: `Sparse` = the layout E9 measured, `Preallocate` = `fallocate` through `FileStreamOptions.PreallocationSize` where the platform has it, no P/Invoke, and the explicit fallback where it has not: the free space of the volume is read (`DriveInfo`) and a file that does not fit is refused with an `IOException` before it is created; `Auto` = the measured choice, below). `ReservationOutcome` says what happened (`Preallocated`, `FreeSpaceChecked`, `Unchecked`, `Sparse`). The real source preallocates on Linux only (`OperatingSystem.IsLinux()`; the BCL has equivalents on macOS and Windows that were not run here); elsewhere the fallback runs, and it is tested through a fake source. (6) **A defect of cost found by G3 and fixed:** the first version of `Lce` read +21 % (random pairs, scalar) over the prototype; its prologue (a disposal check, three sign checks, the clamp) became one sign test on the OR of the three arguments and the disposal check moved to the early-return path (+10.6 % remains: the clamp costs about 1.5 ns on a comparison that is itself 15 ns).

**The choice of the default, `Auto` = `Preallocate`, with the numbers** (`analysis/em/rows_1b7b_g3.tsv`, `run_1b7b_g3.sh`, test class `WriterCostTests`; 400 MB output for 10^8 symbols as in E9 (4 bytes per symbol), 1 MiB windows, cold page cache (`sync`, `drop_caches`) before every run, the input BWT warm, 3 runs per cell in alternating order, device writes from `/sys/block/vda/stat` after the `sync` that follows the run, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit` 96 MiB): **one sequential cursor (the SA, LCP, CHILD writers):** time of the writes prototype 0.684 s, production sparse 0.677 s, production with `fallocate` 0.665 s; device writes 4.018 / 4.018 / 4.033 B per symbol; **four cursors, each with its own contiguous range and window (the Psi writer of E9 for DNA):** 1.748 s (the prototype, one file per cursor, it cannot share a file) / 1.834 s sparse (+4.9 %) / 1.817 s with `fallocate` (+3.9 %); device writes 4.026 / 4.170 / 4.170 B per symbol (one file with four ranges costs 3.6 % more than four files, and 4.17 is the figure that E9 measured for its own `PsiStream` on the shared file); creating the 400 MB file takes 0.8 ms sparse and 1.5 ms with `fallocate` (ext4); RssAnon above the test host's baseline 1.0 MB (one cursor) and 3.2 MB (four). `fallocate` against sparse: time -1.8 % (one cursor) and -0.9 % (four), device writes +0.4 % and +0.0 %: **no cost that these runs can resolve, and a full disk is an `IOException` at creation instead of a fault on a mapped page**; hence `Auto` = `Preallocate`. The harness reproduces E9's finding that windows below 1 MiB are not free (Psi pattern, 64 KB windows: 3.02 s and 6.12 B per symbol sparse, 8.29 for the prototype's four files, against 1.8 s and 4.17 with 1 MiB: flush-on-dispose of the view accessor). **Full disk, tested:** `fallocate` on a real 4 MB `tmpfs` (mounted by the test, ignored where it cannot be) refuses a 32 MB file with "Failed to create ... because the disk was full" at creation and leaves no file (the BCL removes it); a source whose reservation fails, a volume with too little free space on a platform without `fallocate`, `/dev/full` through a symlink (the device is not sized) and a missing directory all surface as an `IOException` / `DirectoryNotFoundException` and leave nothing behind. **Not tested, by design:** a sparse file on a full disk (the mapped write would raise a signal and end the test host).

| gate | measured | result |
|---|---|---|
| G1 contract (reader against the in-RAM oracle, writer round trip, failure modes) | **108 Esa tests** (100 written first, 99 red and 1 green against the throwing stub; 8 added after the code in response to the audit). **Reader** against the bit-level oracle `TextOracle` (independent of the production code: the layout is computed bit by bit from its definition): `Symbol`, `Window` and `Key` at every position 0 .. n + 2 for every width b = 1 .. 8 and every length n = 0 .. 90; **exhaustive texts** (binary to length 11, 4-ary to 5, 8-ary to 3, 16-ary to 3, 256-ary to 2, 5-ary at b = 3 to 4: 76,988 texts) with every position, key, window, every pair for `Lce` on the vector and the scalar path, and the comparison of all suffix pairs; the key order against the lexicographic order of the first symbols; **alignment**: every bit offset around the byte offsets 4,096, 8,192 and 65,536 and the last 150 symbols and 200 past the end of a 70,000-symbol text for b = 1 .. 8; **random positions** in 2,000,003-symbol texts with planted repeats for b = 1 .. 8 (20,000 keys, symbols and windows, 2,000 common-prefix pairs on both paths), 8 threads reading one reader; past the end (any position up to `long.MaxValue` reads 0, negative positions are refused); `ReadSymbols` for every range of 70-symbol texts and the whole 10^6-symbol text with 300 random windows for b = 2, 3, 4, 8; `Lce` clamped to the text, step counts at the edges of the vector loop (max = 0, 1, 31, 32, 33, 127, 128, 129, 256, 1,000), the vector flag guarded by the hardware; **positions and offsets above 2^31 and 2^32 on sparse files with synthetic values**: b = 2 (4.4x10^9 symbols), 3 (3x10^9), 4 (4.3x10^9), 8 (2.3x10^9), 33 symbols planted at 2^31 - 3, 2^31, 2^32 - 3, 2^32, 2.15x10^9 and n - 40 (symbol, key, window, `ReadSymbols`, `Lce` all equal to the planted values), a section at the file offset 5x10^9, writers at offsets 4.9x10^9 and across 2^32; **damaged input** (all raise `InvalidDataException` with a message that names the damage, or the natural file exception, never a crash or a hang): truncated by 1 byte, by the padding, to the data, to 1 byte, empty; longer than the layout; a section of the wrong length, past the end of the file, at a negative offset; the unused bits of the last byte nonzero; each of padding bytes 0, 1, 31, 62, 63 nonzero; widths 0, -1, 9, 16, 33, `int.MinValue` (also on a 64-byte file, which would otherwise divide by zero); negative and enormous lengths; a text packed with width b opened with width b' (20 width pairs x 6 lengths: caught by the length or by `Verify`); a symbol above sigma, a wrong count, length, width, alphabet size or SHA-256 in `Verify`; a missing file and directory, a directory as the file, a null path; reads after `Dispose` (every member, also past the end, also at zero room) and the unmapping of the view (`/proc/self/maps`). **Writer**: round trip for 13 alphabet sizes (1 .. 256) x 6 lengths against the oracle, the width rule, an N-containing DNA text, all 256 byte values, `MinBits`; the file is independent of block (6 sizes) and window (4 sizes) and two writes are byte-identical; reads and writes counted (2n read, n b / 8 + 64 mapped, windows of at most the asked size); the length limit; **atomic finish** (the target does not exist between the passes, a changed input of three kinds leaves an existing target untouched and no `.tmp`, a missing directory is found before the input is read); failure modes: `/dev/full` through a symlink as the temporary file (both reservation modes), a real full file system (a 4 MB `tmpfs` mounted by the test), a source whose reservation fails, a volume with too little free space, a missing directory and input; a section of a larger file (dirty region overwritten, neighbours untouched) and at offset 5x10^9; the explicit fallback on a simulated platform. **Output file**: ranges with relative offsets, refusal outside the range, 4 threads on 4 ranges of one mapping (12 MB), `Flush`, `Complete`, disposal order, 0-length files, offsets above 2^32, the reservation modes against fake and real sources (a 256 MB file takes its blocks with `fallocate` and none sparse; the free space of the volume is the witness)  | **PASS** (253 External tests green, 0 skipped: the tmpfs test ran as root) |
| G2 equality with the prototype (differential tests in `EmProto.Tests`, prototype code unchanged) | **writer: byte-identical packed files to the prototype `Prepare` and the same N, width, alphabet, counts and SHA-256** on `dna_10m`, `prot_1m`, `rep_10m` (from `/tmp/stbench/data`), an N-containing text with IUPAC letters and runs of N (2 M symbols), a 256-symbol text (10^6 bytes), protein, binary, DNA and the 11 alphabets of sigma 1 .. 256 at every length 0 .. 40 (with blocks of 8 and windows of 64 bytes), two block / window settings each, and **`dna_100m` against the `.pk` and `.pk.meta` of the E9 setup (the E6 pipeline's file)**; **reader: `Key`, `Symbol` equal at 10^6 random positions per width b = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8** (positions 0 .. n, `Window` at every eighth), on `dna_10m`, `rep_10m`, `prot_1m` and the `dna_100m` file of E9 as well, and 1,000 or more random pairs per width and per path for `Lce` (equal results **and equal step counts**, vector and scalar, in both) and for `CompareSuffixes` (equal sign and prefix); **mapped writer: byte-identical files** for random `Append` / `WriteAt` scripts at three window sizes and for writes at offsets above 2^32 bytes (4,294,967,290 .. 4,999,999,998) on a 5 GB sparse file | **PASS** (18 tests) |
