| G4 portability | the writer and the reader on a simulated platform without `fallocate` and with free space known / unknown / too small (`FakeReservationSource`): no exception except the refusal of a file that does not fit; `AvailableFreeBytes` returns -1 for a directory that does not exist and for an empty path and never throws; the reader has no platform dependency (no `/proc`, no P/Invoke); the scalar path equals the vector path in every comparison of the reader tests and of the differential tests (the scalar path is what arm64 runs; `UseVectors` cannot be switched on without AVX2) | **PASS** (simulated; no run on macOS, Windows or arm64) |
| G5 no regression (test policy of PROGRESS-S1, level 2) | the affected set of `src/SuffixTree.Esa` (the only project that changed; the diff of `src` is five files in `External/`, `apps/` is untouched), run on the head `8ce2133f` in Release: **`SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default 3,022 passed + 8 skipped** (2,914 + 8 before, +108 new; 1 m 55 s), **Exhaustive 71 of 71** (9 m 18 s) and **Fuzz 30 of 30** (8 m 39 s) because `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed, **`SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` 672 of 672** (always for 1b.7), **`SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` 497 of 497** (479 + the 18 differential tests; it holds the new tests; 14 m 38 s; the three `[Explicit]` cost tests are not part of a run); `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` (references Esa, has no tests) builds. **Skipped, with the rule:** the checked build `-p:EsaBounds=true` (the new code has no `ESA_BOUNDS` conditional and reaches memory through raw pointers of a mapping, not through `Buf<T>`: nothing is compiled differently; the 1b.7a orchestrator skipped it on the same ground), Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props did not change). No widening trigger fired: no contract type of Core, container format, section table, shared props, shared test helper, base class, data file read by tests or reflection changed; **one new test starts a child process** (`mount` / `umount` for the 4 MB `tmpfs`, in `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests`, which ran in full; it is ignored where `mount` is not available), which the policy lists as a coupling that project references do not show: nothing outside that test class depends on it | **PASS** (nothing red, nothing skipped that the policy requires) |

Reading. (1) **The reader costs what the prototype costs** where it matters for PH3 (a key is 7.5 ns, +2 %) and the production file is byte for byte the file that E6-E8 produced. (2) **The scalar common prefix pays about 1.5 ns for the clamp** that makes it memory-safe for any caller; PH3 calls it only on key ties, and PH4 uses the kernel's own PLCP loop, so this is not the number that decides the builder's time; if the builder wants the prototype's raw loop it can call `Window` and compare itself. (3) **A full disk under a mapped window is now an exception at creation, for the price of 0.7 ms and no measurable change of the write pattern**; the cost is that the reservation is the BCL's `fallocate` (`FALLOC_FL_KEEP_SIZE`, then the length is set) and that a file system that does not support it ignores the request without an error, so `ReservationOutcome.Preallocated` says "the platform was asked", not "the blocks are there". (4) `Dist` and its target are unchanged (sparse); the builder constructs its big outputs through `MappedOutputFile` (open point (c) below).

**Mutation audit** of the new code (hand-made, watchdog, in a separate git worktree, analyzers off for the mutant builds; `analysis/mutation/make_1b7b_mutants.py`, `make_1b7b_round2.py`, `run_1b7b_mutants.py`, `run_1b7b_audit.sh`, `1b7b_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b7b.py`, `summary_1b7b.tsv`): four rounds, **201 mutants, 179 killed (5 of them by the watchdog on a hang or by a crash of the test host), 22 argued equivalent one by one (`1b7b_equivalent.py`), 0 open.** Round 1 listed 140 (119 killed, 17 survived, 4 did not compile): the survivors were 11 equivalents and 6 gaps of the tests (the alphabet and the counts of `Verify` need not be equally long; a width of 0 or -1 on a 64-byte file would divide by zero; a disposed window or reader that stays mapped is invisible to the file contents; the sparse creation of the public constructor of `MappedWindowWriter` was not observed; `MinBits` and the length change of `Pack` were not distinguished by message or by order), 3 of the 4 that did not compile were replaced (compiler errors of the mutated code; the fourth, `O15`, is a variant of the `fsync` equivalents and is argued); round 2 re-ran the 9 (all killed, the tests now read `/proc/self/maps` and the free space of the volume as witnesses); round 3 added the 46 mutants that were written and dropped before round 1 to bound the time (they are listed in `make_1b7b_mutants.py` as `DROPPED` and were never run before round 3) and 15 fresh ones on the vector loads, the verification loop, the layout check and the scan: 61, of which 44 killed, 16 survived (10 equivalent, 6 gaps: a negative length refused only after an existing file was touched, an explicit fallback that did not truncate, the width and the length checks that a 64-byte file lets through, `WindowBytes` checked after the input was read) and 1 did not compile (replaced); round 4 re-ran those 7 (all killed). **The audit found no defect of the production code, only gaps of the tests**; the equivalents are mostly the same two classes as in 1b.7a (durability: `fsync`; observability: a second `Dispose`, a disposal check that the BCL repeats) plus the two ways in which the common prefix may continue or stop at the partial symbol at the end of a window (same result, different step count) and the hardware guard of the vector flag, which a machine without AVX2 would kill.

Not verified: any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the simulated platform only; `fallocate` through the BCL is a Linux fact here, and the BCL's own equivalents on the other two were not run); a text of more than 10^8 symbols through the writer end to end (the reader and the mapped writer were run on sparse files with synthetic values at 2^31 and 2^32 symbols and above 2^32 bytes, the writer on `dna_100m`, which is 10^8; 3x10^9 would be ESTIMATES by x30: the writer's time per symbol has not been compared with the prototype's, only the bytes); the behaviour of `fallocate` on file systems other than ext4 and tmpfs (a file system without support ignores it); a sparse file on a full disk (the signal); a read that races `Dispose`; `ReadSymbols` is generic (no vector unpack: a 2-bit AVX2 unpack was not built, nothing needs it yet); `Verify` at 10^9 symbols; the `UseVectors` path on a machine without AVX2 (simulated by switching it off). Defects found: in the **prototypes** (not fixed, they are not touched): `PackedText.UseVectors` can be set to true on hardware without AVX2, `CompareSuffixes` of the same position returns 1, `Lce` compares the padding as symbol 0 when `max` exceeds the text, `Prepare` writes the target in place and does not notice an input that grew between the passes; in the **production code of 1b.7a**: a failed creation of a `MappedWindowWriter` left the file (now removed); in the **new code**: the cost defect of the common-prefix prologue (found by G3, fixed); the audit found no defect of the production code, only gaps of the tests.

**Open points handed to ADR-003 and the owner (the container format is the owner's decision; nothing here changes it).** **OPEN POINT 1 (the owner's, the container format): TEXT codec 4 is under-specified in ADR-003.** Section 4 kind 1 says only "Codec 4: reserved (2-bit packed DNA, S2)", ADR-004 sections 4.2 and 4.5 pull it forward into S1b as "2-bit (b-bit) packed" and Q3 of section 6a asks for `codec` and `bits_per_symbol` in the TEXT entry, but no document says where `bits_per_symbol` lives, which bit order is used, what the last byte and the tail look like, and what `COUNT * ELEM_WIDTH == LENGTH` (section 6, level `Header`) means for it. 1b.7b implements the layout of the prototype (the `.pk` file of E3-E8, the file the pipeline outputs and `e8-verify` checks), changed in no byte, and proposes this amendment to ADR-003 (not applied; ADR-003 is not edited here):

> `TEXT`, **codec 4: dense symbols packed with b bits.** Needs `ALPHABET` (as codec 2: the symbol `s` is the `s`-th smallest distinct code unit; only text whose code units are all below 256 is eligible). `b = max(1, ceil(log2 SIGMA))`, 1 <= b <= 8, is stored in `PARAM0` (`bits_per_symbol`); `PARAM1` = 0; `COUNT = n`; `ELEM_WIDTH` = 0 (the payload is not an array of whole bytes). Layout: one big-endian bit stream, symbol `i` in the stream bits `[i*b, (i+1)*b)`, the first bit of a byte is its most significant bit; the unused low bits of the last data byte are zero; **64 zero bytes follow the data and belong to the payload**, so `LENGTH = ceil(n*b/8) + 64` and a reader may load 8 bytes (or, for the 256-bit compare, 41 bytes) at the byte of any symbol `i <= n`; a read at or beyond `n` yields the symbol 0. Validation at level `Header` replaces `COUNT * ELEM_WIDTH == LENGTH` by `LENGTH == ceil(COUNT * PARAM0 / 8) + 64`, `1 <= PARAM0 <= 8`, `2^PARAM0 >= SIGMA`, the unused bits and the 64 padding bytes zero (O(1): a reader reads 65 bytes). Level `Sections`: the CRC-32C covers the whole payload including the padding. Level `Structure`: every symbol is below SIGMA and the symbol counts equal those of the build (the reader's `Verify`).

**OPEN POINT 2 (the owner's): `TEXT_SHA256`.** ADR-003 section 3 defines it as the SHA-256 of the text "as UTF-16LE code units"; ADR-004 section 4.2 (PH0) and the prototype hash the **input bytes** (the fingerprint of `state.json`). For a text of bytes the two differ. 1b.7b implements the prototype's value (`PackedTextInfo.Sha256` = SHA-256 of the input bytes, lower-case hex; `MappedTextReader.Verify` recomputes it from the symbols and the alphabet). Proposal: for codec 4 define `TEXT_SHA256` as the SHA-256 of the text's code units as bytes (one byte per code unit), and keep UTF-16LE for codecs 1 to 3 if S5 needs the logical-hash compatibility; or, if the UTF-16LE value is wanted for every codec, PH0 feeds the hasher a zero byte after every input byte (the cost is a second SHA-256 stream of the same volume; not measured here).

**OPEN POINT 3 (the owner's): the symbol counts `C[]`** (PH6 reads them, the bucket offsets of PH3 start from them) have no home in ADR-003: SIGMA and ALPHABET are there, the counts are not. Proposal: a `uint64` array `COUNTS` of SIGMA entries stored as a second payload of the `ALPHABET` section (or an own optional section kind from the reserved range 12-4095); they are cheap to recompute (one sequential pass of the reader) and `Verify` does that.

**OPEN POINT 4 (small, the owner's or the orchestrator's): the width for DNA with N and IUPAC.** Section 6a Q3 says "b = 4 when sigma <= 16 (A, C, G, T, N and IUPAC)"; the prototype and every verified file use `b = ceil(log2 sigma)`, i.e. 3 bits for sigma 5 to 8 (A, C, G, T, N) and 4 bits only from sigma 9. The writer follows the prototype; `PackedTextWriterOptions.MinBits` = 4 forces the other reading and the file stays valid (the width is in the entry). Nothing else in the layout depends on b. **Decided by the owner on 2026-11-18 (section 6c): points 1, 2 and 4 as the prototype and the proposals above, point 3 as an own optional section `COUNTS` (kind 13); ADR-003 is amended accordingly (its "Amendment 2026-11-18" note).**


**Open points handed to 1b.7c-e:** (a) 1b.7c-d: the reader's `Key`, `Symbol` and `Lce` are the prototype's functions; `Lce` and `CompareSuffixes` take `long` positions and clamp, and `Lce` is safe for any arguments (so the PH3 comparator needs no precondition); `ReadSymbols` is the sequential read for the scans of PH1 and for `Verify`; (b) 1b.7e: the TEXT section is written by `PackedTextWriter.Pack` into `MappedOutputFile.CreateWriter(sectionOffset, info.TotalBytes)` after `Scan` (the section sizes follow from `PackedTextInfo`), `Complete(fsync: true)` ends the file, the section CRC-32C is a sequential pass of the reader's mapping (not built), and `e8-verify` can be given an adapter that opens the TEXT section with `MappedTextReader.OpenRegion`; (c) every big output of the builder (SA, LCP, CHILD, NEXTSYM, PSI, the container itself) should be created through `MappedOutputFile.Create` with `Auto` so that a full disk is refused at creation, and the LCP files whose size is known after PH4-B2 are created then; `Dist`'s targets still use the sparse constructor: moving them is a change of the write pattern of 1b.7a that is not measured here (ISA by DIST scatters through windows in random order, the patterns measured here are sequential and per-symbol cursors); (d) the pre-flight of ADR-004 4.3 (free space >= final size + S_max + spill bound) can use `IReservationSource.AvailableFreeBytes`; (e) positions: the writer refuses n above 2^32 - 4 (`MaxSymbols`, the 32-bit positions of the builder); the reader has no such limit (tested at 4.4x10^9 symbols); (f) `PackedTextInfo.Counts` are the `C[]` of PH3 and PH6 and `Letters` the ALPHABET; their place in the container is open point 3.

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-18, 1b.7b, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `4aec286c`, tree clean; the diff since `b5316104` is new files in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (`MappedOutputFile`, `MappedTextReader`, `PackedTextFormat`, `PackedTextWriter`; 866 added lines in `src`) and an extension of the 1b.7a file `MappedWindowWriter.cs` (+56 / -15 lines: the public constructor unchanged, a failed creation now removes its file), tests, `analysis/**`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; ADR-003, `apps/` and every other existing production file are untouched; a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names found nothing. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,022 passed + 8 skipped** (2,914 + 8 before), Exhaustive **71 of 71**, Fuzz **30 of 30**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **497 of 497** (479 + 18 differential tests). Skipped by me with the rule: the checked build (no `EsaBounds` conditional and no `Buf<T>` in the new code), Vectors, Ukkonen, Mcp.Core (unchanged projects). One new test starts a child process (`mount` for a 4 MB tmpfs) and is ignored where `mount` is unavailable; it lives in `Esa.Tests`, which ran in full. **Own measurement of G3** with the implementer's harness (`run_1b7b_g3.sh`, the explicit cost test classes in `EmProto.Tests`) but my own input (my `e6` packed `dna_100m`, SHA-256 equal to the reference, and my BWT), 3 runs per cell, medians, production against prototype: **key (scalar) +3.0 %** (implementer +2.2 %), **symbol +5.2 %** (-0.4 %); **Lce on random pairs, scalar +19.1 %** (the reader class: +30.5 %), **vector +19.4 %** (the reader class: -11.0 %); near matches on `a_1m` scalar +1.0 %, vector -0.1 %; Fibonacci scalar +9.6 / +9.8 %, vector +4.8 / +7.7 %. The scatter of single runs of the same variant on random pairs is 17-28 ns, larger than the differences, but the sign agrees with the implementer's +10.6 % (scalar): **the +-10 % gate is NOT demonstrated for the Lce on random pairs (mine +19 %, the implementer's +10.6 %)**; the production Lce pays for being memory-safe for any argument (a clamp), about 3-4 ns per call. I accept this as a stated trade-off and add an open point for 1b.7d: if the profile of PH3 shows the Lce hot, an unchecked variant for trusted internal callers. **Writer and `posix_fallocate`:** the packed files are byte-identical to the prototype's (18 differential tests pass). Time and device writes of a 400 MB output for 10^8 symbols, cold cache, 3 runs: fallocate against sparse **+7.6 % (E9 Psi pattern) and +10 % (one sequential cursor) in total time, +8 % in wall time with the sync** (the implementer: -0.9 % and -1.8 %); device writes equal (4.170 and 4.170 B/char for the Psi pattern, 4.033 and 4.018 for one cursor); the prototype and the sparse creation are equal to each other (1.807 and 1.805 s). 64 KB windows cost 6.1-8.2 B/char against 4.17 (the flush-on-dispose finding of E9 reproduces). So the cost of `Auto = Preallocate` is between 0 and 10 % of the write time on this ext4 virtual disk (the two measurements disagree in sign); the choice stands on what it buys (a full disk is an `IOException` at creation, not a fault on a mapped page) with the I/O unchanged. **Mutation audit:** round 1 recounted from the result files, 140 rows as reported; the totals 201 mutants, 179 killed, 22 equivalent (argued), 0 open are the implementer's. **Not re-measured:** the differential tests beyond their passing, the reader on sparse files at 2^31 / 2^32 symbols (synthetic values), `fallocate` on file systems other than ext4 and tmpfs, anything on macOS, Windows or arm64, a text above 10^8 symbols through the writer, the writer's time per symbol against the prototype. **Cost:** the implementer reports about 3.3 h and 0.9-1.0 M tokens (an estimate; the counter shown to me covers only the part after the resume, 0.56 M), inside the forecast of 2-4 h and 0.6-1.0 M. **Verdict:** 1b.7b is accepted with the Lce exception above. **The four ADR-003 gaps of the implementer's subsection (TEXT codec 4 layout, TEXT_SHA256 of bytes against UTF-16 code units, the home of the symbol counts, the bit width for sigma 5..8) need the owner's decision before the container writer of 1b.7e; they do not block 1b.7c and 1b.7d.**

### 1b.7c: PH4 + PH5 on a given suffix-array file (2026-11-18)

The third of the packages 1b.7a-e of section 4.5 (authorised 2026-11-18, section 6c; forecast 3-5 hours and 0.8-1.2 M tokens, +-50 %, economy cap 7 hours or 1.8 M tokens, stated before the launch; gates G1-G6 and the abandon rule were fixed by the orchestrator before the work). Production code for **PH4 (base mode only) fused with PH5 through the B3 hook** exactly as E6 runs them (owner decisions 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-18: layout A, codec Packed, no run-length encoding of chains, the builder's gate RssAnon <= 320 MB at every n with the default parameters and four threads): input = the packed text (`MappedTextReader`) + the suffix-array file (`n + 1` little-endian `uint`, `SA[0] = n`) + options; output = the LCP base array (U8 / U16 / U32 by the rule of ADR-002 section 4, `LCP[0] = LCP[1] = 0`), the overflow list (pairs `(rank, value)`), the child table of layout A (4 bytes per entry, `n + 1` entries), the statistics text of the prototype's `.stats` (NODE_COUNT, internal nodes, LRS depth and rank, max LCP) and a result with the counters, the peaks and the RssAnon verdict. Code `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (namespace `SuffixTree.Esa.External`, **10 new files** in `External/`, `External/Lcp/` and `External/Child/`, 1,719 lines, all `internal`; **one existing file edited, `MappedTextReader.cs` (+7 lines: `Prefetch`)**, no file outside `External/`, no csproj, no line of `apps/` changed): `LcpChildBuilder.cs` (the stage: `LcpChildBuilder.Build`, `LcpChildOutputs`, `LcpChildOptions`, `LcpChildTuning`, `LcpChildParameters`, `LcpChildRules` = the parameter rule, `LcpChildResult`, `RssVerdict`), `Lcp/ExternalLcp.cs` + `Lcp/LcpBuild.cs` (PH4: B1 / B2 / B3 on `DistScratch` / `DistWriter` / `DistReader`, the hook `ILcpChunkConsumer`), `Lcp/PlcpScan.cs` (the PLCP loop over `MappedTextReader`), `Lcp/LcpWidth.cs`, `Lcp/LcpGeometry.cs` (the chunk arithmetic on `ChunkDivider`), `Lcp/LcpTuples.cs`, `Lcp/LcpOutputWriter.cs` (B3's write side over two `MappedWindowWriter` ranges), `Child/ChildScan.cs` (PH5: the lcp-interval scan on `SpillStack`, both codecs as struct sinks, NODE_COUNT / LRS), `Child/AlignedWindowWriter.cs` (the CHILD windows over a range of a `MappedOutputFile`). The prototypes are **not rewired and not touched**; layout F (PSV / NSV), NEXTSYM, the bounded PH4 mode and the second B3 thread are not built.

Choices made (small; none is a public API, a file format or a behaviour verified in E4-E9): (1) **The stage is path based, its writers are range based.** `LcpChildBuilder.Build(saPath, text, outputs, options)` creates three files (and the optional statistics file) with `MappedOutputFile.Create` in `Auto` mode, so a full disk is an `IOException` at creation (LCP files after B2, as 4.3 asks; CHILD at the first block, as E6 did), but `LcpOutputWriter` and `AlignedWindowWriter` take **a range of a `MappedOutputFile`** and `ChildScan.Create(n, file, start, options)` takes the range of the child table: 1b.7e can aim them at sections of the container without changing them. (2) **A corrupt suffix array is refused, not trusted:** besides the length and `SA[0] = n` of the prototype, B1 refuses an entry outside `[0, n)` and **B2 checks that every position of its chunk occurs exactly once with a bit set of R bits per task** (the prototype checks the tuple counts of the chunks only, which a repeated entry inside one chunk passes: by reading, not run on the prototype); the price is `R / 8` bytes per B2 task (1 MB at R = 8 M: 78.37 MB per task at the reference geometry, two tasks still fit under 160 MiB), the exceptions are `InvalidDataException` (data from a file, the convention of 1b.7b). A permutation that is not the suffix array of the text cannot be detected without the verifier of E8; the stage stays memory safe on it (the reader clamps every read). (3) **The CHILD file is created at the first block of the hook, not at the start**, as in E6: created earlier it would add 4 B/char to the disk peak that the 12 B/char of scratch already sets. (4) **The CHILD windows are counted in `IoCounters.MappedWriteBytes` at disposal (4 bytes per write, one atomic add per run) and `AddWindow` at every window**; the prototype did not count them (E6 added them at the run level), so the comparison of the counted I/O subtracts them. (5) **`ChildCodec.Tagged` is a parameter** (the owner chose Packed, which is the default): it costs a 15-line sink and the guard `n <= 2^30 - 1`; layout F is not built. (6) **Failure semantics:** every output of a failed build is removed (the LCP files by PH4 itself, the rest by the stage; a stale file at an output path is replaced only when the build gets as far as creating it, the header check of the suffix-array file comes first), the scratch subdirectories `ph4` and `ph5` are removed, the scratch root only when this build created it and it is empty; an `IOException` of the spill ends the build (the consumer's exception passes through B3 unchanged). (7) **The RssAnon gate is a verdict, not an exception:** the stage samples `/proc/self/status` every 10 ms with the sampler of 1b.7a and reports `RssAnonPeakMb` (null where there is no `/proc`) and `RssVerdict` = `Unavailable` / `Within` / `Exceeded` against the cap of the owner (320 MB; the figure is the whole process, as the owner's gate is). (8) **The parameter rule is `ScaleRules` for these two stages**: R = 8,000,000 (P = 375 at 3x10^9), the B2 workers from the 160 MiB cap at the reference geometry (two of four), buffers 1 MiB / 32 KB, windows 1 MiB x 8, stack blocks of 256 K entries, spill flush 16 MiB, caps 160 + 64 MiB; `ScaleN` / `RuleN` (the emulation of E3-E7) are kept because the comparison with the prototype at 100 Mbp needs the same geometry. (9) **`MappedTextReader.Prefetch`** (bounds-checked, a hint) replaces the prototype's raw pointer in the PLCP loop; **no unchecked `Lce` was needed** (G3). (10) The statistics file takes sigma from the caller (`LcpChildOptions.Sigma`): the stage does not know it. (11) fsync of the outputs is an option (`SyncOutputs`, off: the prototype's phases did not include it).

The first run of this gate (rows kept as `rows_1b7c_g3_first_harness.tsv`) timed the prototype without the deletion of its spill file and its scratch directories and made a^n look +19 %: the harness was corrected so that both sides time the build call including what it removes, and the run was repeated in full; the abandon rule (more than 1.5x after the unchecked-`Lce` option) did not come near, **and no unchecked `Lce` was built**. | **PASS** (-10.0 .. +4.3 % in nine cells, counted I/O equal in all, RssAnon +3.5 % at the reference parameters; no unchecked `Lce` needed; the abandon rule was not near) |

| G5 reported: Packed against Tagged on the read side, the production `.cld` of `dna_100m` loaded into the existing `PackedChild` / `ChildTable` / `EsaIndex` path (reflection on the private constructors; the in-RAM structures unchanged) and the Tagged table built in memory from the same suffix array and LCP; byte-equal to the in-RAM packed table entry by entry; three processes, four passes each (the first "cold", the others "warm"), 40 M child steps of a depth-first walk through `FirstLIndex` / `NextLIndex`, 300,000 counts of 20-mers that occur, 300,000 that do not, 100,000 locates of 12-mers | **Packed +15.1 % per child step in the warm passes (12.5 ns against 10.9 ns; the depth-first walk of 40 M steps: 0.500 s against 0.434 s, 0.484-0.552 against 0.418-0.460 over nine passes; +15.9 % cold, 0.501 against 0.432 s): below the 25 % that the brief says to flag to the owner.** The counts of 20-mers that occur (+11.0 % warm, +4.0 % cold), that do not occur (-3.0 % warm, +7.8 % cold) and the locates of 12-mers (-6.6 % warm, +4.9 % cold) are inside the spread of the passes (min-max ranges overlap); the child step of the walk is the one number that is stable. Production `.cld` equal to the in-RAM packed table entry by entry; answers equal on both codecs in every pass (`rows_1b7c_g5.tsv`, `tables_1b7c_g5.py`, `run_1b7c_g5.sh`; three processes, the first started with Packed, the second with Tagged, the third with Packed). The cause of the 15 % was not investigated (the Packed step needs the LCP value to tell the kinds apart; Tagged has the kind in bits 30-31 of the entry: a reading of the code, not a measurement). At 3x10^9 the Tagged codec cannot be used (2^30 - 1 symbols), so the choice of the owner stands: Packed. | reported |
| G6 no regression (test policy of PROGRESS-S1, level 2) | the affected set of `src/SuffixTree.Esa` (the only project of `src` that changed: the diff is 11 files in `External/`, `apps/` is untouched), run on the head `5ff27456` in Release (no source file changed afterwards; only documents): **`SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default 3,153 passed + 8 skipped** (3,022 + 8 before, +131 new; 1 m 28 s), **Exhaustive 73 of 73** (71 + the 2 new; 8 m 55 s), **Fuzz 32 of 32** (30 + the 2 new; 8 m 37 s), **the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) 3,154 passed + 7 skipped** (it is part of the set this time: the new code reaches memory through `Buf<T>`; 1 m 26 s), **`SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` 672 of 672**, **`SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` 505 of 505** (497 + the 8 differential tests; 10 m 10 s; the explicit cost tests are not part of a run); `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` builds. **Skipped, with the rule:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props did not change). No widening trigger fired: no contract type of Core, container format, section table, shared props, shared test helper, base class, data file read by tests or reflection changed; no new test starts a child process; the new tests read `/proc/self/fd` and `/proc/self/maps` where the platform has them (they degrade where it has not: `Assert.Ignore` / a null list), the differential tests read the prototype files of `/tmp/stbench`, which nobody changed | **PASS** (nothing red, nothing skipped that the policy requires) |

Reading. (1) **The fused stage costs what the prototype costs and writes what it wrote.** G3: the time of PH4 + PH5 is between -10 % and +4.3 % of the prototype's in nine cells of three runs (the spread inside a cell is up to 9 %), the counted I/O is equal to the byte in all of them, RssAnon at the reference parameters is +3.5 % (164.8 MB at 10^8 against the 320 MB gate of the owner), and the files of G2 are byte-identical at 10^8. (2) **What the production code does that the prototype does not costs nothing that a run resolves:** the permutation bit set of B2 (R / 8 bytes per task: +2 MB of arena at R = 8 M, not isolated in time), the bounds-checked reader (no unchecked `Lce` was needed: the abandon rule of 1.5x was not approached), the budget-charged arenas and the RSS sampler (-5.4 .. +2.7 %), and in return **a corrupt suffix array ends in an exception with no output left** (the prototype's checks pass a repeated entry inside one chunk: by reading, not run). (3) **Packed against Tagged: +15.1 % per child step** (not flagged, below 25 %), measured at 10^8 on a depth-first walk; whole queries move by -7 .. +11 % inside the spread; Tagged cannot exist above 2^30 - 1 symbols, so the owner's decision stands. (4) **The a^n case is the spill, as designed:** 16 B x (max LCP + 1) = 155,189,248 B at 10^7 (48 GB at 3x10^9: ESTIMATE, not run); the owner decided against the run-length encoding and the stage reproduces the prototype's bytes. (5) **Phase split at 10^8 (production, A cold):** B1 1.07 s, B2 2.97 s, B3 with the fused scan 2.84 s; the scratch peak is 12 B/char (1,200,000,000 B), as E4 measured and a test now pins.

**Mutation audit** of the new production logic (hand-made, watchdog, in a separate git worktree, analyzers off for the mutant builds; `analysis/mutation/make_1b7c_mutants.py`, `make_1b7c_fresh.py`, `make_1b7c_r4.py`, `make_1b7c_round2.py`, `run_1b7c_mutants.py`, `run_1b7c_audit.sh`, `1b7c_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b7c.py`, `summary_1b7c.tsv`): four rounds, **298 mutants, 271 killed (4 of them by the watchdog on a hang or by a crash of the test host), 27 argued equivalent one by one (`1b7c_equivalent.py`), 0 open.** Round 1 listed 235 (194 killed, 34 survived, 7 did not compile) over `LcpWidth`, `LcpGeometry`, `PlcpScan`, `LcpOutputWriter`, `LcpBuild`, `AlignedWindowWriter`, `ChildScan`, `LcpChildBuilder` and `Prefetch`; the survivors were 24 equivalents and **10 gaps of the tests**, closed with tests before round 2: a window size that is not a power of two (5, 6, 12, ...) was accepted, a failed `ChildScan.Create` was never checked for the arena it had allocated, the deletion of the Phi and rank chunk files as soon as they are read (the scratch peak is now pinned to 12 B/char, the file count to the chunk), a failure inside a B2 worker (every corrupt suffix array was also caught by a later check of the same type, so a swallowed worker exception went unseen), the final-length check (now `internal` and tested directly), the pair buffer of `LcpOutputWriter` that is only touched when a whole slice escapes. Round 2 re-ran 17 (15 killed, 2 equivalent: the Kasai invariant makes the carry reset at the smallest suffix redundant (P03), the other is a prefetch hint). Round 3 listed 55 fresh mutants of what the new tests reach (the plumbing of every parameter of the rule into its stage, the final-length check, the ranges of the CHILD writer, the buffers of PH4, the window bookkeeping): 52 killed, 1 equivalent, 1 gap (a lazy scan that does not close its file: the tests now read `/proc/self/fd` and `/proc/self/maps` after every build and every failure), 1 did not compile. Round 4 re-ran the gap and the mutant that did not compile and added 8 fresh ones on the descriptors and mappings (every `using` of a file or a mapping in PH4, the release of a CHILD window in PH5): 10 of 10 killed. The equivalents are of four kinds: hints (the prefetch, 5), checks that restate what another check of the same type guarantees (the geometry and the budget constructors, the writer's own completeness check, 10), unreachable states (a build that delivered no block, a position outside its chunk, 3) and mathematical identities (`LRS_DEPTH = max LCP`; the three `PlcpScan` limit mutants are equivalent **only because the production `Lce` clamps its length: an unchecked variant would make them real**, 9 in all).

Not verified: any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the simulated platform and the scalar path only); a text of more than 10^8 symbols (3x10^9: P = 375 chunks, 78.37 MB per B2 task, the time of PH4 + PH5 about 8 s per 10^8 symbols on this machine are ESTIMATES by x30; the E8 run on the owner's Mac measured the prototype at that size: 309.7 MB RssAnon); the child table of a text between 2^30 and 2^32 - 4 symbols (sparse files for the limit checks only; the Packed codec is exercised below 10^8); a build that takes the CHILD range at a section offset of a larger file (`ChildScan.Create` with `start` is tested, the builder does not offer it); the verifier of E8 on a text above 2x10^8; `SyncOutputs` on a real device (the fsync is a call, not timed); a full disk under a mapped window of a sparse file (the outputs are created with the reservation, the scratch is not: a full disk during the build is a signal in the scratch writes of `DistScratch`, an `IOException` there); the time of the permutation bits on its own (1 MB per task at R = 8 M; the production side of G3 is not slower, but the bits were not switched off for a run); the prototype was not run on a suffix array with a repeated entry inside one chunk (the claim that its counts pass it is by reading).

**Open points handed to 1b.7d-e.** Which of the earlier open-point bullets (E3b to E9, 1b.7a, 1b.7b) concern 1b.7c and what was done with each is listed in PROGRESS-S1.md, Log "S1b 2026-11-18 work package 1b.7c"; what stays with later packages: PH1-PH3 (the in-RAM base case, the sample buckets, the rank file as `Dist.Scatter`, the comparator: 1b.7d), PSI / BWT / PH6 and the container writer with its CRC pass (1b.7e), resume, 40-bit positions and both strands, NEXTSYM, layout F and the bounded PH4 mode (not built). For 1b.7d-e: (a) **1b.7e** calls `LcpChildBuilder.Build` with the SA file of PH3, the reader of the text and one `IoCounters` for the whole build (`LcpChildOptions.Counters`) and reads from `LcpChildResult` what the header needs (`Lcp.Kind`, `Lcp.OverflowCount` = `PARAM0` of `LCP`, `Scan.NodeCount`, `InternalCount`, `LrsDepth`, `LrsRank`, `MaxLcp`, `StatsText(sigma)` for `BUILD_INFO` or a side file); the three outputs are separate files at paths the caller gives: a container that wants them as sections either copies them (4-5 B/char of extra I/O) or builds `LcpOutputWriter` / `ChildScan` over `MappedOutputFile` ranges (their constructors take one) and calls `ExternalLcp` / `ChildScan` directly, which needs the section sizes of the LCP before B3 (the width is known after B2: the order of 4.3); (b) the two arena budgets (160 + 64 MiB) are created inside `LcpChildBuilder` from `LcpChildOptions.ArenaCapBytes` and `ChildArenaCapBytes`; a builder that wants one shared `ArenaBudget` across PH1-PH5 would pass `ExtLcpOptions.Budget` and `ChildBuildOptions.Budget` itself (both exist); (c) the RssAnon verdict is the whole process: the builder host (the MCP server, a test host) counts; 1b.7e reads `RssVerdict` and `RssAnonPeakMb` per phase if it wants per-phase figures; (d) PH4 needs the packed text open as a `MappedTextReader` for the whole stage (random reads, 8 per symbol of `Phi`), the SA file closed for writing and complete; (e) the scratch is `ScratchDir/ph4` and `ScratchDir/ph5`, 12 B/char at the peak of PH4 (E4) and the spill 16 B x (max LCP + 1) (a^n: 155 MB at 10^7, 48 GB at 3x10^9: the owner decided against the run-length encoding).

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-18, 1b.7c, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `f625f679`, tree clean; the diff since `9e6a1c51` is ten new files in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (1,719 lines), `MappedTextReader.cs` +7 lines (`Prefetch`), tests, `analysis/**`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; ADR-002, ADR-003, `apps/`, every csproj and every other existing production file are untouched, the prototypes are not touched; every commit has both trailers; a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names and for a model identifier found nothing. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me, all on `f625f679`):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,153 passed + 8 skipped** (2 m 12 s; 3,022 + 8 before), Exhaustive **73 of 73** (10 m 58 s), Fuzz **32 of 32** (9 m 17 s), the **checked build** (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`; the new code uses `Buf<T>`) **3,154 + 7 skipped**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **505 of 505** (15 m 23 s; 497 + 8 differential tests); skipped with the rule: Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), Ukkonen and Mcp.Core (unchanged projects). Nothing ran in parallel with a timing run. **Own G2 at 10^8** (the implementer's cost-test class as the driver, my own script, inputs hard-linked, 4 threads, the 3x10^9 geometry scaled, cold cache): SHA-256 of `.pk .sa .lcp .ovf .cld` equal to `/tmp/stbench/e7/<name>.refs.sha` and the `.stats` file equal to the one of `e6`, on `dna_100m` and `genome_100m`; **`e8-verify` 11 of 11 on the production outputs of both** (4.5 s and 5.9 s of verification). **Own G3** (the same class, cold cache before every run, variants alternating inside a run number, 3 runs per cell, medians of the build call; production against the prototype's fused PH4 + PH5 on the same files): `dna_100m`, scaled geometry (R = 266,667): 7.49 s -> 6.78 s, **0.906**; `genome_100m`, scaled geometry: 7.68 s -> 6.99 s, **0.909**; `dna_100m`, the real R = 8,000,000 (P = 13): 7.69 s -> 7.46 s, **0.971** (whole process wall time including the start of the test host: 0.904, 0.938, 0.982); `.lcp .ovf .cld` equal to the reference in **all 18 runs**; the gate (+-10 %) is met, the production code is not slower than the prototype (the cause of the 3-9 % gain was not investigated; candidates are the bounds-checked `Prefetch` in the PLCP loop and the cost of the prototype's disposal). **RssAnon:** the test's own sampler 59.6 MB (scaled geometry) and 161.0 MB (R = 8 M) for production against 59.4 and 154.9 MB for the prototype (+3.9 %); **my external sampler of the test-host process (children of the `dotnet test` process, 10 ms): 154.8 MB production, 155.0 MB prototype at R = 8 M**, the gate of 320 MB is met by 2x (my first external sampler read the `dotnet test` command process, not the test host, 26 MB: discarded and redone). The warm-cache cell and the a^n cell of the implementer's table were not re-run by me. **Own fault injection on the 100 M suffix array** (a copy with one edit each, run through the production stage): a repeated entry inside a chunk -> `InvalidDataException` "text chunk 18 holds 266668 Phi tuples, expected 266667: the suffix array is not a permutation"; an entry of 4,294,967,280 -> `InvalidDataException` "sa[60000001] ... is outside [0, 100000000)"; `SA[0]` = 0 -> `InvalidDataException`; each leaves no output file, no scratch file and no open descriptor (only the empty scratch directory that the harness itself creates); **two adjacent entries swapped (still a permutation, wrong order) is accepted silently and produces wrong files**, as the subsection says (the verifier's `sa_order` is the check for this, not the builder: 1b.7e must not rely on PH4 to refuse an unsorted suffix array, and the validation levels of the container must say so). **Mutation audit recounted** from `summary_1b7c.tsv` and the listed files: 298 distinct mutants (317 runs), **271 killed, 27 equivalent, 0 open**; I did not re-argue the 27 equivalents one by one. **My own 13 fresh mutants** (the PLCP carry decrement and the two escape boundaries, the width rule bars and the U16 escape value, the last-child flag, the left boundary after a pop, the packed sink's `k != previous + 1`, the up tag, the LRS tie rule, the rank-chunk count and the last rank chunk's length), run through the implementer's runner in a separate worktree (removed afterwards): **13 of 13 killed**, by the first-stage tests. **G5 re-run by me** (3 processes, cold cache before each, the first variant alternating): the pure child step (a depth-first walk of 40 M steps) **Packed +16.7 % cold, +16.3 % warm** against Tagged (implementer +15.1 %); the whole queries between 0 and +22 % (cold: count of present 20-mers +10 %, absent +4 %, locate of 12-mers 0 %; warm: +4 %, +20 %, +22 %) with a spread of the same size (the Tagged passes alone vary by about 10 % inside one process), so they are not resolved: **nothing exceeds the 25 % that is to be flagged, the owner's codec decision stands; the figure to quote is about +16 % per child step**. **Not verified by me:** anything on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the platform cases are simulated); anything above 10^8 (the 3x10^9 figures are ESTIMATES: P = 375, 78.37 MB per B2 task, 48 GB of a^n spill, about 8 s per 10^8); the claim that the prototype's per-chunk tuple counts pass a repeated entry inside a chunk (by reading, not run); the 27 equivalence arguments; the child table between 2^30 and 2^32 - 4 symbols (sparse limit checks only); a full disk in the scratch during the build. **Cost of the package (agent's report):** about 3.7 hours of wall clock (forecast 3-5 h met), about 1.2 M tokens by the earlier packages' convention (the forecast's upper edge, 0.8-1.2 M), the cap (7 h, 1.8 M) not reached.

### 1b.7d: PH1-PH3, the difference-cover rank file, the partition and the bucket sort, to the suffix-array file (2026-11-18)

The fourth of the packages 1b.7a-e of section 4.5 (authorised 2026-11-18, section 6c; forecast 3-5 hours and 0.8-1.3 M tokens, +-50 %, economy cap 7 hours or 1.8 M tokens, stated before the launch; gates G1-G6 and the abandon rule were fixed by the orchestrator before the work). Production code for **PH1 (the rank file of the difference cover, E3b), PH2 (the partition by splitters) and PH3 (the bucket sort, E3) exactly as E6 runs them**: input = the packed text (`MappedTextReader`) + options; output = the suffix-array file (`n + 1` little-endian `uint`, `SA[0] = n`, created at its final length through `MappedOutputFile.Create` in `Auto` mode, written through mapped windows of at least 1 MiB) and a result with the counters, the peaks and the RssAnon verdict, in the style of `LcpChildBuilder` / `LcpChildResult`. The rank file is scratch: PH1 creates it, PH3 reads it through a read-only mapping, and it is deleted when PH3 is done. With this package PH0 (`PackedTextWriter`) -> PH1 -> PH2 / PH3 -> PH4 / PH5 (`LcpChildBuilder`) run in one process with **no prototype code**. Code `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (namespace `SuffixTree.Esa.External`, **15 new files** in `External/`, `External/Dcs/`, `External/Sort/` and `External/Sa/`, 3,274 lines, all `internal`; **two existing files edited, `MappedTextReader.cs` (+23 / -4: the reader of the reduced strings) and `Dist.cs` (+5 / -1: `DistOptions.Files`)**, no other file of `src`, no csproj and no line of `apps/` changed): `Sa/SaBuilder.cs` (the stage: `SaBuilder.Build`, `SaBuildOptions`, `SaBuildTuning`, `SaBuildRules` = the parameter rule, `SaBuildParameters`, `SaBuildResult`), `Dcs/DifferenceCover.cs` (Singer and pruned covers, ported unchanged), `Dcs/SampleGeometry.cs`, `Dcs/RankTable.cs` (`FastMod32`, the read-only rank file), `Dcs/BucketRun.cs` (`ScratchLedger`, `RunStore`, `BucketRun`, `RunReader`), `Dcs/NameSinks.cs`, `Dcs/RankInverter.cs` (the inverse permutation as `Dist.Scatter` with `SampleRankMapper`), `Dcs/SampleNamer.cs` (the naming of the sample: sample buckets, merge, names), `Dcs/ExternalDcs.cs` (the level recursion and the base case), `Sort/SuffixOrder.cs` (the DCS comparator), `Sort/LeafSorter.cs`, `Sort/PositionStore.cs`, `Sort/SaSink.cs`, `Sort/BucketSorter.cs` (PH2 / PH3), `SeededRandom.cs` (SplitMix64 and `WorkPool<T>`).

Choices made (small; none is a public API, a file format or a behaviour verified in E3-E9): (1) **The stage is path based.** `SaBuilder.Build(text, saPath, options)` creates the suffix array **before PH1** (`Auto` reservation, so a full disk is an `IOException` at the first step, not a fault on a mapped page in PH3; the prototype made the file sparse (`SetLength`) and mapped one view per slice of K x 4 bytes, 133 kB at the scaled geometry, 4 MB at the real one, with this package the suffix array is written through windows of at least 1 MiB as the E9 rule asks) and the rank file `ranks.bin` in the scratch root; PH1 uses `ScratchDir/ph1`, PH3 `ScratchDir/ph3`. (2) **The rank file is written by `Dist.Scatter`** (`DistTarget` over the slots, a skipping mapper `SampleRankMapper` that drops the empty suffix and the class separators and counts what it emitted); **existing-file edit 1: `DistOptions.Files`**: when set, `Scatter` creates its target through `MappedOutputFile.Create` (reservation, `CreateWriter`), otherwise it is sparse as before (1b.7a callers unchanged). (3) **The reduced strings of the recursion are read by `MappedTextReader`** (**existing-file edit 2: `OpenReduced` and `FromMemoryReduced`**, 32-bit big-endian symbols with the 64 bytes of padding, the same `Key` / `Symbol` / `Lce` / `CompareSuffixes` as the byte reader; `ReadSymbols` and `Verify`, which are for the 1 to 8 bits of the file codec, refuse such a reader) (the prototype's `PackedText` took 1 to 32 bits; `MappedTextReader` took 1 to 8, and a second reader class was not wanted). (4) **The base case** is `SaisReference.Build` on an `IntSymbols` view at most N_base = 16 Mi symbols (the owner's decision, unchanged); above it the external bucket sorter runs on the reduced string. (5) **Failure semantics as in 1b.7c:** every output of a failed build is removed (the suffix array, the rank file, the scratch subdirectories; the scratch root only when the build created it and it is empty), no descriptor and no mapping stays open, and **the exception keeps its type** (`IOException`, `InvalidDataException`, `ArenaBudgetExceededException`, `ArgumentException`, a hook's own exception): the prototype wrapped worker failures in `InvalidOperationException` ("sample naming failed", "build failed"). (6) **No static counters:** the file numbers of `RunStore` and `PositionFiles` belong to the store, the scratch figures to a `ScratchLedger` per build, and the files of the nested sort of a level carry the tag of the build, so a failed PH1 leaves nothing. (7) **Checks the prototype does not make:** the inversion refuses a reduced string with more entries than samples and checks that it ranked every sample position (`Emitted == Samples`), `RankTable.Open` checks the length of the rank file (`InvalidDataException`), the suffix array is length-checked at the end, and **`VerifyRanks` (option, off by default)** reads the rank file once between PH1 and PH2 / PH3 (distinct ranks in [1, S + |D|], the unused slots zero). (8) The RssAnon gate (320 MB at four threads) is a verdict in the result (`Unavailable` without `/proc`, `Within`, `Exceeded`), never an exception. (9) **The parameter rule is the one of E6** (`ScaleRules`, `ScaleN` / `RuleN`: K_s, N_base, R, K_sort = the reference values x n / ScaleN with the floors 3 / 4 / 16 / 3, K = n x 10^6 / ScaleN, run spill 64 KiB x f; ScaleN 0 = the reference values), the same periods (a Singer period near 1024, 993) and the same seeds, so that the bytes are those of the prototype; the output never depends on any of them. (10) **The comparator calls the clamped `MappedTextReader.Lce`** (G5 below: no unchecked variant was built). (11) **The BWT is not produced by PH3** (an open point, as the brief fixed).

Gates (fixed by the orchestrator before the work; the numbers are mine, measured on the head named in the PROGRESS entry):

| gate | measured | result |
|---|---|---|
| G1 contract (the stage against the oracles: the naive suffix array, the definition of the sample ranks, the prototype's difference covers) | **152 new default Esa tests** (121 written first and run against a throwing stub: 117 red, 4 green that pin behaviour that already existed; the rest after the code, from the review of the parts and from the audit: `SaPinnedTests` (9 cases: the shape of the work and the counted I/O pinned) and `SaGapTests` (12 tests)) **+ 5 explicit tests** in the Exhaustive and Fuzz classes. **Oracles:** the naive suffix array (PH2 / PH3 and the whole stage), the definition of the sample ranks (groups of equal 64-bit key and X-prefix, class-major reduced string, ranks from 1) for PH1, the prototype's covers (differential). **Exhaustive (**76 of 76**, 9 m 49 s in Release (75 in the full run; the 76th, the PH1 test over the strings, after the correction of two wrong assertions of mine: the expected number of binary strings was 4,094 instead of 8,190 and a depth assertion cannot hold for strings of 14 symbols; 33 s together with the whole-stage test): **12,847 strings** through PH1 and through the whole stage, and **72 family runs** (nine families at 10^5 and 10^6 x four settings of K, threads and geometry) with identical SHA-256 per family and equal to the oracle):** every string over sigma 1 up to length 14, sigma 2 up to 12, sigma 3 up to 7 and sigma 4 up to 5 through PH1 and through the whole stage with rotating tiny parameters (X 2-57, K_s 3-22, N_base 4-43, K 3-50, fan-out 2-5, oversample 1-5, windows of 1-200 bytes, 1-4 threads), and the nine families at 10^5 and 10^6 symbols with four settings of K and of the thread count each (one of them the scaled geometry). **Fuzz (**34 of 34**, 8 m 42 s at the default seed and scale; the two new tests draw the text from the generator families of the S1a harness (alphabets up to 256 symbols) and the period, K_s, N_base, K, fan-out, oversample, windows, threads and seed per case (1,500 cases of up to 300 symbols with tiny parameters and 200 cases of up to 20,000 symbols with random parameters per seed and scale unit): **six seeds at scale 10 are 102,000 cases, all equal to the oracles** (seeds 1-6, 1 m 5 s each)).** **Cover property:** every X from 2 to 4,096 is a cover by exhaustion (`Verify` and an independent check), the Singer sets are perfect difference sets, the covers of X up to 300 and the nearest Singer periods are pinned by SHA-256. **Determinism:** two runs give the same file and the same statistics; 1, 2 and 4 threads x K of 9, 400 and 5,000 x seeds give the same SHA-256 on six families. **Failure modes** (each leaves no suffix array, no rank file, no scratch directory (the scratch root only if the build created it), no open descriptor, no mapping and nothing in the scratch account): a full disk at the creation of every one of the files in turn (an injected reservation source), a spill that fails at the naming and at the partition, an arena cap that is too small (PH1 and PH2 / PH3 each: the cap is one budget per phase, PH2 / PH3 is reached exactly when PH1 fits; a sweep of 14 caps), a hook that throws at every hook point with 1 and 3 threads and at about every 45th call of PH1 (the exception keeps its type), a disposed reader, a short or damaged text, a text above the design limit, a reader of 32-bit symbols, options out of range (refused before any step runs), an existing scratch root (kept, empty or with a foreign file, also a file whose name looks like another build's tag). | **PASS** |

One run: PH0 0.52 s, PH1 1.86 s, PH2 / PH3 13.09 s, PH4 / PH5 6.77 s (`dna_100m`), RssAnon of the process 114.8 MB (own sampler). | **PASS** |

The call as a whole is 0.993 .. 1.093 of the prototype's; **0.28-0.35 s of the production call lie outside both phase clocks** and the prototype's timed region leaves the same work outside (the construction of the difference cover, 993 residues; the harness builds it before its clock), see G5. **Counted I/O (equal):** PH1 reads 25,780,456 B and writes 25,780,456 B (`dna_100m`), 82,833,076 / 82,820,080 B (genome-like, A), mapped 12,890,228 / 26,196,004 B; PH2 / PH3 reads 784,815,468 B, writes 784,429,740 B, mapped 400,000,004 B (the suffix array). **Scratch peak** (PH1: 25,780,456 B on `dna_100m`, the same figure on both sides once the prototype's harness counts the rank file as the pipeline does; whole build 427-433 MB against 427-432 MB, a difference of -1.2 .. +0.8 % that follows the thread schedule: the differential test asserts at most +2 %). **Windows:** the suffix array is written through windows of at least 1 MiB (a test pins the default parameters; the prototype mapped one view per slice of K x 4 bytes). **RssAnon of the test host** (the builder's own sampler, 10 ms, and the independent `sample_rss_1b7a.py`, which agree within 1.3 MB): production 81-97 MB (A) and 117-148 MB (B) against 79-92 and 120-155 MB for the prototype, **at most 147.7 MB at the real geometry against the gate of 320 MB**; in the whole-chain run 114.8 / 102.4 MB. Reading. (1) **PH1-PH3 cost what the prototype's cost and write what it wrote:** per phase -3.8 .. +9.2 %, the counted I/O equal to the byte in all cells, the files byte-identical at 10^8 on two texts and on the 10^6 families, the scratch within the thread jitter. (2) **The comparator needs no unchecked `Lce`.** (3) **A full disk is found first**: the suffix array is created, with its reservation, before PH1 starts; the prototype discovered it as a fault on a mapped page. (4) **What the production code does that the prototype does not** (failure handling, the type of the exception, the tag of the files, the length checks, the windows of at least 1 MiB) costs nothing that the cells resolve. (5) The 0.28-0.35 s that the production call spends outside the two phase clocks are in every variant, also with sparse files and without the sampler; the prototype's harness builds the difference cover before its clock, the production call builds it inside: **measured:** the call spends **0.28 s before PH1 starts** (6 runs, 0.277-0.286 s: `DifferenceCover.Create` for the Singer period 993, the parameter rule, the creation of the suffix array) and **4-8 ms after PH3**, while the prototype's harness builds the same cover in **0.284 s** (3 runs) before its clock starts; the whole-call ratios of G3 are therefore too high by that 0.28 s (1.8 % in cell A, 4-5 % in the cells B at 6-7 s), and the phase ratios, which are measured alike on both sides, are not affected. The cost is fixed (independent of n).

**Mutation audit** of the new production logic (hand-made, watchdog, in separate git worktrees, analyzers off for the mutant builds; `analysis/mutation/make_1b7d_mutants.py`, `mutants_1b7d_data.py`, `split_1b7d_halves.py`, `run_1b7d_mutants.py`, `run_1b7d_audit.sh`, `SuffixTree.Esa.Slim1b7d.csproj.template`, `1b7d_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b7d.py`, `summary_1b7d.tsv`): one round of 285 mutants (the lists hold 561; the even positions of each list were run), three more rounds on the survivors and their gaps. Stage 1 = the External tests of `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` in a slim project (the filter of each file names its test classes), watchdog 300 s (a hang or a crash of the test host counts as killed), the two slowest tests (`TheOutput_IsIdentical*`, 30 s each: five builds per mutant) left out; analyzers off and warnings not errors for the mutant builds. **Round 1a: 285 mutants, 194 killed (7 by the watchdog, 6 by a crash of the test host), 9 did not compile (compiler warnings as errors: rebuilt in 1s), 82 survived.** The survivors showed gaps of the tests (the shape of the partition and of the sample buckets, the counted I/O, the position store, the radix sort on keys that differ in one digit, the budgets of the two phases, the scratch root and the accounts of the caller, the boundary of the base case, a failure at every call of the hook and under every arena cap, a reduced string in memory, the covers) and `SaPinnedTests` (9 cases) and `SaGapTests` (12 tests) were written for them. **Round 1s: the 82 and the 9, 91 mutants with the new tests: 50 killed (1 by a crash), 41 survived.** **Round 2: 4 more gaps** (a second `Dispose` of a file source, an option refused before any step, the ledger of the caller after a failure, equal splitter indices through a pinned case): **4 of 4 killed.** **Heavy pass:** the 37 that are argued below against the two tests that were left out: 37 survived, as expected. **Final: 285 run, 248 killed, 27 argued equivalent, 10 kept as a second line of defence (not equivalent and not killed: no injectable fault reaches them), 0 open;** the 276 mutants at the odd positions were not run (the budget of the package); stage 2 (the differential tests of `EmProto.Tests`) was not run on any mutant, all 248 kills are by the Esa tests; the 14 kills by the watchdog or a crash were not re-run one by one (the kernel killed a runaway mutant for lack of memory twice during the audit). The 27 equivalent ones are argued one by one in `1b7d_equivalent.py` (for example: `Lce` clamps, so the limit of the common prefix is free; the retry of a degenerate partition is dead code; `Verify` and `IsPerfect` are used by tests only; an fsync is not observable); the 10 of the second line are checks that sit behind a tested check at another level, or failure paths in the middle of a call (the disposal of the writers of a partition when the scan fails after other writers spilled, the flag of the rank file after a failure inside the inversion, the open-writer check of `Complete`).

Defects found. **In the prototype** (not fixed there, not touched; fixed or avoided in the production code; by reading and by the failure tests): (1) a failure of a worker is wrapped in `InvalidOperationException` ("sample naming failed", "build failed"), so the caller cannot tell an `IOException` (a full disk) from a defect: the production code rethrows the original exception (`ExceptionDispatchInfo`); (2) the `pos-*.bin` files of the nested `EmBuilder` of a level carry no tag of the build, so a failed PH1 leaves them behind, and the rank file is not removed on a failure: the production files carry the tag of the build and a failed build removes all of them (tests with an injected full disk at the creation of every file, a failing spill, a throwing hook at every point, a budget too small); (3) static counters (`BucketRun.s_counter`, `PosWriter.s_fileCounter`, `ScratchTracker`, `MappedIo`) that two builds in one process share: per-store numbering and a `ScratchLedger` and `IoCounters` per build; (4) nothing checks that the inverted suffix array of the reduced string ranks every sample position (a defect of a level would leave zeros in the rank file and a wrong order in PH3 without a message): `Emitted == Samples` and the optional `VerifyRanks`; (5) `EmBuilder.Partition` does not dispose its writers when a step fails (descriptors stay open until the finalizer): disposed on every path; (6) `oversample *= 2` of the degenerate retry is an `int` (overflow after 26 retries of one bucket, the point (c) of E8): ported as it is because the loop ends as soon as the sample is the whole bucket (the splitters are then exact quantiles and the split cannot be degenerate), so **no test reaches even one retry** and the overflow is unreachable below 2^31 sampled positions; the retry itself is dead code, argued in the audit. **In the new code** (found by the tests and the audit before the commit named in PROGRESS-S1): none in the logic: after the code was green on the contract tests the audit and the sweeps of failures showed gaps of the tests, never a wrong result of the production code (a failed `BucketSorter.Run` leaves the bytes of its pending buckets in a ledger that is not its own, which the builder releases as a whole: a design, pinned by a test). **In the tests and harnesses:** the first oracle of PH1 assumed that sample names are the ranks of the groups of equal X-prefixes, while the prototype groups by the 64-bit key and the X-prefix (fixed: the oracle takes both and also asserts the number of distinct names and of shared positions); the G2 harness wrote a three-line `.pk.meta` where `e8-verify` reads seven (fixed: n, bits, sigma, letters, counts, SHA-256, name); the first G3 harness subtracted the rank file from the prototype's PH1 scratch peak twice (the cold rows of `rows_1b7d_g3.tsv` carry the prototype's figure without it; read as reported + 12,890,228 B they equal the production figure to 12 B; fixed for the warm rows); a test of the thread-count identity took 2.5 minutes at 15,000 symbols (now 30 s at 3,000, the same checks).

Not verified (stated, not hidden): any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the simulated platform and the scalar path only); a text of more than 10^8 symbols (the figures of 3x10^9 are ESTIMATES; E8 measured the prototype at that size on the Mac); **the external levels of the recursion at the real geometry**: at 10^8 symbols the reduced string has 3.2 M symbols and fits N_base = 16 Mi, so the real-geometry cells (B) end in the base case; the external levels run in the tests (tiny parameters, up to level two), in the scaled geometry (N_base about 0.5 M, one or two levels) and in the G2 / G3 cells A, and the real geometry reaches them above about 5x10^8 symbols (not run); the page-cache floor of PH1 (185 text scans, ADR-004 6b) and of PH3 under a limited page cache (the E9-class 0.5x test); `SyncOutput` on a real device; a full disk during the build at a real size (an injected reservation source only); the unreachable retry of the partition; a text between 2^31 and 2^32 - 4 symbols (the limit is checked with a sparse file only; positions are `uint`); both strands (40-bit positions); resume; the BWT (not produced by PH3).

**Open points handed to 1b.7e.** Which of the earlier open-point bullets (E3b to E9, 1b.7a to 1b.7c) concern 1b.7d and what was done with each is listed in PROGRESS-S1.md, Log "S1b 2026-11-18 work package 1b.7d". (a) `SaBuilder.Build(text, saPath, options)` is path based and creates the suffix array itself (`MappedOutputFile.Create`, `Auto`); a container writer that wants it as a section either copies it (4 B/char of I/O) or needs an overload that takes a `MappedOutputFile` and a range (`MappedSaSliceSink` already takes the file object): a few lines, not done. (b) One `IoCounters` for the whole build: `SaBuildOptions.Counters` and `LcpChildOptions.Counters`; the result carries `Io`, `Ph1Io`, `Ph23Io`, the arena and scratch peaks per phase, the RssAnon peaks per phase and for the call, `Reservation`, `RecursionLevels`, `Parameters` (what the header and the report need). (c) The two arena budgets (160 MiB each, the phases run one after the other) and the two of PH4 / PH5 (160 + 64 MiB) are separate; the peak of the process is the largest of the stages, not the sum (the whole-chain run: 114.8 MB RssAnon); one shared `ArenaBudget` is `SortOptions.Budget` and `ExtDcsOptions.Budget` set by the caller. (d) **PSI / BWT / PH6:** PH3 does not produce the BWT (one random text read and one byte per suffix inside the leaf sorter would, ADR-004 5a E9 point d); the primary row (the row of suffix 0) has to reach PH6 from the SA file (a scan) or from PH3 (one number, not built). (e) The text must stay open for PH1-PH3 and PH4 (random reads); the scratch of the stage is `ScratchDir/ph1`, `ph3` and `ranks.bin` (about 8 B/char at the PH3 peak and 0.26 B/char of rank file), the rank file is removed when PH3 is done. (f) The RssAnon verdict is the whole process; with `MeasureRss` off or without `/proc` it is `Unavailable`. (g) The reduced strings of the recursion are read by the same `Lce`, `Key` and `CompareSuffixes` as the byte reader (32 bits per symbol, the vector path included; `ReducedTextReaderTests` compare them with a naive oracle on both paths). (h) The orchestrator's finding of 1b.7c (two adjacent entries swapped in the suffix array are accepted by PH4) is about the input of PH4: the production PH3 now writes that input, and `e8-verify`'s `sa_order` stays the acceptance check of the container (1b.7e).

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-19, 1b.7d, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `4e1e4b57`, tree clean; the 19 commits since `91d7650a` are 15 new files in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (`Dcs/`, `Sort/`, `Sa/`, `SeededRandom.cs`), `Dist.cs` +5/-1 (`DistOptions.Files`) and `MappedTextReader.cs` +23/-4 (the reduced reader), tests, `analysis/**`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; ADR-002, ADR-003, `apps/`, `wiki/`, every csproj and every other existing production file are untouched (the only `csproj` in the diff is the template `analysis/mutation/SuffixTree.Esa.Slim1b7d.csproj.template`, which only the audit runner copies into a throw-away worktree), the prototypes are not touched; every commit has both trailers; a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names and for a model identifier found nothing. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me on `4e1e4b57`):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,305 passed + 8 skipped** (4 m 10 s, Release; 3,153 + 8 before, +152 as stated), Exhaustive **76 of 76** (14 m 21 s), Fuzz **34 of 34** (9 m 57 s), the **checked build** (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) **3,306 + 7 skipped**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **513 of 513** (16 m 39 s; 505 + 8 differential tests); skipped with the rule: Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), Ukkonen and Mcp.Core (unchanged projects); nothing ran in parallel with a timing run. **Own G2 at 10^8** (the implementer's cost-test class as the driver, variant `e2e`, my own script, 4 threads, the 3x10^9 geometry scaled, cold cache): the whole production chain PH0 -> PH1 -> PH2/PH3 -> PH4/PH5 gives SHA-256 of `.pk .sa .lcp .ovf .cld` **equal to the E7 references** and `.stats` equal to `e6` on `dna_100m` and `genome_100m`; **`e8-verify` 11 of 11 on both** (4.6 s and 6.2 s); one cold run per text: PH0 0.63 / 0.53 s, PH1 2.25 / 2.39 s, PH2/PH3 13.19 / 13.58 s, PH4/PH5 6.98 / 7.32 s. **Own G3** (the same class, variants `proto` and `prod` alternating inside a run number, cold cache before every run, medians of the phase times; PH1 and PH2/PH3 each against the prototype's `ExternalDcs` + `EmBuilder`): scaled geometry (cell A, 3 runs) `dna_100m` PH1 1.62 -> 1.72 s (**+6.3 %**), PH2/PH3 13.56 -> 13.15 s (**-3.0 %**), `genome_100m` PH1 2.11 -> 2.19 s (**+3.8 %**), PH2/PH3 13.60 -> 13.32 s (**-2.1 %**); **real geometry** (cell B): the first 3-run set gave `dna_100m` PH2/PH3 5.47 -> 6.06 s (**+10.9 %**, 0.9 point over the gate, the prototype's own runs spread 5.35-5.83 s), so I repeated cell B with **7 runs per variant on both texts**: `dna_100m` PH1 0.304 -> 0.305 s (+0.1 %), PH2/PH3 5.59 -> 5.95 s (**+6.6 %**), `genome_100m` PH1 0.965 -> 0.910 s (-5.7 %), PH2/PH3 6.03 -> 6.45 s (**+6.9 %**); the gate (+-10 % per phase) is met on the larger samples, but **production PH2/PH3 at the real geometry is slower than the prototype in every set (the implementer's own medians +1.3 % and +5.5 %, mine +6.6 % and +6.9 %), about 6-7 %**, and the cause was not investigated (the implementer's G5 finds no cost in the clamped `Lce` or in the `Auto` reservation, which I did not re-measure). The counted I/O (PH1 and PH2/PH3 read, write and mapped bytes) is **equal to the byte** in all 4 cells and in all 28 runs of the repeated cell, the suffix array equals the E6 file in all 46 runs, the scratch peak is within the thread jitter (at most +0.6 % in one run). **RssAnon:** my external sampler on the test host reads at most **149.4 MB** over all runs, the in-process figure at most 146.6 MB (`genome_100m`, real geometry) and 106.7 MB for the whole chain (gate 320 MB, verdict `Within` in every row). **Own fault injection** (the production stage under the harness, `dna_100m`, scratch or output on 100-300 MB tmpfs mounts): the scratch full in PH3 -> `IOException: No space left on device`, **the original exception type, not wrapped**, no suffix array left, the scratch root left as an empty directory; the suffix array not fitting (300 MB for 400,000,004 bytes) -> `IOException ... because the disk was full` **at the creation, before PH1**, nothing left; a text cut to 70 % -> `InvalidDataException: truncated`, nothing left; a missing output directory, a scratch root below a regular file and a missing text -> `DirectoryNotFoundException`, `DirectoryNotFoundException`, `FileNotFoundException`, nothing left. **Mutation audit recounted** from `summary_1b7d.tsv`: 285 mutants = **248 killed + 27 equivalent + 10 second-line defence + 0 open**, as stated; the 276 mutants of the lists that the implementer did not run (even positions only) are the open part of the audit, so I took **27 of them at random (seed fixed) and wrote 10 own mutants of the comparator** (`SuffixOrder.CompareCore`), 37 in two separate worktrees with the implementer's runner (watchdog, stage 1 and 2): **27 killed, 10 survived**. Of the 10 survivors, 5 change no output of the build (`CompareCore`: the key skip one symbol too long, the witness of equal residues taken from residue 1, a zero extension counted as a full period, which only change how many symbols or ranks the comparator reads; `BuildLevel`: no floor of 64 for the sort spill; `PosWriter`: a completely filled bucket handed over from memory although a file exists) and 5 are **test gaps** that no test of the package closes: `Dist.Scatter` without `Files` creates the target instead of opening it (`Y02`), the **arena cap of PH3 is not enforced and no test notices** (`Q52`: `new ArenaBudget(long.MaxValue)`), `ArenaGuards` is not passed to PH1 (`Q76`), the empty scratch directory is not removed (`H08`), the `TiePositions` statistic is not counted (`F06`); 5 of 27 is 19 % of the unrun half, which would be about 50 of its 276 mutants (a rough extrapolation, not a measurement). None of the five changes the suffix array; `Q52` weakens the guard behind the RssAnon gate (the gate itself is measured by G3). **I did not re-run the 14 watchdog or crash kills one by one, nor G5 and G4.** Recorded as open points for 1b.7e: close `Q52`, `Q76` and `Y02` with tests when the builder gets its overload over a `MappedOutputFile` (the same options are touched), and pin `F06` and `H08`; the cost-only comparator mutants need a counter test (`LceSymbols` / `RankLookups` for a pair whose extension is 0) if the cost of the comparator is to be protected against a silent regression.

### 1b.7e: PH6 (the BWT and Psi), the container writer and the end-to-end builder (2026-11-19)

The last of the packages 1b.7a-e of section 4.5 that build the writer (authorised 2026-11-18, section 6c; forecast 6-9 hours and 1.6-2.4 M tokens, +-50 %, economy cap 10 hours or 2.8 M tokens, stated before the launch; gates G1-G6 and the abandon rule were fixed by the orchestrator before the work; the reader, `SuffixIndexFactory.Open`, the validation levels and the `e8-verify` adapter are 1b.7f and were not built). Production code for **PH6 (the BWT pass and Psi by sigma-way distribution, E9), the container writer (PH7: the section table, the checksums, the header, the atomic publication of ADR-003 as amended on 2026-11-18) and the end-to-end builder `SesaBuilder.Build(inputPath, targetPath, options)`**: PH0 -> PH1-PH3 -> PH4-PH5 -> PH6 -> seal in one process, **no prototype code**, every big output built directly into its section of `<target>.tmp` (created through `MappedOutputFile.Create`, `Auto`), one `IoCounters` for the whole build, per-phase budgets, per-phase RssAnon and verdict, the `Reservation` pre-flights, and a result object. Code `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External`: **8 new files, 1,648 lines, all `internal`**: `Sesa/Crc32C.cs` (CRC-32C: the hardware path `Sse42.X64.Crc32` / arm64 `Crc32.Arm64.ComputeCrc32C`, a slice-by-8 table path, one entry point), `Sesa/SesaFormat.cs` (the constants, `SesaSection`, `SesaHeader`, `AlignedOffset`, `EncodeHeaderPage`), `Sesa/SesaLayout.cs` (`SesaPlan`: the sections known after PH0; `SesaTail`: the LCP and its overflow list), `Sesa/SesaChecksums.cs` (the sequential checksum pass over a read mapping), `Sesa/SectionLcpChildBuilder.cs` (PH4 + PH5 with the SA read from a range of the container, the CHILD range known up front and the LCP placed by a callback), `Sesa/SesaBuilder.cs` (the end-to-end builder, options, result, hook points), `Ph6/BwtPass.cs`, `Ph6/PsiStream.cs`. **Five existing files edited** (the only edits, each an overload or an option; `git diff --stat 48c2ee4b -- src`: 13 files, +1,830 / -38 with the eight new ones): `MappedOutputFile.cs` (+44 / -3: `Grow`, `Length` with a private setter, `_map` no longer `readonly`), `Sa/SaBuilder.cs` (+34 / -7: the private `BuildCore` and the overload `Build(MappedTextReader, MappedOutputFile, long, SaBuildOptions)`), `Sort/SaSink.cs` (+13: `MappedSaSectionSink`), `Lcp/ExternalLcp.cs` (+18: `LcpPlacementRequest`, `LcpPlacement`, `ExtLcpOptions.SaOffset` and `.Place`), `Lcp/LcpBuild.cs` (+73 / -28: the offset of the SA, the placer, creation and writing in one `try` / `finally`). ADR-001/002/003, `apps/`, every csproj, `wiki/` and the prototypes are untouched.

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| G1 contract (PH6 against the naive definitions, exhaustive strings of alphabets 1-4, families at 10^5-10^6, the header and table exactly as ADR-003, CRC-32C against an independent implementation, atomicity and every failure mode) | **PASS.** Written first and red: **169 of the 174 new Esa tests and 3 of 3 differential tests** against throwing stubs in `fe062757` (the 5 green ones pin constants and the section sink that already worked); final: **206 new default Esa tests** (`Crc32CTests`, `SesaFormatTests`, `SesaLayoutTests`, `Ph6Tests`, `SesaBuilderTests`, `SesaFailureTests`, `SesaSectionTests`, `SesaGapTests`, `SesaAuditTests`), `SesaExhaustiveTests` (Explicit, Exhaustive: every string over sigma 1 up to length 10, sigma 2 up to 10, sigma 3 up to 6, sigma 4 up to 5, 4,000+ strings, with rotating tiny parameters, with and without PSI; the families at 10^5 and 10^6 at three geometries; the four widths of the LCP written as their sections) and `SesaBuildFuzzTests` (Explicit, Fuzz: 1,120 cases); **BWT and Psi equal the definitions** (`BWT[j] = T[SA[j] - 1]`, `PSI[i] = ISA[SA[i] + 1]`) on every string, the primary row is found at row 1, an inner row and the last row, Psi composed n + 1 times from the primary row visits every row once, 256 symbols (4 passes) and one symbol (a^n) work; **the container is decoded by an independent reader written from ADR-003 only** (`SesaOracle` in the tests: no product type, a bitwise CRC-32C) and **by `analysis/em/sesa_sections.py` with the CRC-32C of `crc32c_ref.c`**: the header and every table entry field by field, alignments (64; 4,096 from 1 MiB), HAS_CRC and REQUIRED flags, the page after the table zero, no gap byte non-zero, the file ends with its last section, every section CRC-32C; **atomicity**: a failure at every one of the 14 hook points, at every trace point of every stage, with every arena cap too small, with a disk that fills at every creation of a file (an injected reservation source; no real full device), a free-space figure below the need at the early and at the late pre-flight (to the byte), an input that shrinks between the passes, a missing input or directory, a target that is a directory: the original exception type is kept and **no target, no `.tmp` and no `.tmp.d` is left, no descriptor and no mapping of any of them**; an existing target is untouched by a failed build; between the header and the magic the `.tmp` has no magic and there is no target; two builds of the same input are byte-identical at different times, thread counts (1-4), sizes, seeds and arena caps, with and without PSI. The suffix array that is a wrong-order permutation is **not** detected by the builder (one test documents it). |
| G2 identity at 10^8 (`dna_100m`, `genome_100m`; 4 threads, cold cache, 3x10^9 geometry scaled, PSI on, `analysis/em/run_1b7e_g2.sh`, rows `rows_1b7e_g2.tsv`) | **PASS.** The container is built from the text file, its sections are extracted by `analysis/em/sesa_sections.py` (an independent decoder, no production code) and hashed: **TEXT, SA, LCP, LCP_OVERFLOW (absent = the empty hash) and CHILD equal the E7 references** (SHA-256) and the statistics equal `e6` on both texts; **PSI equals the E9 reference** on `dna_100m` (`d6f9f05e...`) and, for `genome_100m`, for which E9 kept no PSI hash, the E9 prototype was run on the E6 files (`2a47de42...`) and the in-RAM oracle `ISA[SA[i] + 1]` agrees; every section CRC-32C equals the value of `analysis/em/crc32c_ref.c` (an independent C implementation, bitwise table): 8 and 9 sections, 0 mismatches; **`e8-verify` 11 of 11 on both** (4.4 s and 5.5 s) on the extracted sections. Whole build 24.7 s (`dna_100m`) and 25.6 s (`genome_100m`): PH0 0.5 s, PH1 1.8 / 2.1 s, PH2 / PH3 12.8 / 13.0 s, PH4 / PH5 6.6 / 6.7 s, BWT 1.4 / 1.6 s, Psi 1.0 / 1.0 s, checksums 0.33 / 0.28 s, seal (header, two fsyncs, rename) 6 ms; files of 1,325,019,393 and 1,348,644,448 bytes (`genome_100m`: 1,390,028 overflow pairs, the file grew by 11,124,063 bytes after B2); counted I/O of the whole build on `dna_100m`: 3,910,595,933 B read, 2,810,214,300 B written, 1,378,758,919 B mapped, scratch peak 1.2x10^9 B; RssAnon of the whole build 109.9 / 112.6 MB in process, 107.3 / 109.9 MB by the external sampler. |
| G3 cost (4 threads, cold cache before every run, variants alternating inside a run number, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit=0x6000000`, `DOTNET_gcConcurrent=0`, medians; `analysis/em/run_1b7e_g3.sh`, `summarize_1b7e_g3.py`, rows `rows_1b7e_g3.tsv` (3 runs per cell), `rows_1b7e_g3x.tsv` (7 more for the PH6 cell and the real geometry), pooled) | **PASS, with the BWT phase not resolvable at +-10 % and one overhead near the flag.** **(1) PH6 production against the E9 prototype** (10 runs per variant and text, prototype with its 1 MiB windows): `dna_100m` BWT 1.691 against 1.719 s (**-1.7 %**), Psi 0.991 against 1.011 s (**-2.0 %**); `genome_100m` BWT 1.846 against 2.121 s (**-12.9 %**, faster), Psi 1.001 against 1.010 s (**-0.9 %**); the counted I/O is **equal to the byte** (BWT: 400,000,004 read, 100,000,001 mapped; Psi: 100,000,001 read, 400,000,004 mapped) and the BWT and PSI files of all 120 runs (prototype and production) have one SHA-256 per text. The BWT scan reads the text at random from a cold cache, so its time spreads 1.4-2.7 s between runs of the same variant (production 1.42-2.35 / 1.39-2.67, prototype 1.31-2.41 / 1.39-2.71 s): a +-10 % gate cannot be resolved by that phase with 10 runs; production is not slower than the prototype on either text. The prototype with its own 64 KB windows needs 2.33 / 2.39 s for Psi (2.3 times): the 1 MiB window is the rule of E9 and the default here (a test pins it). **(2) The whole container build against the sum of the separate phases plus the checksum pass** (PH0, PH1, PH2 / PH3, PH4 / PH5, BWT, Psi, checksums, each as its own run of the same code, no header, no rename): scaled geometry (3 runs) `dna_100m` 24.67 against 24.66 s (**+0.0 %**), `genome_100m` 27.40 against 27.28 s (**+0.4 %**); real geometry (10 runs) `dna_100m` 17.77 against 17.22 s (**+3.2 %**), `genome_100m` 19.27 against 18.42 s (**+4.6 %**): **not above the flag of 5 %, but close on `genome_100m`**; the container's wall clock includes 0.4-0.7 s that no phase clock counts (the construction of the difference cover, 0.28 s per build as in 1b.7d, the scratch directories, the sampler threads), the sum of the separate phases does not.
