The counted I/O differs by the header page and the magic only: **+4,104 bytes written** (4,096 + 8) and +237 mapped bytes in every cell, reads equal. **(3) Scratch peak:** 1.2x10^9 B in the container and in the sum of the phases in every cell (the peak is PH4 in base mode, 12 B/char; the 10^8 B BWT is below it). **(4) RssAnon of the whole build, at most 320 MB:** in-process sampler and the independent external sampler of the test-host process (`sample_rss_1b7a.py`, 10 ms) **agree within 0.05 MB in all 44 container runs**; scaled geometry 105.9 / 103.8 MB median (maximum 116.6 / 112.5), **real geometry 254.6 MB (maximum 256.9) on `dna_100m` and 288.4 MB (maximum 295.5) on `genome_100m`**, verdict `Within` in every row; the peak is the end of PH4 / PH5 (PH6 at most 191.4 MB, the seal the same as PH6), so PH6 and the checksums add nothing to the peak of 1b.7d (the margin on `genome_100m` is 24.5 MB). |
| G4 portability (simulated; nothing ran on macOS, Windows or arm64) | **PASS as simulated.** `OnAPlatformWithoutProcAndWithoutFallocate_TheBuildRuns_TheFiguresAreNull_AndTheVerdictIsUnavailable` (a hostile `IProcSource`, a reservation source that cannot reserve and reports -1 free bytes: the build runs, the RssAnon figures are `null`, every verdict is `Unavailable`, the pre-flights are skipped, the output is byte-identical), `OnLinux_TheVerdictIsWithin_AndEveryPhaseHasAFigure`, `TheBytes_DoNotDependOnThreadsSizesSeedsOrArenaCaps`; the table path of the CRC is tested against the bitwise definition and against the hardware path. **Not run: macOS, Windows, arm64** (the arm64 `Crc32.Arm64` path compiles and was never executed). |
| G5 reported | **Checksums:** the pass over a finished container reads it once through a read mapping in 16 MiB views: per section of 400 MB **0.076-0.084 s with the hardware instruction (4.8-5.3 GB/s, warm cache) against 0.28-0.29 s with the slice-by-8 table (1.37-1.46 GB/s)**, 3.4 times; a whole pass over the 1.3 GB of `dna_100m` is 0.28-0.31 s (about 1 % of the build; the result ends in the `crc` column of the rows). **`Auto` reservation:** the creation of the container (fallocate of 1.3 GB) takes 1.9 ms, sparse 1.3 ms; the build takes 26.72 s (`Auto`) against 26.30 s (sparse), -1.6 % with a spread of 1.6 s: **no cost that three runs resolve**; the part behind the reserved length (`genome_100m`: 11,124,063 bytes after B2) is sparse in both. **Publication:** the seal (header page, two fsyncs, magic, rename, scratch removal) takes 5-6 ms with `Fsync` on and off alike (26.57 against 26.66 s), so the two fsyncs are not measurable on this device (the dirty pages are written back long before the end of a 25 s build; a real device and a larger cache may differ); the rename is atomic on POSIX. **PSI:** on `dna_100m` 25.92 s with PSI against 23.28 s without (**+2.64 s, +11.3 %**: BWT 1.72 s, Psi 1.04 s, checksums +0.10 s, i.e. 26 ns per character), **+400,003,072 bytes in the file** (1,325,019,393 against 925,016,321: 4 B/char), +500,000,005 bytes read and +500,000,006 mapped (the BWT: the SA once and the BWT once; Psi: 4 B/char), **+1 B/char scratch** that does not raise the peak, RssAnon +0 (the peak is PH4 / PH5). **Build into sections against the avoided copy:** the container is 24.67 s against 24.66 s for the separate files (G3), and **copying the 1,325,000,077 bytes of the separate outputs into one file would have cost 1.72 s more (+6.5 %, 0.77 GB/s) and 1.3 GB of free space** that the container never needs. |
| G6 test policy (the affected set on the final head `6bd0ff94`) | **PASS, after two fixes of tests of mine.** `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed (eight new files and five edited ones in `External/`; no other project of `src`, no csproj, no line of `apps/`), so **run:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,511 passed + 8 skipped (3,305 + 8 before; 8 m 55 s, Debug)**, Exhaustive **79 of 79 (76 before; 10 m 46 s, Release)**, Fuzz **36 of 36 (34 before; 9 m, Release)**, the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) **3,512 passed + 7 skipped (3,306 + 7 before; 6 m 46 s, Release)**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672 (20 s)**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **516 of 516 (513 + 3 differential tests; 16 m 6 s)**, the build of `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` **builds (6 s)**; **skipped with the rule:** `SuffixTree.Tests` (Vectors: no SA or LCP kernel changed), Ukkonen and `SuffixTree.Mcp.Core.Tests` (unchanged projects); no full-solution run; no timing run overlapped any other work (every cell of G3, the repeats and G5 ran alone on the machine; the audit and G6 came after). **The first run of G6 (on `021af8fa`) failed in three assertions of my own new tests, none in the code:** the RssAnon verdict of a test host that had already run 3,500 tests was asserted `Within` the default 320 MB (the host held 513 MB; fixed in `e442e85b`: the verdict is checked against the figure, and an unconditional `Within` uses a cap above any process; G3 measures the figure in a process of its own), and a whole-build peak was compared with a phase peak to the last digit although the two samplers read at different instants (fixed in `6bd0ff94` with a tolerance of one change of interval); the Esa default and the checked build were run again on the final head `6bd0ff94`, Exhaustive, Fuzz, Persistent, EmProto and the Bench build ran on `021af8fa` (the two fixes change default tests only, and no file of `src` changed after `f3533a1b`). |

Reading. (1) **PH6 costs what the prototype's costs and writes what it wrote:** per phase -12.9 .. -0.9 % over 10 runs (the BWT spreads 1.4-2.7 s between runs of one variant), the counted I/O equal to the byte, the BWT and PSI files equal in all 120 runs and to the E9 references at 10^8 on two texts. (2) **Building into the sections costs nothing and saves the copy:** the whole build is 0.0-0.4 % above the sum of its phases in the scaled geometry and 3.2-4.6 % at the real one (0.4-0.7 s that no phase clock counts), the written bytes are the sum plus the 4,104 of the header, and the copy of the separate outputs that the sections avoid would cost 1.72 s and 1.3 GB at 10^8. (3) **The working set did not grow:** RssAnon of the whole build is 256.9 / 295.5 MB at the real geometry on the two texts (gate 320 MB, margin 24.5 MB on `genome_100m`), the same peak as PH4 / PH5 of 1b.7c-d; BWT, Psi and the checksums add nothing to it. (4) **The container is what ADR-003 says, as far as an independent reader that I wrote from ADR-003 can tell,** and every failure leaves nothing. (5) **PSI costs 11 % of the build and 30 % of the file**; `IncludePsi` is on by default because the gates of G3 hold with it (the rule of the brief), and one constant turns it off: whether a section that no reader reads yet is worth 4 B/char is the owner's decision (open point f).

**Choices made** (small; none is a public API, a behaviour verified in E3-E9 or a change of ADR-001/002/003; the owner may overrule any): (1) **File order and the sparse tail.** The sections are placed in this order after the header page: TEXT, ALPHABET, COUNTS, SA, CHILD, PSI (when built), BUILD_INFO (4,096 bytes reserved, its length is known only at the end), LCP, LCP_OVERFLOW (when `PARAM0 > 0`); every size except those of LCP and its overflow list is a function of n, b and sigma, so the file is created at `ReservedLength` = the end of the LCP at its narrowest width (n + 1 bytes), with the LCP offset aligned for the widest width (4 (n + 1)); after PH4-B2 a placer callback (`ExtLcpOptions.Place`) says where the LCP and the overflow list go and the file grows behind them (`MappedOutputFile.Grow`) when the width is larger or an overflow list exists. **The BCL cannot reserve blocks for an existing file** (`FileStreamOptions.PreallocationSize` throws "Preallocation size can be requested only for new files" for an existing one; checked by hand on the runtime of the sandbox, not pinned by a test), so `Grow` is sparse after an advisory free-space check (`FreeSpaceChecked`, or `Unchecked` where the platform cannot tell): a disk that fills between the check and the writes is a fault on a mapped page, as in the prototype; the part that is known up front (the whole file in the common case: U8 without overflow, `ReservedLength` is the final length) is reserved by `Auto` at the creation. (2) **Two pre-flights, both before anything is written that cannot be undone.** Early (after PH0, before the file is created): free space >= `ReservedLength` + 12 n (the peak scratch of PH4 in base mode, E4, plus the rank file); late (inside the placer, after B2): free space >= the growth + 16 (`MaxLcp` + 1) (the spill bound of the scan stack of PH5, 16 B per entry). Both use `IReservationSource.AvailableFreeBytes`, and a source that reports -1 skips them. (3) **The section table is listed in file (offset) order**, not in kind order; ADR-003 does not say which. (4) **`LRS_RANK` is the engine's convention** (`EsaStatistics.LrsRank`: the left bound of the first interval that closes at the deepest LCP; -1 when `LRS_DEPTH` is 0). ADR-003 section 3 says "first rank r with LCP[r] == LRS_DEPTH", which is that left bound plus one; the writer follows the engine and the prototype, and ADR-003 is not edited (open point for 1b.7f: pick one wording). (5) **CHILD codec id 1 = layout A, Packed**; the Tagged codec has no id in ADR-003 and the container stage refuses it. (6) **Element widths and counts that ADR-003 leaves open:** ALPHABET `ELEM_WIDTH` = 2 (UTF-16 code units, `COUNT` = sigma), COUNTS `ELEM_WIDTH` = 8, LCP_OVERFLOW `ELEM_WIDTH` = 8 and `COUNT` = number of (rank, value) pairs, BUILD_INFO `COUNT` = number of lines and `ELEM_WIDTH` = 0, LCP `COUNT` = n + 1 with `PARAM0` = number of overflow entries. (7) **BUILD_INFO holds facts about the output only** (format, writer, algorithm, n, sigma, text codec and bits, LCP kind and number of overflow entries, child layout, PSI yes / no): no time, no thread count, no sizes, no path, so that two builds of the same input are byte-identical (tested at different times, thread counts, sizes, seeds and arena caps). (8) **Failure semantics.** A failure removes `<target>.tmp` and `<target>.tmp.d` (scratch) and leaves no target; an **existing target is kept** untouched by a failed build and replaced atomically by a successful one (`File.Move(overwrite)`); a stale `.tmp` or `.tmp.d` (a directory or a file) of an earlier killed build is deleted at the start; there is no `Renamed` hook (a failure after the rename is not a failure of the build). The header page is written with the first 8 bytes zero, fsynced, the magic written last and fsynced, then the rename (`SesaBuildOptions.Fsync`, on by default). (9) **Budgets per phase, as 1b.7d and 1b.7c:** PH1-PH4 160 MiB each, PH5 64 MiB, PH6 its own `Ph6CapBytes` = 160 MiB (the windows of the Psi pass are charged to it like an arena block); the process peak is the largest stage (they run one after the other), reported as `ArenaPeakBytes`. (10) **PH6 on the container.** The BWT is a scratch file of n + 1 bytes (`<scratch>/ph6/bwt.bin`, created through `MappedOutputFile.Create`, charged to the scratch ledger of the build, deleted when Psi is done); its symbol counts are compared with those of PH0 (a suffix array that is a permutation but belongs to another text is found here, at the cost of nothing); Psi goes **straight into the PSI section** through one mapped window per symbol of a pass (64 symbols per pass, windows of 1 MiB, a test pins the default), and `PSI[0] = 0` is written explicitly. The row of suffix 0 is found by the BWT pass (the scan of the SA that builds the BWT sees every entry). (11) **A wrong-order suffix array that is a permutation is not detectable by the builder** (it checks `SA[0] = n`, the range of the entries, and that every symbol count matches; it does not compare suffixes): one test documents this. (12) **CRC-32C is computed in one sequential pass over a read mapping of the finished file** (16 MiB views, in file order), after the container is closed; the alternative of checksumming while the sections are written does not work because most sections are written out of order (PH3 buckets, PH5 windows, the Psi cursors). The pass reads the file once (G5).

**Defects found.** *Prototype:* none; E9's BWT and Psi are reproduced byte for byte (the Psi hash of `genome_100m` had not been recorded in E9 and was derived from the prototype and checked against the in-RAM oracle). *Production, by the tests of this package:* (1) the first restructure of the creation in `LcpBuild` (B3) leaked the base file when the creation of the second one failed; the existing test `AFullDisk...` of 1b.7c caught it, the creation moved into the `try` / `finally`; (2) the LCP offset was aligned for width 1 (a 16- or 32-bit LCP of 1 MiB or more would not start at a multiple of 4,096, which ADR-003 section 3 requires): found while writing the layout, the offset is aligned for the widest width and a layout test pins it. *Production, by the audit:* none: every one of the survivors was a missing assertion, an argued equivalent or a defence (below). *Tests and tooling:* `ProcMaps` did not see a file that was removed while it was mapped (the kernel appends " (deleted)"), so no failure test could see a leaked mapping of a removed container (fixed; V10 is killed by it); the golden counts of PH4's arena peak depend on how its workers interleave (taken out of the pins after one failure under load); an expectation of 215 mapped windows that did not hold (the windows of the PH0 writers alone); the audit runner: **a test host that stops at the first failure (`NUnit.StopOnError`) can hang, and the watchdog counted that as a kill, so the 59 mutants that the first two rounds reported as "killed by the watchdog" were not all kills: 56 of them were killed by an assertion when run again without it, 3 are genuine infinite loops (`P07`, `S09`, `W05`); the reruns (rounds r3-r14) run without it, with a time limit of 360 s, and a time limit counts as a kill only from such a run.** *Documents:* the `LRS_RANK` wording of ADR-003 (choice 4).

**Mutation audit** (`analysis/mutation/mutants_1b7e_data.py`, `make_1b7e_mutants.py`, `run_1b7e_mutants.py` through `run_1b7e_audit.sh` in separate git worktrees with a watchdog, analyzers off and the slim test project `SuffixTree.Esa.Slim1b7e.csproj.template`; results `results_1b7e_r*.tsv`, status of every mutant `summary_1b7e.tsv`, arguments `1b7e_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b7e.py`): **600 hand-made single-site mutants listed** (585 of the code of this package in the 13 files it added or edited, the five gaps of the audit of 1b.7d (`d_Y02`, `d_Q52`, `d_Q76`, `d_H08`, `d_F06`) and ten mutants of the comparator that change only a counter (`d_C01`-`d_C10`)); **467 run, 133 NOT run** (the odd positions of the lists of `SesaBuilder` (47), `BwtPass` (22), `SesaLayout` (12), `MappedOutputFile` (11), `LcpBuild` (10), `Crc32C` (8), `SesaFormat` (8), `SaBuilder` (7), `SesaChecksums` (5), `SaSink` (3); the first half of every list ran completely: 304 of 304, the second 157 of 296 until the cap of time), **stage 2 (the differential tests of `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests`, for the survivors of stage 1) ran for the survivors of the first 70 or so mutants of round r1 and from then on for the PH6 files only** (the other files have no differential test, and a build of that project cost two minutes); it killed no mutant that stage 1 had not killed in the end (`P49` was killed by it first). **Result: 427 killed + 3 killed by the watchdog (infinite loops of the mutants: `P07` a read of 0 bytes, `S09` a view of 0 bytes, `W05` a read below one entry) + 25 argued equivalent + 12 second-line defences + 0 open** (467 = 430 + 25 + 12; one more mutant, `P34`, did not compile in its first form and was written again). The first round (304 mutants, narrow filters) killed 221 (24 of them by the watchdog, rerun as described above) and left **83 survivors of stage 1 (82 after stage 2)**: 49 were killed later, 24 are argued equivalent and 10 are defences; the second round added 143 kills (35 by the watchdog, rerun), 1 equivalent and 2 defences; the survivors were closed by **21 new tests in `SesaAuditTests` and four changed ones** (the options refused before the first hook; the exact table entry of every section for the U8, U8 + overflow, U16, U16 + overflow and U32 widths; the units, the seconds and the peaks of the result; the verdict at the cap to the last digit; PH6's buffers charged to the shared budget at the size of the input; every range refused at the entry to the byte, before any work, and negative offsets; the text at the limit of the positions; the windows and the counters of Psi, a symbol that is short or in surplus at the end of a window, a window below one entry; the state at the hook points: no BWT scratch after Psi, no descriptor of the container when the checksums have run, PH4's files under `ph4`; a descriptor of the file after `Grow`; the cleanup of the section stage with files in its scratch; the pre-flights asking about the directory of the target and refusing a volume with nothing free; PH6's defaults at the level of the builder; and **pinned counted calls** of three tiny geometries and one text with an overflow list, which fix every size that has to reach its stage); **the five gaps of 1b.7d: `Y02`, `Q52`, `H08`, `F06` are killed by `SesaGapTests`, `Q76` (the arena guards of PH1) is a second-line defence** (the guards change no byte and no counter of a correct stage); **the cost-only mutants of the comparator are closed by the counter test** (`d_C01`-`d_C10` killed: the key skip, the witness of equal residues taken from residue 1, a zero extension counted as a period, the calls, symbols and rank lookups, over two covers and the pairs at the end of the text); the two other cost-only mutants of the 1b.7d audit (the floor of 64 of the sort spill in `BuildLevel` and a full bucket handed over from memory by `PosWriter`) were not listed here. The equivalents: a discard, a field that equals another by definition (`LRS_DEPTH = MAX_LCP`), a cleanup after the rename that finds nothing, an integer format, the check of a big-endian host, a check that cannot be reached by a constant text, options that the overload in use does not read, a negative size that the next line refuses with the same exception, a null that falls into the path mode and is refused there, a buffer that is overwritten before it is read; the defences: an `fsync` (the counters do not count it), the two native blocks of a cursor pass that are not freed, the guards of the arenas, a flush of the windows, a completion that only checks that no writer is open, the thread count of PH4, and the cleanup of a failed constructor of the output writer that no injectable fault reaches (each with its argument in `1b7e_equivalent.py`).

**Proposal: the definition of the optional section PSI (kind 12), for ADR-004 only; ADR-003 is not edited and the owner decides whether it moves there.** `KIND` = 12, `FLAGS`: `REQUIRED` clear, `HAS_CRC` set; `COUNT` = n + 1; `ELEM_WIDTH` = `POS_WIDTH` = 4 (little-endian `uint`); `CODEC` = 1 (plain array); `PARAM0` = the **primary row** (the row r with `SA[r] = 0`); `PARAM1` = 0. Content: **`PSI[i] = ISA[SA[i] + 1]` for `SA[i] < n`, and `PSI[i] = 0` for `SA[i] = n`** (`i = 0`, the empty suffix, has no successor). Equivalently `PSI` is the inverse of the LF-mapping of the BWT `BWT[j] = T[SA[j] - 1]` (row 0, the empty suffix, is the `C` offset 1; the primary row has no symbol, which is why it is stored), and composing `PSI` n + 1 times from the primary row visits every row exactly once (tested). It is built by the sigma-way distribution of E9 (one scan of the BWT with one output cursor per symbol), costs one pass over the SA and the text (the BWT) and one pass over the BWT, 1 B/char of scratch and 4 B/char of file, and lets a reader walk the text forward (the suffix 0, 1, 2, ... in rank order) without the text. A reader that does not know kind 12 ignores it (`REQUIRED` is clear); a writer without it produces a valid file (`IncludePsi` = false). The definition is the one of the E9 reference (`PSI[0] = 0`, `start[0] = 1`), reproduced byte for byte at 10^8 on two texts and equal to the in-RAM oracle `ISA[SA[i] + 1]` on every string of the exhaustive set.

**Not verified / limits (1b.7e).** (a) Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64: nothing ran there (G4 is the simulation of a platform without `/proc` and without `fallocate`, a hostile `IProcSource` and a source that reports -1 free bytes; the arm64 `Crc32.Arm64` path was never executed, only compiled; the table path is tested against the bitwise definition and the hardware path on x64). (b) A text of more than 10^8 symbols: the 3x10^9 figures are ESTIMATES (E8 measured the prototype there); the real-geometry cells run to 10^8 only. (c) A full disk at a real size (injected reservation sources and a 300 MB tmpfs were used, not a 1.3 GB device that fills during PH3), and **the growth of the tail is sparse**: a disk that fills between the late pre-flight and the writes of the LCP is a SIGBUS on a mapped page, not an `IOException` (one short window, only when the LCP is wider than 1 byte or has an overflow list). (d) `fsync` on a real device: the cost was measured on the sandbox file system only, and `File.Move` over an existing target is atomic on POSIX; on Windows the semantics of an overwrite are the .NET ones. (e) A power loss between the two fsyncs: the order is right by construction (header with a zero magic, fsync, magic, fsync, rename) and tested by holding the build at each point, but a kill of the machine was not simulated. (f) The reader: nothing in this package reads a container except the checksum pass, the tests and `analysis/em/sesa_sections.py` (an independent decoder that extracts the sections for the `e8-verify` run of G2); `SuffixIndexFactory.Open`, the validation levels, checked and trusted access, and an `e8-verify` adapter that reads the container directly do not exist. (g) `Resume` / `state.json`, 40-bit positions, both strands, NEXTSYM, BUCKET, ISA and RMQ sections, layout F and the Tagged child codec in a container: not built. (h) A suffix array that is a wrong-order permutation: not detectable by the builder (a test documents it); the `Verify` level of the reader (1b.7f) is the check. (i) The independent decoders (`SesaOracle` in the tests, `sesa_sections.py`, `crc32c_ref.c`) were written by the same implementer as the writer, from ADR-003, not by a second person.

**Open points handed to 1b.7f (and to the owner).** (a) **The reader**: `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` dispatch between v6 and SESA files, the validation levels (`Header`: magic, header CRC, table sanity; `Structure`: section CRCs and the cross-field rules; the checked and trusted modes of ADR-001), a bounded child-table descent (the `Checked` mode must bound every walk, 1b.3), reading the sections through `MappedFile` views with the alignment this writer gives them (64 bytes, 4,096 from 1 MiB). (b) **`e8-verify` adapter** over the container (the 11 checks run today on sections extracted by `sesa_sections.py`). (c) **ADR-003 wording to settle** (this package did not edit it): `LRS_RANK` (engine convention versus "first rank r with LCP[r] == LRS_DEPTH", choice 4), the order of the section table (choice 3), `ELEM_WIDTH` / `COUNT` of ALPHABET, COUNTS, LCP_OVERFLOW and BUILD_INFO (choice 6), CHILD codec 1 (choice 5), and the PSI definition above. (d) `Resume` / `state.json` (the journal of E8 / ADR-004 section 4.2): the builder is one shot; a killed build leaves a stale `.tmp` and `.tmp.d`, which the next build deletes. (e) The sparse growth of the tail (limit c): make the file the final length before PH4 starts when the first B2 pass is cheap enough to run before the creation, or keep the sparse growth and document the SIGBUS window; the owner's call. (f) `IncludePsi` default and what a reader needs PSI for (suffix links through PSI, forward text walk): decided by the first user of the section. (g) The `HAS_CRC` pass costs one read of the file (G5): a reader's `Structure` level does the same pass again, so a cached "verified" flag in the file is possible but not defined.

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-19, 1b.7e, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `254a2f95`, tree clean; the 22 commits since `48c2ee4b` are 8 new files in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (`Sesa/Crc32C`, `SesaFormat`, `SesaLayout`, `SesaChecksums`, `SectionLcpChildBuilder`, `SesaBuilder`, `Ph6/BwtPass`, `Ph6/PsiStream`; 1,648 lines) and edits of five existing files in the same folder (`MappedOutputFile` +47, `Sa/SaBuilder` +41, `Sort/SaSink` +13, `Lcp/ExternalLcp` +18, `Lcp/LcpBuild` +101/-28 in total, the 1b.7c tests that touch `LcpBuild` still pass), tests, `analysis/**`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; ADR-001/002/003, `apps/`, `wiki/`, every csproj (the only `csproj` in the diff is the audit template `SuffixTree.Esa.Slim1b7e.csproj.template`) and the prototypes are untouched; all 22 commits have both trailers, one title has `1b.7e:` with a colon instead of `1b.7e ` (pushed, not rewritten); a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names and for a model identifier found nothing. Hygiene: 47 per-run sidecar files `analysis/em/rows_1b7e_*.tsv.crc.*` (188 KB) are committed; they are the CRC rows of single runs and could be folded into their rows file. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me on `254a2f95`):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,511 passed + 8 skipped** (6 m 24 s, Release; 3,305 + 8 before, +206), Exhaustive **79 of 79** (16 m 20 s), Fuzz **36 of 36** (13 m 6 s), the **checked build** **3,512 + 7 skipped**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **516 of 516** (16 m 39 s; 513 + 3 differential tests); skipped with the rule: Vectors, Ukkonen, Mcp.Core (no changed inputs); nothing ran in parallel with a timing run. **Own G2 at 10^8** (the implementer's cost-test class as the driver, variant `container`, PSI on, scaled geometry, cold cache, my own scripts): a reader of the container that I wrote from the table of ADR-003 sections 3-4 (not from the product) and my own CRC-32C program (check value of `123456789` = `e3069283`) find on `dna_100m` and `genome_100m`: magic, version 1.0, endian probe, `FILE_SIZE` equal to the real length (1,325,019,393 and 1,348,644,448 bytes), 8 and 9 sections, **every section CRC-32C correct**, every section of 1 MiB or more at a multiple of 4,096, no overlap, `COUNT * ELEM_WIDTH == LENGTH`, `TEXT_SHA256` equal to the SHA-256 of the input text, `NODE_COUNT`, `LRS_DEPTH` and `LRS_RANK` equal to the E6 statistics (162,149,501 / 25 / 14,863,022 and 166,050,648 / 499,999 / 75,208,039); the payload SHA-256 of TEXT, SA, LCP, LCP_OVERFLOW and CHILD **equal the E7 references**; **PSI equals my own oracle** (a C program that derives `PSI[i] = ISA[SA[i] + 1]`, 0 where `SA[i] = n`, from the SA file alone) on both texts and equals E9's reference file on `dna_100m`; **`e8-verify` 11 of 11 on both** with the sections extracted to files (4.5 s and 5.6 s; my first run lacked the `.stats` file and failed 1 of 11, my omission). **Header CRC wording:** the stored value is the CRC-32C of header bytes `[0,124)` followed by the section table, with the four CRC bytes **left out**; the other reading of ADR-003 ('the CRC field treated as zero', four zero bytes in the stream) gives a different value, so the ADR text must say which (open point for 1b.7f). **Own G3** (real geometry, cold cache before every run, 5 runs per variant, the variants alternate; the whole container build against the separate phases plus the checksum pass, the implementer's cost-test class, my own external RssAnon sampler on the test host): `dna_100m` 18.53 s against 17.07 s (**+8.5 %**), `genome_100m` 19.09 s against 19.25 s (**-0.9 %**); the implementer's 10 runs gave +3.2 % and +4.6 %, so the overhead is not resolvable below about 5 % at this noise (the per-phase medians move by up to 12 % between the two variants in both directions); the counted I/O is equal in all runs, the file is 1,325,019,393 and 1,348,644,448 bytes; **PH6** (5 runs per variant, both texts, E9's prototype against production): BWT **+9.1 % and +9.4 %**, Psi **-2.5 % and -6.9 %**, counted I/O equal, BWT and PSI SHA-256 identical to the prototype's in all 30 runs; the BWT phase spreads 1.56-2.82 s between runs of one variant, so the +-10 % gate is not resolvable on that phase (the implementer's 10 runs gave -1.7 % and -12.9 %); **RssAnon of the whole build:** the harness sampler at most 255.9 MB (`dna_100m`) and 291.5 MB (`genome_100m`), my external sampler at most 244.0 and 278.0 MB (the two differ by a constant 6-13 MB; I did not find the reason), against 320 MB: **a margin of about 28 MB at 10^8; the plateau of E8 for the prototype at 3x10^9 was 309.7 MB, so the margin at the target size is thin (ESTIMATE)**. **Own fault injection** (the container build under the harness, `dna_100m`, tmpfs volumes of 300 MB, 1.7 GB, 2.8 GB and 4 GB): a 300 MB volume is refused at once by the pre-flight (`IOException: not enough free space ... about 2,525,019,393 bytes (the container 1,325,019,393 and 12 bytes per character of scratch)`), an **existing target with other content is kept**; 1.7 GB is refused the same way, nothing left; 2.8 GB passes and leaves only the target; **`kill -9` after 14 s** leaves `x.sesa.tmp` (the reserved length, first 8 bytes zero) and `x.sesa.tmp.d/` as documented, and **the same call over the leftovers succeeds, leaves only the target, whose section hashes equal the references**. **Mutation audit recounted** from `summary_1b7e.tsv`: 600 listed = **427 killed + 3 killed by the watchdog + 25 equivalent + 12 second-line defence + 0 open + 133 not run**, as stated; of the 133 unrun mutants I took **31 at random (seed fixed) and wrote 8 own mutants of `SesaLayout`**, 39 in two separate worktrees with the implementer's runner: **32 killed (2 of them by hang), 7 survived**. Of the 7: three change nothing a test can see (`W14` the SA buffer is zeroed, `C16` the arm64 tail of the CRC which cannot run on this host, `O8` the TEXT offset aligned as a small section, which is 4,096 anyway); **four are test gaps or second-line defences: `O1` (the LCP_OVERFLOW list of 1 MiB or more aligned as a small section: no unit test has a list of 131,072 entries, only the 10^8 files do, so the alignment rule of ADR-003 section 3 for that section is checked only by the G2 run), `V10` (a failed overflow writer leaves the base writer open), `B102` (guards are not passed to the BWT pass, like `Q76` of 1b.7d), `B118` (BUILD_INFO may overrun its 4 KiB room, unreachable while its content is facts only)**; 4 of 31 is 13 % of the unrun part, which would be about 17 of 133 (a rough extrapolation). **I did not re-run G4 and G5, did not re-read the 25 equivalence arguments, and did not re-run the 3 hang kills.** Recorded for 1b.7f: a unit test with a LCP_OVERFLOW list of at least 131,072 entries (an `a^n` text of 300,000 symbols has about that many), the other three as tests when the options are touched, the header CRC wording, and the `IncludePsi` default (on; +11 % build time and +4 B/char: 13.25 B/char with PSI, 9.25 without; the S1 size gate of 16 B/char holds either way; the measurement of 1b.9 reports both).

### 1b.7f: the reader over the container, the validation levels, the dispatcher and the `e8-verify` adapter (2026-11-19)

The last package of 1b.7 (authorised by the owner's decision of 2026-11-19 "continue without stopping", PROGRESS-S1; forecast 5-8 hours and 1.4-2.2 M tokens, economy cap 10 hours or 2.8 M tokens, stated before the launch; gates G1-G6 and the abandon rule were fixed by the orchestrator before the work: Checked more than 2x slower than in memory, or any existing Esa test result changing, means stop; neither fired). Production code: **the reader** and **the engine over the file**, **`SuffixIndexFactory.Open(path, options)`** (a SESA container or a v6 file by its magic) and the **`e8-verify --container` adapter**. `SesaFile` maps the whole file once, read-only (one view, the pointer acquired once and released in `Dispose`); `SesaHeaderReader` is the `Header` level; `SesaVerifier` is the `Sections` and `Structure` levels; the typed views are `SaView` (the SA), `EsaText` (TEXT codec 4 and ALPHABET), `CompactLcp.FromMapped` (LCP and LCP_OVERFLOW), `MappedChild` (CHILD codec 1, Packed) and the PSI pointer, each a pointer plus a `long` length over the one mapping; `EsaSuffixTree` / `EsaIndex` / `EsaNavigator` / `EsaSuffixIndex` run over them (no second engine). **8 new files, 1,518 lines** (`EsaOpenOptions.cs`, `SuffixIndexFactory.cs`, `SaView.cs`, `EsaText.cs`, `External/Sesa/SesaFile.cs`, `SesaHeaderReader.cs`, `SesaVerifier.cs` in `SuffixTree.Esa`; `Verify/VerifySources.cs` in `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto`) and **21 existing files edited, +655 / -118** (17 in `SuffixTree.Esa`: +601 / -85; 4 in `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto`: +54 / -33), **one `ProjectReference`** (`SuffixTree.Esa` -> `SuffixTree.Persistent`), no prototype code; **ADR-003 is amended (2026-11-19)**, ADR-001 / ADR-002, `wiki/`, every other csproj, the prototypes and the shipped behaviour of every existing member are untouched (the 3,511 + 8 skipped Esa tests that existed at the start of the package give the same results; the 516 EmProto tests are unchanged and green). Not built (the brief's list): `Resume` / `state.json`, 40-bit positions, both strands, NEXTSYM / BUCKET / ISA / RMQ sections, the Tagged child codec in a container, a change of the builder.

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| G1 contract (round trip equals the in-memory tree on every member and through the conformance suite over a file-backed factory; every byte of the header page flipped is refused at `Header`; 10^3 random body flips are caught at `Sections`; sealed damage is caught at `Structure`, adjacent SA swaps included; `Checked` never reads outside the mapping; two builds give byte-identical files; the v6 loader rejects a container, the dispatcher opens both kinds) | **PASS.** Written first and red (`25554c35`: **112 of 114 new Esa tests red** against throwing stubs, the 2 green ones pin the v6 loader's exception and the SESA magic). **Round trip:** every member of `ISuffixTree`, `ISuffixTreeNavigation` and `ISuffixArrayAccess` equals the definitional oracles and the in-memory tree on the named texts, the adversarial families (N = 150 and the small sizes), random strings and a repeat that needs the overflow list, at every level, `Checked` and `Trusted`; the existing `IndexConformanceTests` (154 cases, unchanged) also run over two file-backed factories (`Checked`, `Trusted`; a factory may now refuse a text whose code units exceed 255, which a container cannot hold: **2 more skips**, 8 -> 10). **`Header`:** all **8,192** flips of the 4,096 bytes of the header page (two masks, 0x01 and 0xFF, over the header, the table and the zero tail) are refused (`InvalidDataException`; the flips of the magic and of the CRC bytes with the right message); truncations at every boundary of the page and the sections and at a stride of 97 bytes, and trailing bytes, are refused; the version rules (a larger major is `NotSupportedException` before the CRC is looked at, a smaller one is corrupt, a larger minor opens), an unknown optional section ignored and an unknown REQUIRED one refused, an unknown codec and `POS_WIDTH` 8 `NotSupportedException`, every rule of the table (overlap, alignment, counts, widths, sums) has a refusal case. **`Sections`:** **1,000 of 1,000 random body flips** (a container of 1,800 symbols with every section kind, the LCP with an overflow list) caught; the flips are invisible at `Header` (the contract of the level, tested). **`Structure`, sealed (every section CRC recomputed):** an adjacent swap of the SA at **every** rank of five named texts (`banana`, `mississippi`, a^12, (ab)^6, a DNA string of 18 symbols), and more than 5,000 swaps over every string of sigma 1-4 up to length 8 / 8 / 5 / 4 (each file opens at `Sections` and is refused at `Structure`); an SA that is not a permutation or has no sentinel; every LCP value and every entry of the overflow list; every cell of the child table; one PSI entry and the primary row; a TEXT symbol changed or one above sigma; two COUNTS entries moved by one (the sum is unchanged); `NODE_COUNT`, `LRS_DEPTH` and `LRS_RANK`; `TEXT_SHA256` only when asked; a gap byte only from `Sections` on. **Safety:** **300 sealed hostile bodies** (240 of SA / CHILD / LCP / PSI, 60 of the overflow list, a share of the words replaced by random or extreme values) opened at `Header` / `Checked` in memory with an `mprotect` guard page directly after the image and, in alternate rounds, before it, and **every operation of the contract run on each**: only `InvalidDataException` (and the plain rejection of a locus that a damaged table produced), no read outside the mapping, no hang (a 30 s watchdog); a child table that loops, an LCP that lies about the child LCP, an engine over a file that was disposed under it, and a clean image at both edges of the guarded region. **Exhaustive** (Explicit): every string over sigma 1-4 up to the tiers 10 / 10 / 6 / 5 (more than 4,000) through the file equals the in-memory tree at `Structure`; the families at 10^5 and 10^6 through the builder and the reader at three geometries and every level; **fuzz** 3,000 cases up to 4,000 symbols and 150 up to 60,000. **Unit test of 1b.7e's gap O1:** an LCP_OVERFLOW list of 139,745 pairs (U8) and of about 134,000 pairs (U16), at least 1 MiB, starts at a multiple of 4,096, the file ends with it, every section of 1 MiB or more is at 4,096, the whole file conforms to ADR-003 by the independent decoder and is read back at `Structure` with SHA-256. **Two builds:** `TwoBuildsOfTheSameInput_AtDifferentTimes_AreByteIdentical` (1b.7e) is green, and the two builds of `genome_100m` of G2 have the same size and the same SHA-256 of every section. **Dispatcher:** a container opens as the Esa tree and a v6 file as the persistent tree by the magic alone, the options are ignored for v6, a file of neither kind or a `.tmp` with a zero magic is `InvalidDataException("unknown suffix index kind")`, `/dev/null` and `/dev/zero` are refused without a hang, `PersistentSuffixTreeFactory.Load(container)` keeps its existing exception. |
| G2 identity at 10^8 (`dna_100m`, `genome_100m`; `analysis/em/run_1b7f_g2.sh`, rows `rows_1b7f_g2.tsv`) | **PASS.** The container is built by `SesaBuilder.Build` (1b.7e, 4 threads, the E6 geometry scaled to 3x10^9, PSI on) and opened cold at all three levels: **Header 7.5 / 8.5 ms, Sections 0.65 / 0.53 s (the files are 1.33 / 1.35 GB), Structure 24.7 / 27.8 s (26.9 / 24.3 s with `TEXT_SHA256`), RssAnon peak 101.9 / 109.2 MB**, `dna_100m` / `genome_100m`; **identity against an oracle that is not the engine** (a header parser of its own, the input file mapped as bytes, a binary search over the SA and the text): 1,000 patterns sampled from the text (1-40 symbols) and 1,000 random ones that the oracle itself finds **absent**, counts and locate sets compared (a pattern with more than 100,000 occurrences by its count and the first 1,000 results in SA order: 892 and 878 patterns located completely, 108 and 122 by that prefix), **count mismatches 0 and 0, locate mismatches 0 and 0** (the file is opened at `Header`/`Checked`); **`e8-verify --container` 11 of 11** on both (the adapter reads the sections in place: 5.1 s and 6.2 s with 4 threads, `--input` given), and the SHA-256 of the five sections it hashes (`.pk .sa .lcp .ovf .cld`) are **equal to the hashes of the sections that 1b.7e extracted with `sesa_sections.py`** on both texts, and the same in all three builds of each text that were made for this package (the sizes are equal too: 1,325,019,393 and 1,348,644,448 bytes); **a sealed SA swap at the middle rank was refused after 5.5 s (dna) and 4.4 s (genome), and a changed child cell after 22.7 s and 23.4 s**, both at `Structure`, with the CRC recomputed. (The first G2 run of this package sampled the "absent" patterns as random strings that the oracle had not checked, of which 670 and 675 of 1,000 were absent; the run above is the one with the oracle's own choice.) |
| G3 cost (cold = the page cache dropped by `sync` and `echo 3 > /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches` before every process, one process per run; the container of `dna_10m` is 132.5 MB; `analysis/em/run_1b7f_g3.sh`, `summarize_1b7f_g3.py`, rows `rows_1b7f_g3.tsv`) | **PASS for the open, `Sections` and `Structure`; the throughput is FLAGGED (Checked is more than 25 % slower than the tree in memory).** **Cold `Header` open at 10 Mbp: 7.0-9.1 ms over 10 runs, median 7.6 ms, all 10 at most 10 ms** (5.0 ms of it is the header level's own time in a fresh process, which is the first call of the code; the same open again in the same process takes 0.05-0.09 ms). **`Sections` 63-66 ms (2.0 GB/s, hardware CRC); `Structure` 1.44-1.52 s**, a bitmap of 1.25 MB and buffers of 33.6 MB, **RssAnon 80 MB at the peak against 33.6 MB before the open (+45 MB; the gate is 320 MB)**; at 10^8 `Sections` is 0.53-0.65 s, `Structure` 24.7-27.8 s and the RssAnon peak 102-109 MB. **Throughput of the file against the tree in memory** (`dna_10m`, warm, median of 5 repeats per process, **one tree per process, 7 processes per tree in the six orders and the first again**; count of 20-mers, locate of 8-mers, count of absent 24-mers, child walk to depth 9): memory 720k / 759k / 775k queries per second and 11.9 M nodes per second; **`Checked` -29.0 / -31.2 / -39.2 / -36.6 %, `Trusted` -29.2 / -24.0 / -21.4 / -32.0 %**; `Checked` against `Trusted` +0.3 / -9.4 / -22.7 / -6.8 %; the seven processes of one tree differ by up to 25 % (memory: 571k-815k on the first workload). Not the abandon rule (Checked more than 2x slower), but above the 25 % of the flag, **and the cause is not the checks** (see the reading). The first measurement of this package put the three trees in one process in a fixed order and showed the file within +-10 % of the tree in memory (`Checked` 575k against 545k on the first workload) with `Trusted` last and slowest (381k); the order inside a process was a bias, the protocol was changed to one tree per process, and the numbers above are the ones that this package reports (the first rows are in the history of `rows_1b7f_g3.tsv`). |
| G4 portability (simulated; nothing ran on macOS, Windows or arm64) | **PASS as simulated.** The table path of the CRC (`hardwareCrc: false`) gives the same verdict as the hardware path on a damaged and on a clean file; the reader reads no `/proc`, no `fallocate` and no clock (the RssAnon figures of G2 / G3 come from the harness, not from the product); a device (`/dev/null`, `/dev/zero`) and a file that cannot be read at an offset are refused by the dispatcher without mapping; a container held in memory shorter than its header page (`SesaFile.FromMemory`) is `InvalidDataException`; a big-endian host would be `NotSupportedException` (the endian probe, not run). **Nothing ran on macOS, Windows or arm64:** the arm64 CRC path was never executed, the Windows mapping semantics (a mapped file cannot be replaced while open, `FileShare`) and a 32-bit process were not tried. |
| G5 reported (`Checked` against `Trusted`, the cost of each level, the working set of `Structure`) | **`Checked` against `Trusted`:** no difference that seven medians resolve above the noise between processes (0.3 / -9.4 / -22.7 / -6.8 %, a spread of 25 % for one tree): the bounds of the reads and the monotone rules of the walks are not what makes the file slower than the tree in memory. **Levels** at 10 Mbp / 10^8: `Header` 7.6 ms / 7.5-8.5 ms; `Sections` 64 ms / 0.53-0.65 s (2.0 GB/s); `Structure` 1.5 s / 24.7-27.8 s, **as much as the build** (27 s at 10^8; `TEXT_SHA256` adds the hash of the 25 MB text, +0.3 s, inside the +-3 s of the pass). **The working set of `Structure`** is the bitmap (n / 8 bytes: 12.5 MB at 10^8), two `int` arrays of the Phi chunk (8 bytes per position of the chunk) and the frame array of the child scan (touched as deep as the tree: 500,000 frames, 8 MB, at 10^8): **RssAnon 102 and 109 MB at the peak at 10^8, 33.6 MB of it the process before the open**; **the dial of the chunk** (`dna_100m`, `analysis/em/run_1b7f_g5.sh`, rows `rows_1b7f_g5.tsv`): 1 Mi positions 35.4 s and a peak of 77 MB (+42 MB), **4 Mi (the default) 25.7 s and 102 MB (+67 MB)**, 8 Mi 23.6 s and 134 MB (+98 MB), 16 Mi 22.2 s and 200 MB (+162 MB): the 100, 24, 12 and 6 scans of the suffix array (400 MB) that these chunk sizes need differ by 13 s between the extremes for 94 scans, which is 0.14 s per scan, and the 22 s that remain with 6 scans do not depend on the chunk; the default keeps the pass under the 320 MB gate with the room of the process (and the managed heap that the build harness limits to 96 MB would not hold it: reader runs do not set that limit). |
| G6 test policy (the affected set on the final head `0b82a1a2`; only documents change after it) | **PASS.** `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed (7 new files and 17 edited), `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` changed (1 new file and 4 edited) and `SuffixTree.Esa` references `SuffixTree.Persistent` now (its source is untouched), so **run:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,729 passed + 10 skipped (3,511 + 8 before; 9 m 3 s, Debug)**, Exhaustive **82 of 82 (79 before; 18 m 28 s, Release)**, Fuzz **38 of 38 (36 before; 13 m 33 s, Release)**, checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) **3,730 passed + 9 skipped (3,512 + 7 before; 6 m 47 s, Release)**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672 (21 s)**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **533 of 533 (516 + 17 new; 17 m 5 s)**, the Bench build builds (28 s); **skipped:** `SuffixTree.Tests` (Vectors: no SA or LCP kernel changed), Ukkonen and `SuffixTree.Mcp.Core.Tests` (their inputs did not change); no full-solution run. **No result of a test that existed before changed**; the 2 new skips are the conformance cases whose text needs code units above 255, which a container cannot hold; 218 new default Esa tests, 17 new EmProto tests, 3 + 2 Explicit classes (Exhaustive, Fuzz) and the Explicit cost driver. |

Reading. (1) **Opening costs the header, not the file:** a cold `Header` open of 10 Mbp (a 132.5 MB container, cache dropped, one process per run) takes 7.0-9.1 ms (median 7.6 ms, all 10 runs at most 10 ms); most of it is the first call of the reader code in a fresh process (the second open in the same process takes 0.05-0.09 ms), the work itself is O(sections) and independent of n (7.5 and 8.5 ms at 10^8). `Sections` is one sequential pass over the file at the speed of the CRC instruction (64 ms at 10^7 and 0.53-0.65 s at 10^8, about 2 GB/s); `Structure` is the cost that a caller must plan for: **about as much as the build** (25-28 s at 10^8 against 27 s for `SesaBuilder.Build`, 0.25 us per symbol; 1.4-1.5 s at 10^7); the Phi pass scans the suffix array once per chunk of 4 Mi text positions (24 scans at 10^8), and the chunk is the memory/time dial (G5), but the scans are a small part of the time (0.14 s each). (2) **The file is slower than the tree in memory, and the checks are not the reason that the measurement can show** (G3, one tree per process, 7 processes per tree in alternating orders: `Checked` 29-39 % slower than the tree in memory on four workloads, `Trusted` 21-32 % slower, `Checked` against `Trusted` between 0.3 and -23 % with a spread of 25 % between processes of one tree): the checks of `Checked` cost at most what the noise hides, the gap to the tree in memory is in the storage (the packed text, the mapped pages, the views), it is **above the 25 % that the gate asks to flag and well below the 2x of the abandon rule**; `Trusted` is kept as ADR-001 and ADR-003 describe it and removes the checks of the hot loops only. (3) **The structure pass is the only code that sees a wrong-order SA that is a permutation**; the order check compares each adjacent pair from the stored LCP on and requires the common prefix to be exactly the stored value, so an array damaged in its order cannot satisfy it, and nothing weaker than a full recomputation was accepted for the LCP, the child table or Psi. (4) **A file that is consistent in every check is accepted** (the threat model of `Structure` is accidental damage and the damage the tests inject, not an adversary who recomputes every array; a file from an untrusted source is memory safe in `Checked` mode either way, tested in guarded memory).

**Choices made** (small; none changes an existing behaviour or public signature, a verified behaviour of E3-E9 or ADR-001/002; ADR-003 changes only as the amendment of 2026-11-19 says; the owner may overrule any): (1) **Dispatcher direction.** `SuffixTree.Esa` references `SuffixTree.Persistent` (the one `ProjectReference` edit), not the reverse: the v6 format is the older one and has no knowledge of the engine, `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` calls `PersistentSuffixTreeFactory.LoadPersistent` after reading the first eight bytes through a file handle (nothing is mapped for the decision, a device or a pipe that cannot be read at an offset is refused, not waited for), and `PersistentSuffixTreeFactory.Load(container)` keeps its existing exception; `EsaSuffixTreeFactory.Open` opens containers only and refuses a v6 file as "not a container". A zero magic (the `.tmp` of an unfinished build) is "unknown suffix index kind". (2) **One mapping and views of the one pointer.** `MemoryMappedFile.CreateFromFile` with one view of the whole file, the pointer acquired once and released in `Dispose` (idempotent; every member of the tree throws `ObjectDisposedException` afterwards); no section is copied and none is allocated per section. A failed open releases whatever it took in `SesaFile.Create`, and `EsaSuffixTree` owns the file. (3) **No second engine: a second storage form behind the same members.** `SaView` (an `int[]` or a pointer and a count), `EsaText` (a string or the packed section and the alphabet), `ChildTable.FromMapped` over `MappedChild`, `CompactLcp.FromMapped` (the overflow pairs read in place as words and found by binary search, the arrays of the pyramid made on first need), `EsaNavigationPack.BuildMapped` (the ISA scatter with a -1 fill, the permutation and range validated) and a mapped construction path of `LcpPyramid`; `EsaIndex`, its search, traversal and analysis partials, `EsaNavigator` and `EsaSuffixIndex` keep one implementation. The internal members that exposed arrays (`Text`, `Prepared`, `SuffixArray`) throw `NotSupportedException` for an index over a file; the consumers use `Sa` and `TextView`. (4) **`Checked` and `Trusted`.** `Checked` bounds every read of the SA, the child table, the LCP, its overflow list and every symbol position against its section, and bounds every walk by a monotone rule: the l-indices of an interval increase and stay in (a, rb], a child's depth is strictly greater than its parent's, a leaf's depth is strictly greater than its parent's, the parent of the target of a suffix link or a leaf link is strictly shallower than the target (otherwise the walks of the generic algorithms would not advance); a rule that fails is `InvalidDataException` (an index in memory keeps its `InvalidOperationException`). `Trusted` leaves out the checks of the hot loops (the pattern descent, locate, the child walk) and documents that a corrupt file may crash or give garbage. (5) **The levels.** `Header` (the default) is O(sections): magic, version (a larger major is `NotSupportedException` before the CRC is read, so a larger major with a broken CRC is still "not supported"), the endian probe, the table offset, entry size and count, the header CRC (ADR-003 as amended: the four CRC bytes are left out of the stream), the reserved word, the zero tail of the page, every header field, every table entry (flags, widths, counts, alignment, inside the file), the layout (no overlap, the file ends at the last section, the gaps are zero from `Sections` on), the required sections and their agreement with the header, the alphabet (ascending, below 256), COUNTS (every count at least 1, the sum n), the statistics (`NODE_COUNT` in n + 2 .. 2n + 1, `LRS_DEPTH`, `LRS_RANK`), BUILD_INFO (UTF-8, at most 4 KiB, the line count); `Sections` adds the CRC-32C of every section that has one (the hardware instruction where the CPU has it, the slice-by-8 table elsewhere, the same value; a section of more than 1 GiB is chained in pieces on the table path) and the zero gaps; `Structure` adds the pass below. (6) **The structure pass in bounded memory.** The permutation is a bitmap of n + 1 bits; the adjacent order and the LCP values come from a Phi pass (Kasai's scan in text order) that serves a chunk of 4 Mi text positions per scan of the SA (two `int` arrays of the chunk, 32 MiB; the carry of a long common prefix is verified over 64 symbols at its start); Psi is checked from the ranks of the same pass; the child table is checked by an lcp-interval scan with a stack of 4 Mi frames that spills to a temporary file (`FileOptions.DeleteOnClose`) when the tree is deeper, comparing every packed cell and counting the non-zero cells that the scan does not define; the header statistics are recomputed, the text is recounted (every symbol below sigma, COUNTS, `TEXT_SHA256` when `VerifyTextSha256` is set and the header records one: 32 zero bytes mean "not recorded"). The limits are mutable for tests (`SesaVerifierTuning`). **`Structure` is refused for n >= 2^31 - 2** (`NotSupportedException`; the 32-bit views are the reader's limit, S2 widens them); `Header` and `Sections` read such a container. (7) **`NotSupportedException` and not corrupt:** a larger major version, an unknown REQUIRED section kind (the defined kinds 7-10 are ignored when optional and refused when REQUIRED), `POS_WIDTH` 8, a TEXT codec other than 4, an LCP codec other than 1-3, a CHILD codec other than 1 (the Tagged layout is refused), `BUILD_FLAGS` without the sentinel suffix array; everything else that is wrong is `InvalidDataException`. (8) **Options.** `EsaOpenOptions` (`Validation`, `Access`, `VerifyTextSha256`) is a sealed record in `SuffixTree.Esa`; the defaults are `Header` and `Checked`; the enum values are compared explicitly (no `Enum.IsDefined`, no LINQ on the open path: both cost first-call JIT in a fresh process). (9) **`LRS_RANK` = the left bound of the first deepest interval** (the engine's and the builder's convention; ADR-003's "first rank r with `LCP[r] == LRS_DEPTH`" is one more, see the amendment); `banana`: SA = 6 5 3 1 0 4 2, `LRS_DEPTH` 3, `LRS_RANK` 2. (10) **The adapter reads the container in place.** `e8-verify --container <file>` (additive: the existing command line, the 516 EmProto tests and the files of `e8-verify` are unchanged) runs the eleven checks of `OutputVerifier` on the sections through an `IVerifySource` (`FileVerifySource` for the seven files of E6-E9, `ContainerVerifySource` for a container: `.pk .sa .lcp .ovf .cld` are sections, `.pk.meta` and the `.stats` text are derived from the header), exit 2 for a container that is not one; the SHA-256 lines are those of the sections. (11) **The sparse tail of 1b.7e (limit (c), open point (e)) is documented, not removed** (the smaller and safer choice; see "The sparse-tail window" below). (12) **The cost drivers are explicit tests** (`SesaReaderCostTests`, `[Explicit]`, run by `analysis/em/run_1b7f_g2.sh` and `run_1b7f_g3.sh`, one variant per process, the cache dropped by the script; the reader runs do not set `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit=0x6000000`, which the build harness sets and which a 32 MiB chunk pair and a 64 MiB frame array do not fit).

**The sparse-tail window (limit (c) and open point (e) of 1b.7e: documented, not removed).** The container is created with its final length reserved for everything whose size is known after PH0 (TEXT, ALPHABET, COUNTS, SA, CHILD, PSI, BUILD_INFO, through `MappedOutputFile.Create`, `Auto`: the blocks are allocated), and its tail, the LCP and its overflow list, is added when PH4's first pass has fixed its width and the number of escapes: `MappedOutputFile.Grow` extends the file, and the BCL cannot reserve blocks for an existing file, so that part is **sparse**. Two pre-flights stand before it (12 B per symbol of free space at the start; the growth plus 16 B x (max LCP + 1) after PH4-B2), and they are the only protection: if another process fills the volume (or a quota is reached) **after the late pre-flight and before the LCP writers have stored their last page**, a store into a mapped page of the tail is a `SIGBUS` that ends the process; it is not an exception that the caller can catch. The window starts after the second pass of PH4 and ends with the last write of the LCP in the creation pass fused with PH5, so it is **at most the 7.3 s of PH4 and PH5 of a 27 s build at 10^8** (`ph45`, `rows_1b7f_g2.tsv`: 7.28 s and 7.40 s), and (the analysis of 1b.7e) it exists only for an LCP wider than one byte or with an overflow list; a build that ends normally has written every byte of every payload, so **a finished container has no hole and a reader never sees one** (the zero gaps between the sections are checked by `Sections`). Why it stays: making the file its final length before PH4 starts needs the first B2 pass to run before the creation, a change of the builder's phases that the brief puts out of scope for 1b.7f, and a disk that fills within seven seconds after a pre-flight that found room for the whole growth is the less likely failure; the same `SIGBUS` also ends a process whose mapped file is truncated or replaced in place by someone else. What a caller can do today: build on a volume that nothing else writes to (the scratch directory is `<target>.tmp.d` on the same volume), and treat the target as immutable once published. The owner may still decide for the other course (a final-length-first creation, one more B2 pass of PH4 before the creation, measured in G5 of the change).

**The three survivors of the 1b.7e audit that were left for this package.** `O1` (no unit test had an LCP_OVERFLOW list of 131,072 pairs, so the alignment of a section of 1 MiB or more was seen only by the 10^8 runs): **closed** by `SesaOverflowSectionTests` (U8: 2.1 M random symbols + a repeat of 140,000, 139,745 pairs; U16: 1.5 M + 200,000, 134,465 pairs; the section starts at a multiple of 4,096, the file ends with it, the whole file conforms, the reader reads it back at `Structure` with SHA-256 and equals the in-memory tree on 300 sampled counts and a 2,000-symbol locate). **The brief's example does not work: an `a^n` text of 300,000 symbols has an LCP of 32-bit entries, which has no overflow list**; a repeat of B symbols adds B - 255 escapes to a U8 LCP and B - 65,535 to a U16 LCP, which is where the sizes above come from. `V10` (a failed overflow writer leaves the writer of the base array open): a test of the failure (`AnOverflowWriterThatCannotBeCreated_LeavesNoOpenWriter_OfTheBaseArray`), killed in the audit below; `B102` (the BWT pass gets no guards) and `B118` (BUILD_INFO may overrun its 4 KiB room): **their options were not touched in this package** (the builder is unchanged), so no test was written; `B102` is a second-line defence (a guard only poisons the arena of a pass and changes no output of a correct pass; the build tests run with `ArenaOptions.Guarded(0xCD)` for the whole build, so the poison of every other pass is exercised and the BWT pass's is not observable) and `B118` is unreachable while BUILD_INFO has facts only (the text is about 300 bytes against 4,096); both are re-run in the audit below and argued in `1b7f_equivalent.py`.

**Defects found.** *Prototype:* none. *Production, by the tests of this package (all fixed, each with its test):* (1) **a child whose depth is not deeper than its parent's** (a damaged child table or LCP) looped the generic MEM algorithm in checked mode: `MakeChild` and the suffix-link walks now refuse it (`InvalidDataException`), found by the first round of the hostile bodies; (2) **`LengthOf(null)` was 0** for a node that a damaged table could not produce, so the `while (mLen > yEnd)` loop of the MEM walk never ended: it throws `InvalidDataException` for a null node of an index over a file (second round of the hostile bodies); (3) a failed open released the view twice (`ObjectDisposedException` instead of `InvalidDataException`): `SesaFile.Create` now owns the release of everything it took; (4) **the structure pass did not fit the memory the harness allows**: with the first default chunk of 8 Mi positions (64 MB of chunk arrays) and a 64 MiB frame array, a `Structure` open at 10^8 failed with an `OutOfMemoryException` under `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit=0x6000000`, the limit of the build harness; the default chunk is 4 Mi (the buffers are 33.6 and 41.6 MB at 10^8) and the reader runs do not set that limit (a finding about sizing, not a wrong answer). *Tests and tooling (mine):* the identity oracle's first version aliased a locus (`TryExtendRight(in locus, c, out locus)`) and reported false mismatches on `genome_100m`; `FileInfo.Length` of `/tmp/stbench/e7/genome_100m.txt`, a symlink to the E6 file, is the length of the link (the oracle now asks the open handle); the **first sample of "absent" patterns was random strings of length 1-40 over the alphabet, of which only 670 and 675 of 1,000 were absent** (the short ones occur), so the brief's 1,000 present + 1,000 absent was not met in the first G2 run: the sample now keeps only the patterns that the oracle finds absent and the G2 rows are from that run; a `BitmapBytes` expectation of mine rounded to bytes while the report counts whole 64-bit words; conformance cases for texts with code units above 255 cannot run through a container and are skipped for the file-backed factories. *ADR wording (amended):* the header CRC (the four CRC bytes are left out of the stream), `LRS_RANK` one off, the table order, widths and counts, the CHILD codec, PSI. *Brief:* the `a^n` example above. *Process:* the **tests of the adapter (`ContainerVerifyTests`) were written after the adapter**, the other new code had its tests first; the adapter's tests were then checked against mutants (the mutation audit lists 11 for the adapter).

**Mutation audit** (hand-made single-site mutants, each built and tested in Release in separate git worktrees with a watchdog, no `NUnit.StopOnError`, a limit of 360 s, analyzers off for the mutant builds, the slim test project `SuffixTree.Esa.Slim1b7f.csproj.template` that compiles the External, Contract and Facade tests of `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` against `SuffixTree.Esa`, the adapter's mutants against `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests`; `analysis/mutation/mutants_1b7f_data.py`, `make_1b7f_mutants.py`, `run_1b7f_mutants.py` through `run_1b7f_audit.sh`, `1b7f_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b7f.py`, `summary_1b7f.tsv`, results `results_1b7f_*.tsv`): **226 mutants listed, 226 run, 0 NOT run** (223 of the new production code in 19 files and the three survivors of the 1b.7e audit that its orchestrator left for this package, `V10`, `B102`, `B118`); **result: 198 killed + 3 killed by the watchdog (`M04` a hang, `R06` and `O06` a crash of the test host: a read of unmapped or damaged memory that the original refuses) + 17 argued equivalent + 8 second-line defences + 0 open** (226 = 201 + 17 + 8). **Round 1 (223 run, the three of 1b.7e were listed after it): 135 killed, 2 by the watchdog, 2 did not compile (`W23`, `W38`: rewritten), 84 survivors**, of which 23 are argued below and **61 were gaps of the tests**. They all had one cause: **a rule of the reader that another check of the same pass also catches survives every test that only asks for an `InvalidDataException`** (the permutation, the order and the Phi pass see the same damaged array; the descent, the walk and the two navigators see the same damaged table; the header rules overlap with the layout and the cross-checks). They were closed by **`SesaReaderIsolationTests` (75 cases)**, each of which damages one thing, seals the file again and **pins the message of the rule that is meant to see it**: the numbers of the header page (the `POS_WIDTH`, `SIGMA`, the statistics of an empty text, `NODE_COUNT`, `LRS_DEPTH`, `LRS_RANK`), the table entries (a repeated kind, the TEXT entry, the count and width of every array, the escapes of the LCP, the alphabet, `COUNTS` at zero, above and below n, a section that ends past the file by one byte, a file longer than its last section, the alignment of a section of 1 MiB), the structure pass (`SA[0]`, the position n at a rank above 0, a repeated position, an LCP longer than the shorter suffix or too short, `LCP[0]` and `LCP[1]`, the order, a letter that does not occur when no COUNTS is stored, the counts, UTF-8 and the lines of BUILD_INFO, the arrays of an empty text, a section without the CRC flag, a section checksum chained in pieces, the refusal of a text of 2^31 - 2 symbols), the descent and the walks over a child as deep as its parent (an LCP of 1 at a first l-index whose parent has depth 1: the damage is seen only where the depth of the child is read), over a child deeper than the text, over a first l-index outside its interval and over a root outside the text (the walk must stop **before** it reports the node), a leaf no longer than its parent's depth in both navigators, an edge longer than the comparison buffer (a pattern of 650 symbols changed at every position), the inverse refused for a repeated position, the failed opens that must leave nothing mapped (`/proc/self/maps` after a refusal at the header, the sections and the structure level), a text reader that must be disposed with its file, a named pipe refused by the reader and by the dispatcher, the locate of an index over a file with a skip and a small buffer against the definition; and, in the adapter's tests, a container without COUNTS (the recount) and the default scratch directory. Two small seams were added to the verifier so that two rules can be tested at all: the size of a piece of the section checksum (`SesaVerifierTuning.CrcPieceBytes`, 1 GiB by default: a section of 2 GiB or more is chained) and the rule on the length of a text (`RequireSupportedLength`). **Round 2: the 61 gaps + `N02` + the 2 that did not compile + the three of 1b.7e = 67 re-run on the new tests: 62 killed (`O06` by a crash of the test host), 5 survived** (`B102` and `B118`, argued; `N02`, argued; `D16`, whose test compared the file with the engine in memory **that shares the mutated code** and now compares it with the definition; `V03`, whose test met the repeated position in the inverse first). **Rounds 3 and 4: `D16` and `V03` killed** (the second after the suffix array was changed from a repeat to an exchange of two entries, which keeps it a permutation). **Closed by an argument, not a test (25)**, in `1b7f_equivalent.py`: *equivalent (17)*: `D01` (a key equal to the symbol leaves the loop at the `break` above), `D08` and `O08` (the same refusal repeats one line later), `W09` (the table and the hardware CRC give one value), `W32` (the bit n is never asked), `W37`, `W39`, `W46`, `W49`, `W50`, `W66`, `W79` (checks that restate what an earlier check of the same pass proved: the Phi pass's LCP equality and carry after the order check, the child scan's single definition of a cell), `W73` (the spill moves exactly half), `R02`, `R03` (the frame repeats the checks of `CountChildren` and `CheckFirst`), `Y02` (the next line begins with the same check), `B118` (BUILD_INFO cannot reach its room); *second-line defences (8)*: `R07`, `R08` (the same exception one line or one iteration later, only the events the visitor got before it differ), `N02` (the bound that keeps the inverse inside its buffer: with `>` the read past the end is refused only because the byte there is not -1; the checked build refuses it with another exception), `N05`, `N07`, `N08` (the equality side of three bounds that keep the walks finite on a damaged table; no construction was found), `F02` (the failure of `FromFile` is taken only above 2^31 - 2 symbols), `B102` (guards poison an arena and change no output). **The audit found no defect of the production code, only gaps of the tests**, and one fact about the platform: the `catch (NotSupportedException)` of the dispatcher is live on .NET 10 (a named pipe cannot be read at an offset), which `Q05` proved by dying. The pinned messages make the isolation tests as brittle as the messages (a reworded refusal breaks them: that is the intent); the 1b.7e rule that the audit runs without `NUnit.StopOnError` was kept the three kills by the watchdog were not re-run one by one.

**Not verified / limits (1b.7f).** (a) Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (G4 is a simulation; the arm64 CRC path was compiled, never run; the hostile-body tests need Linux `mmap` / `mprotect` and are skipped elsewhere; a big-endian host and a 32-bit process were not tried; the Windows rule that a mapped file cannot be replaced was not tried). (b) A text of more than 10^8 symbols: the reader ran at 10^8 on two texts; **the `Structure` time at 3x10^9 is an ESTIMATE and the pass does not scale linearly**: the Phi pass scans the suffix array once per chunk of 4 Mi positions, so for a fixed chunk the scans grow as n^2 (24 scans of 400 MB at 10^8, about 715 scans of 12 GB at 3x10^9); the share of the scans is small at 10^8 (0.14 s per scan of 400 MB), but the 12 GB suffix array of 3x10^9 symbols would be scanned about 715 times at the default chunk (about 4 s each, about 48 minutes of scans) and about 180 times at 16 Mi positions (about 12 minutes), on top of the linear rest (about 10 minutes): about an hour for the default, an ESTIMATE that no run has checked; `Header` and `Sections` are linear (`Sections` 0.6 s at 10^8, about 18 s at 3x10^9 if the disk keeps the rate, an estimate). (c) Cold-cache query latency: every throughput figure is warm; the first queries of a cold mapped file pay page faults that this package did not measure. (d) A file truncated, replaced or deleted-and-rewritten while it is mapped can fault the process (`SIGBUS`) in either mode (`Checked` bounds the reads against the section, not against the file system). (e) An adversarial container that is consistent in every check is accepted (threat model, above). (f) The independent oracles (`SesaOracle`, the identity oracle, the child-table definition in the tests) were written by the same implementer, from ADR-003; the SHA-256 equality with the sections that 1b.7e extracted with `sesa_sections.py` is the only cross-check by other code. (g) `e8-verify --container` was run on containers of the 1b.7e builder only. (h) Concurrency: `TheSameFile_CanBeOpenedTwice_AndReadFromManyThreads` reads one tree from several threads; a `Dispose` while another thread reads is not safe by design and not tested. (i) `Structure` is `NotSupported` for n >= 2^31 - 2, `POS_WIDTH` 8 and the Tagged child codec are refused, and nothing here reads layout F, NEXTSYM, BUCKET, ISA or RMQ sections. (j) The cold `Header` open was measured on one machine and one disk (a VM): all 10 runs are under 10 ms with a median of 7.6 ms; on a slower device only the read of the header page and the section table is added, nothing else is read. (k) **The file is 29-39 % slower than the tree in memory under `Checked` and 21-32 % under `Trusted` at 10^7 (G3, flagged): the cause was not isolated**; the hypotheses are the packed text reader against a symbol array, the 4 KB pages of a file mapping against the transparent huge pages that a large managed array can get, and the indirection of the views; none was tested, and the gap was measured on `dna_10m` only.

**Open points handed on, classified by the heading of the earlier entry that wrote them ("from X"); done = closed by this package.** **"Open points handed to 1b.7f (and to the owner)"** (from 1b.7e): (a) the reader, the dispatcher, the validation levels, `Checked` / `Trusted`, bounded child-table walks: **done**; (b) the `e8-verify` adapter: **done**; (c) the ADR-003 wording (`LRS_RANK`, table order, `ELEM_WIDTH` / `COUNT`, CHILD codec 1) and the PSI definition: **done by the amendment of 2026-11-19** (PSI moved into ADR-003 as kind 12); (d) `Resume` / `state.json`: **open**, outside this package by the brief (1b.8-1b.10 do not need it, the builder is one shot; S2 for a restartable 3 Gbp build); (e) the sparse tail: **documented** (above), the other course stays the owner's; (f) the `IncludePsi` default (+11 % build time, +4 B/char of file): **open and now better informed**: the reader checks Psi at `Structure` and ignores it otherwise, no engine operation uses it yet (the navigation pack builds its ISA by scatter from the SA in 4 B/char), so what Psi is for (suffix links through Psi, a forward text walk without the text) waits for the first user, in S2 or later; (g) the cached "verified" flag: **open**; the numbers for the owner: `Sections` 0.6 s and `Structure` 25 s at 10^8 against 27 s of build time. **"Open points handed to 1b.7b-e" / "1b.7c-e" / "1b.7d-e"** (from 1b.7a-d): the `Verify` of the reader (1b.7b (f)): **done**; 40-bit positions, both strands, NEXTSYM, BUCKET, ISA, RMQ sections, layout F, the Singer cover cache: **open for S2** (the reader refuses `POS_WIDTH` 8 and any REQUIRED kind it does not know, so such a file can be added later without a silent misread); the arena-peak and 3x10^9 estimates of E7 / E8: unchanged. **"handed to 1b.5 / 1b.6 / 1b.7", "1b.6 / 1b.7", "1b.7 / 1b.8"** (the facade over a file, the dispatch, the bounded walks): **done**; the "1b.8" half: **open for 1b.8** (below). **For 1b.8 (full differential fuzz):** run the harness of 1a.7 over `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` trees (`Checked` and `Trusted`, `Header` and `Structure`) as a further backend next to the in-memory one, and include the 4-thread shared-index test on a mapped tree (the mapping is shared and read-only, the navigation pack with the pyramid is built lazily under the index's own lock as for an in-memory index; `TheSameFile_CanBeOpenedTwice_AndReadFromManyThreads` is the only test of this so far); the reader's own fuzz (`Fuzz/SesaReaderFuzzTests`, Explicit) covers the damage, not the operations. **For 1b.9 (S1 gate measurement):** the numbers it asks for exist for the reader and are in G2 / G3 / G5 above (cold and warm open, `Checked` against `Trusted`, `Structure` time and RssAnon); what 1b.9 still needs from the reader is a cold-cache query measurement, the throughput on `dna_100m` (G3 here is `dna_10m` only) and **the cause of the 29-39 % gap to the tree in memory** (a profile of the descent over the mapped file; a variant that copies the packed text into the managed heap would separate the text reader from the paging). **For 1b.10 (review):** the new public surface is `SuffixIndexFactory.Open`, `EsaSuffixTreeFactory.Open`, `EsaOpenOptions`, `EsaValidationLevel`, `EsaAccessMode`; the internal one is the views and the verifier; the review should read the Checked-mode rules of choice (4) against the code, the `Structure` pass against ADR-003 section 6, and the amendment of ADR-003. **For S2:** the `Structure` pass at scale (a Phi distribution by text position instead of one SA scan per chunk, or an accepted time), the widening of the 32-bit views and of the engine beyond n = 2^31 - 2, the `Resume` journal, a cached "verified" flag, and the readers of NEXTSYM / BUCKET / ISA / RMQ when the writer produces them.

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-19, 1b.7f, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `22f50704`, tree clean; the 10 commits since `e4a0b2da` have both trailers and every title starts `ST-S1: 1b.7f `; the diff is 8 new files in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa` (1,518 lines: `SuffixIndexFactory`, `EsaOpenOptions`, `SaView`, `EsaText`, `External/Sesa/SesaFile`, `SesaHeaderReader`, `SesaVerifier`, and `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto/Verify/VerifySources`), **21 existing production files edited (+655/-118: the engine reads from views as a second storage form: `EsaIndex*`, `EsaNavigator`, `EsaNavigationPack`, `EsaSuffixIndex*`, `EsaSuffixTree*`, `ChildTable`, `ChildLayouts`, `CompactLcp`, `LcpPyramid`, `LeftmostOccurrence`, and four files of `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto`: `Cli`, `Experiments/E8`, `Verify/MetaFiles`, `Verify/OutputVerifier`)**, one `ProjectReference` (`SuffixTree.Esa` -> `SuffixTree.Persistent`, the only csproj change; the Slim template under `analysis/mutation` is not a project), two existing test files (`IndexConformanceTests`, `IndexEngineFactory`: a factory may refuse a text with a code unit above 255, so the two new file-backed factories skip such texts; the existing factories accept everything and their cases are unchanged), tests, `analysis/**`, ADR-003 (amendment dated 2026-11-19), ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; ADR-001/002, `wiki/`, the builder, `Persistent` sources untouched; a search of the added lines for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names and for a model identifier found nothing. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me on `22f50704`; the reverse closure of `SuffixTree.Esa` is `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests`, `SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` and `SuffixTree.EmProto` with its tests):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,729 passed + 10 skipped** (6 m 40 s, Release; 3,511 + 8 before, +218), Exhaustive **82 of 82** (18 m 26 s), Fuzz **38 of 38** (13 m 36 s), the **checked build** **3,730 + 9 skipped**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **533 of 533** (17 m 30 s; 516 + 17), the Bench build clean; skipped with the rule: Vectors, Ukkonen, Mcp.Core (no changed inputs). **Own checks at 10^8** (containers built by the harness variant `container`, scaled geometry, PSI on, cold cache; a throw-away client of the public `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` that is not in the repo): `Open` at **Header 46 / 49 ms** (whole process), **Sections 0.64 / 0.61 s**, **Structure 30.5 / 29.0 s** on `dna_100m` / `genome_100m`; **2,300 queries per text against my own oracle** (binary search over the E6 suffix array and the text file; 1,000 patterns of 1-40 symbols taken from the text, 1,000 that the oracle finds absent, 300 locate sets of 1-1,000 occurrences; fixed seed): **0 mismatches in `Checked` and in `Trusted` on both texts**; **`e8-verify --container` 11 of 11 on both** (5 s and 7 s), and the old `--prefix` mode **11 of 11** on `dna_100m` (the edited verifier is unchanged for files). **Hostile inputs on a 10^6 container** (one process per case, 60 s limit): all 640 bytes of the header and table and 300 random padding bytes flipped one by one (940 cases): **every one refused at `Header`** (938 `InvalidDataException`, 2 `NotSupportedException`); 300 random body bit flips: **every one refused at `Sections`**; **96 sealed damages** (the section CRC and the header CRC recomputed by my own tool: two adjacent SA entries swapped, an SA entry repeated, an SA entry out of range, an LCP value, a CHILD cell, a PSI cell, a TEXT symbol, a COUNTS entry; 8 kinds x 12 seeds): **every one refused at `Structure`** (COUNTS already at `Header`), no crash, no hang; a sealed adjacent SA swap in the 10^8 `genome_100m` container is refused at `Structure` after 6.7 s. **Cost, two flags:** (1) the file-backed tree against the in-memory tree (10 Mbp, one tree per process, 7 rounds with rotating order, warm): `Checked` **-36.9 % count, -33.2 % locate, -29.1 % absent**, confirming the implementer's 29-39 % (the flag of the brief, 25 %, stands;
