the abandon rule of 2x is far); `Trusted` came out slower than `Checked` in my medians (-57 / -40 / -59 %), but one process differs from another by up to 2x (Trusted count 238k-547k per second), so `Checked` against `Trusted` is **not resolvable**, and the cause of the gap to memory is **not found** (hypotheses: TLB misses on 4 KB file pages against huge pages of the managed arrays, the packed text reader, view indirection); (2) **the cold `Open` gate of 10 ms at 10 Mbp depends on what is timed**: with the JIT warmed on another container and the page cache dropped, **0.90-1.49 ms** (10 runs); in a fresh process, **13.5-25.1 ms (median about 16 ms), of which the first-call JIT is almost all**; the implementer's 7.0-9.1 ms lies between the two; the gate text does not say which, so 1b.9 must define it (ReadyToRun would remove the JIT part). **Mutation audit recounted** from `summary_1b7f.tsv`: 226 listed = **198 killed + 3 killed by the watchdog + 17 equivalent + 8 second-line defence + 0 open + 0 not run**, as stated; I re-ran **15 of the implementer's kills (12 at random, fixed seed, and the 3 watchdog ones)** and all 15 are killed (`M04` really hangs, `R06` and `O06` really crash the test host), and I wrote **26 own mutants of `SesaHeaderReader` and `SesaVerifier`** (boundaries of the statistics, counts, alphabet, escapes and sections; the bitmap, the order check, the LCP bound, the PSI checks): **20 killed, 6 survived**; of the 6, **three are equivalent** (`VE3` the LCP bound `l > n` is shadowed by the check `m != l` of the order test; `VE4` two distinct suffixes never compare equal; `VE6` COUNTS above the real count at one symbol imply one below at another because the sum is n, and the Header level checks that sum) and **three are small test gaps: damage in one direction only is tested** (`VE7` the first 8 bytes of a padding gap between sections are not checked for zero, `VE9` a PSI primary row stored below the real one is accepted, `VE12` an `LRS_DEPTH` stored above the real one is accepted at `Structure`). Method note: my first pass used the narrow filter of the round-1 list and showed 21 survivors; with the filter of all new reader test classes 15 of them are killed, because the lists of round 1 lag behind the tests of rounds 2-4. **I did not re-run G4 and G5, the 17 equivalence arguments or the 8 defences, and I did not check the Windows / macOS / arm64 paths.** Recorded for 1b.8-1b.10: define and report both the JIT-warm and the fresh-process cold `Open`; find the cause of the 29-39 % gap to memory (try a test with the in-memory arrays copied from the file, and 2 MB pages); damage in the other direction for `VE9` and `VE12` and a non-zero byte at the start of a padding gap; and **`IncludePsi` (default on) costs +11 % build time and +4 B/char while no engine operation reads PSI: 1b.9 reports the sizes and times with and without it and sets the default to off unless 1b.8 or S3 needs it**.

### 1b.8: the full differential fuzz over the in-memory and the file-backed trees, the Dispose race, and what the corruption fuzz found (2026-11-19)

The fuzz package of S1b (authorised by the orchestrator's brief of 2026-11-19; forecast 3-5 hours and 0.9-1.5 M tokens, cap 7 hours or 2.0 M tokens, stated before the launch; gates G1-G6 and the abandon rule fixed by the orchestrator before the work). Production code changed in six existing files (+234 / -63) and nothing was added: **a reader count around every public member of `EsaSuffixTree`** (`EsaSuffixTree.cs`, `.Navigation.cs`, `.Diagnostics.cs`: the lifetime paragraph of 1b.6 and the limit of 1b.7f, "a `Dispose` during a read is not safe by design and not tested", are replaced by the behaviour below) and **three refusals of a damaged file** (the file-backed `Traverse`: `EsaIndex.Traverse.cs`, `LeftmostOccurrence.cs`; the LCP pyramid over a file: `LcpPyramid.cs`). ADR-001, ADR-002, ADR-003 are untouched.

**The behaviour that changed.** A call that is in flight when `Dispose` runs completes with its answer; a call that starts afterwards throws `ObjectDisposedException`; nothing reads a mapping after it was unmapped. Every public member of `EsaSuffixTree` (the `ISuffixTree` members, the contract members of `ISuffixTreeNavigation` / `ISuffixArrayAccess`, the properties) counts itself as a reader for the length of the call, in a count per thread stripe (eight counts on cache lines of their own); `Dispose` raises a flag and the pins of the navigation pack and the mapped container are released by whoever finds the tree disposed and no reader inside (`Dispose` itself, or the last call that leaves). `Dispose` does not wait: a `Dispose` from inside a visitor of `Traverse` cannot deadlock, and the call that is running simply finishes. The three results of a file-backed tree that read the mapping after the call has returned (`Text`, `ISuffixIndex.Text`, the sequence of `EnumerateSuffixes`) count themselves as readers at every access (a stored `Text` of a disposed tree throws `ObjectDisposedException` at `Substring` / the indexer and reads nothing). **Evidence:** the race was written as a test first and was red: **three of the four race tests killed their process with `System.AccessViolationException`** (`CompactLcp.MappedValue`, `LcpPyramid.Fill` while a reader built the navigation pack, `SaView.get_Item`) on the head of 1b.7f; the five tests run in a child process (an access violation cannot be caught) and pass on the fix (80 rounds of four reader threads against a `Dispose` at a random moment, a `Dispose` that arrives inside a blocked read, a `Dispose` from inside a visitor, the lazy results, six trees of one file). **Cost:** one counter increment, one flag read and one decrement per call: **12.5-12.6 ns more per call than before** (the member `IsEmpty`, which does nothing else, costs 13.0 ns against 0.45 ns; the same instrument measured the first version, one shared count, at 16.7 ns; medians of 15 batches in each of 5 processes), against 1.3-1.7 us (medians) for `CountOccurrences` of a 20-mer and `FindAllOccurrences` of an 8-mer at 10 Mbp: **0.7-0.9 %, at most 5 % (G4 met)**. The protocol of the brief (one tree per process, seven processes per cell, median of five repeats, `FacadeGuardCostTests` and `SesaReaderCostTests`) shows no slowdown (ratio of the medians after / before 0.97-1.17, the paired ratios 0.61-1.55 with medians 0.96-1.10) but cannot resolve a 5 % effect on this machine (a spread of 40 % between processes of one binary); its rows are in `rows_1b8_dispose_cost.tsv` as they were taken (the after rows are the first version of the count, before the stripes). **A flag:** with four threads on one tree the facade call costs more than the call of the index behind it: the medians of the difference are 70-127 ns for the striped count (54-123 ns for the single count) against -49 .. +24 ns before the change, with single batches between -250 and +330 ns: about 3-6 % of a 20-mer count at 1.7-2.2 us, inside the noise of this instrument and not reduced by the stripes; it is the price of the memory safety of a shared tree, left for 1b.9 to measure as a throughput at 4 threads.

**What the harness is.** `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.Esa.Tests/Fuzz/` (the code of the 1a.7 harness, extended; the S8 CI job will run the same classes): a case is a pure function of (master seed, lane, index); the lanes are the three size bands of 1a.7 (n <= 64, 65-2,000, 2,001-10^5), a fourth band to 5 x 10^5 and the corruption and concurrency lanes, with shares of the time budget given on the command line (`analysis/em/run_1b8_fuzz.sh`, environment of `FullDifferentialFuzzTests`). Per text: the engines are the in-memory trees (three child layouts for short texts), the **file-backed trees from a container built by `SesaBuilder` from the text** (the tiny parameters of the builder up to 300 symbols, a small difference cover above; PSI on or off by the case; opened as `Header` / `Structure` in `Checked` / `Trusted` through `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` and `EsaSuffixTreeFactory.Open`), and the references (the Ukkonen tree and the v6 tree in heap mode, **at every n of every band**). Every engine is checked against the definitions where they are affordable (`FacadeChecks.CheckEverything` up to 120 symbols, the `ByDefinition` MEM / MUM / LCS and the definition-level search up to 1,000), against the Ukkonen and the v6 tree by a **digest** of every member (open results reduced to their property, ADR-001 section 7), through the contract checker and a digest of the navigation and suffix-array members, with the contract's negative loci, the argument errors of every member, and the suffix array and LCP array through `ISuffixArrayAccess` against the naive or the verified arrays. A text with a code unit above 255 has no file-backed engine (the container is one byte per symbol; counted). **The operation coverage list** `analysis/s1b/harness_member_coverage.tsv` has one row for every member of the audited interfaces and of the factory entry points (71 rows, derived from the types by reflection; the old `facade_member_coverage.tsv` listed the hand-written classes only); a default test fails when a member has no row or a row names no member, and another proves that the harness calls every member on the in-memory and on the file-backed tree. The counting decorator (`CountingTree`) reports the calls per member and engine.

**Defects found, all by this harness, all fixed with a test.** *Production:* (1) the Dispose race above (a crash); (2) **a suffix array that is in range but not a permutation** (sealed damage; found as `SealedWords` of the suffix array) made `FindMaximalRepeatedPairs` over a `Checked` file close a cycle in the leaf lists of the shared collector and append pairs until `OutOfMemoryException`: the mapped `Traverse` now keeps one bit per text position and refuses a second report of a position, or a position outside [0, n], with `InvalidDataException` (the first version of the check indexed its bitmap with the unchecked value: an `IndexOutOfRangeException`, found by the same fuzz one run later); (3) **an LCP[1] other than 0 or a negative LCP value** made `LeftmostOccurrence` pop its base interval and end in an `IndexOutOfRangeException` of a managed array instead of `InvalidDataException` (found as `SealedByteFlip` / `SealedWords` of the LCP section; four sweep tests, every byte value at every rank of four texts, three of them red before); (4) **a damaged LCP overflow list** made the LCP pyramid of a file throw `InvalidOperationException` ("the LCP pyramid is inconsistent") at query time instead of `InvalidDataException`: found by the **first 30-minute run** (master seed 1, `periodic-noise:676b7e6f031cd313:427:2:59`, `SealedWords` of the LCP overflow section, which `Structure` accepts when the words keep the permutation and the order), fixed (`LcpPyramid` reports `InvalidDataException` when its LCP is mapped, `InvalidOperationException` in memory as before), the case is in `regressions.txt` (the replay was red before the fix; test and fix are one commit, `03287f14`). *Harness (mine, in the first runs):* a children buffer too small for a root of 1,300 children, a wrapper that hid a null visitor, a quadratic MEM output on a Fibonacci word (35 M triples per query) that made one case run for minutes, a header flip of the section count that the harness could not seal. *Existing tests:* none wrong. The harness found no mismatch between the in-memory tree, the file-backed trees, the Ukkonen tree and the v6 tree in any run.

**Container corruption fuzz (item 5).** Eleven kinds of damage are drawn (the kinds of 1b.7f at random and four more): a byte of the header page, a byte of the body, a sealed byte of the SA / LCP / child / PSI section, a truncation, sealed words with random and extreme values, a sealed adjacent swap, trailing bytes, a byte of a section-table entry, a byte of a header field, a sealed byte of the text / alphabet / counts / build info, and a sealed entry replaced by its neighbour, by its own index, by itself +- 1 or by an interior peak of the LCP array minus one. Every open at every level is a success or `InvalidDataException` / `NotSupportedException`, a refusal is monotone in the level, the kinds whose level the 1b.7f tests fixed are refused at that level (a header flip of the magic, the endian probe, the file size or the reserved word at `Header`, a body flip at `Sections`, a sealed flip or swap at `Structure`, the counts at `Header`), a file that every level accepts answers like the undamaged text (digest equal), and a file that the `Header` level accepts is read in `Checked` mode from memory between two inaccessible pages with every kind of operation, each of which must return or throw `InvalidDataException` (a locus that a damaged table produced may fail the argument check of the next call), under a watchdog.

**Budgeted run (G2).** Two runs of 1,800 s on the machine alone, 4 workers per lane, `analysis/em/run_1b8_fuzz.sh` (rows `rows_1b8_fuzz_run1_one_failure.tsv` and `rows_1b8_fuzz.tsv`). **Run 1** (master seed 1, 18:55-19:27 UTC, 314,853 cases) **found one failure**, defect (4) above; it was fixed and the case entered the regression corpus. **Run 2 (master seed 2, 19:28-19:59 UTC, head `03287f14`): 312,042 cases in 1,852 s (the test process: 30 m 52 s), 0 failures.** Lanes: tiny 13,615 cases (n <= 64; 4,973 of them skipped on the file-backed engines because a code unit exceeds 255), small 3,646 (1,483), large 282 (132), xl 8 (1; n from 122,508 to 221,408 in the first and the last), corruption 294,340 (113,702 skipped; **180,638 damaged files**, 89,395 of them read between guard pages with 5,883,231 operations answered and 1,057,345 refused as `InvalidDataException`), concurrency 151 cases = **6,660 repetitions of 4 threads, 26,640 checksums equal to the single-thread run, 6,660 once-only pack builds checked**; every one of the 13 families in every lane (tiny 997-1,104 per family, small 249-314, large 16-28, corruption 22,514-22,939). Engine checks (a check is one text against one engine; the rate is per second of that engine's own check time, summed over the workers): memory-default 17,551 (30.8 per second), memory-packed 15,415 (49.1), memory-reference3 15,461 (52.8), the four file-backed engines 10,962 each (25.6-25.9), Ukkonen 17,551 (27.6), v6 17,551 (23.4); **2.46 x 10^9 calls of the 71 members** (the least called: the factories, `EsaSuffixTreeFactory.Open` 3,686, `Create` / `CreateEsa` 4,352-4,433, `EsaOpenOptions.VerifyTextSha256` 10,886, `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` 40,162, `IDisposable.Dispose` 99,419; every other member at least 117,901), per member and engine in the rows file. **The limit of the references:** the Ukkonen and the v6 tree are compared at every n of every band up to 5 x 10^5 (the 1b.6 tests stopped at n = 150); beyond that the cost is the limit, not a mismatch: the v6 heap tree takes 1.28 s and 69 MB at 10^6, 6.2 s and 275 MB at 4 x 10^6, 14.9 s and 687 MB at 10^7 (the Ukkonen tree 0.74 s and 161 MB, 4.7 s and 483 MB, 12.4 s and 966 MB; the ESA tree 0.10 s, 0.32 s, 1.0 s), and four workers each hold the three trees of their case (`rows_1b8_dispose_cost.tsv`, section refbuild).

**Harness audit (mutation).** 71 hand-made single-site mutants of the production engine (`analysis/mutation/make_1b8_mutants.py`: the search, analysis and matching arms of `EsaIndex`, the traversal, `LeftmostOccurrence`, `EsaSuffixIndex`, `EsaNavigator`, `EsaNavigationPack`, `SaView`, `EsaText`, `LcpPyramid`, the mapped child table, `SesaFile`, `SesaHeaderReader`, `SesaVerifier`, `SuffixIndexFactory`, `EsaSuffixTree`), each built in a separate git worktree and run against the budgeted harness for 60 s at two workers (`run_1b8_mutants.py`, `run_1b8_audit.sh`, watchdog 360 s, analyzers off, heap capped at 3 GB because a mutant can make a list grow without end): **71 listed, 71 run, 0 not run: 51 killed in round 1, 5 more killed after harness extensions (rounds 2 and 3), 2 killed by the existing unit tests of 1b.7f and not by the harness, 11 argued equivalent, 2 second-line defences, 0 open** (`summary_1b8.tsv`, `1b8_equivalent.py`, the first failure of each kill with its minimized text in `fails_1b8.tsv`). The 20 survivors of round 1 were of two kinds: 12 argued (the generic algorithms validate the same arguments one call deeper, a guard that repeats a test of the callee, a bound that two other tests make unreachable, a LCP value smaller than the true one that the order pass of `Structure` refuses first) and 8 gaps of the harness, closed by an extension and re-run: absent patterns that deviate from a present pattern inside an edge (`S03`), loci that are not loci with the answer fixed by the contract (`X07`), the strict levels of the reserved header word, the file size and the symbol counts (`H01`, `H03`, `H04`), and damage that keeps the shape of the tree (`W01`: still not killed, argued); `S06` and `T07` (a child as deep as its parent) need a damaged LCP array that gives exactly that depth where a pattern descends, which the random damage does not draw: the unit test of 1b.7f builds it and kills them. **An injected fault on the file-backed path is found and minimized:** `S05` (one line of `EsaIndex.MatchMapped`, the pattern translated to the wrong symbol) is found in the first case and minimized to the one-symbol text `a` (`file-header-checked:CheckEverything:CountOccurrences`), `C01` (one line of `MappedChild.NextLIndex`) to `aa`, `Y01` (the dispatcher drops the options) by a corruption case to a 2-symbol text.

**Not verified / limits (1b.8; the full list is in PROGRESS-S1).** Nothing ran on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the guard pages of the corruption fuzz need Linux `mmap` / `mprotect`; elsewhere that lane runs without them). A damaged file opened in `Trusted` mode may crash by contract, so the corruption fuzz reads damaged files in `Checked` mode only. The race is a probability: 80 rounds of four readers against a `Dispose` at random moments and the ordering argument in the documentation of `EsaSuffixTree.Enter` (no model checker). The cost of the count at four threads is within the noise of its instrument (3-6 %), not resolved. The audit mutants are single-site and hand-made, for the engine only (the builder, PSI and the reader of `e8-verify` are outside it). The corruption lane works on texts of at most 4,000 symbols and on containers of the fast test writer of 1b.7f. **Test policy (G6, the affected set on the final head `9a9ac926`):** the code did not change after `9a9ac926`; `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,763 passed + 10 skipped** (3,729 + 10 before; 10 m 33 s, Debug), Exhaustive **82 of 82** (17 m 39 s, Release), Fuzz **39 of 39** (14 m 43 s, Release, with the 60-second default budget), checked build **3,764 passed + 9 skipped** (7 m 5 s, Release); skipped: Persistent, EmProto, the Bench build, Ukkonen / `Mcp.Core` / Vectors (their inputs did not change); no full-solution run; no result of an existing test changed.

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-19, 1b.8, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `165467c8`, tree clean; the 24 commits since `31bc0646` (the report says 23) all start `ST-S1: 1b.8 ` with a space and have both trailers; the diff is **six existing production files (+234/-63): `EsaSuffixTree`, `EsaSuffixTree.Navigation`, `EsaSuffixTree.Diagnostics` (the reader count), `EsaIndex.Traverse`, `LeftmostOccurrence`, `LcpPyramid` (three refusals as `InvalidDataException`)**, no new production file, the harness (12 files in `Esa.Tests/Fuzz`, three `Facade` test classes, `SesaTraverseDamageTests`), `regressions.txt` (+13 cases), `analysis/**`, `TEST_HIERARCHY.md`, `SUFFIX_TREE_TEST_MATRIX.md`, ADR-004 and PROGRESS-S1; no csproj, `apps/`, `wiki/`, ADR-001/002/003 change; one rows file, `analysis/mutation/results_1b8_esasuffixtree.tsv`, is classed as binary by git (an odd byte in it; hygiene only); a search of the added lines (as text, `grep -a`) for the owner's name, e-mail and GitHub names, a model identifier and the sandbox account name found nothing. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me on `165467c8`; `EmProto` and `Persistent` are in my set because the reverse closure of `SuffixTree.Esa` contains `EmProto` and its tests, although the implementer skipped them):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,763 passed + 10 skipped** (7 m 11 s Release; 3,729 + 10 before), Exhaustive **82 of 82** (17 m 34 s), Fuzz **39 of 39** (14 m 30 s, with the 60-second budget run of the harness; 38 before), the **checked build** **3,764 + 9 skipped**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **533 of 533** (16 m 40 s), the Bench build clean. **The Dispose race, reproduced independently:** my throw-away client of the public `Open` (not in the repo; 4 reader threads loop on count, contains and locate over one file-backed tree while the main thread calls `Dispose` after a random 0-40 ms; one process per round, 150 rounds per revision, the same client built against both revisions): **before the fix (`31bc0646`) 62 of 150 processes crashed (41 %)**, the other 88 ended with `ObjectDisposedException` in the readers; **after (`165467c8`) 150 of 150 ended cleanly, 0 crashes, 0 hangs, every reader saw only `ObjectDisposedException`**. **Cost of the reader count** (file-backed, `Checked`, 10 Mbp, 7 processes per revision with alternating order, medians): one thread **count +10.5 %, locate +0.4 %, absent +0.2 %** (the 7 runs of one revision spread 311-454 thousand count per second, so +10.5 % is noise), **four threads on one tree +3.4 %** (2.93-3.62 million per second per run, noise too): no cost that this protocol resolves, in agreement with the implementer's in-process instrument (about 12.5 ns per call, 0.7-0.9 % of a 20-mer count); the contended cost that the report lists as not verified is thereby measured at the same resolution (not resolvable, no sign of a slowdown). **Own 30-minute run of the harness** (a different master seed, **3**, on the otherwise idle machine, `run_1b8_fuzz.sh` at `165467c8`, 4 threads): **336,314 cases in 1,953 s, 0 failures**; lanes tiny 14,430 cases (5,223 skipped for a code unit above 255), small 3,919 (1,616), large 242 (108), xl 9 (4), **corruption 317,547 (123,174 skipped, so 194,373 damaged-file cases; 96,345 batteries read between guard pages, 6,339,217 operations answered, 1,141,130 refused as `InvalidDataException`, no crash, no hang)**, **concurrency 167 cases = 7,220 repetitions x 4 threads = 28,880 thread runs, every checksum equal to the single-thread run, the once-only pack build checked 7,220 times**; per-engine checks: memory-default 18,600, Ukkonen 18,600, v6 18,600, memory-packed 16,358, memory-reference3 16,421, each of the four file-backed engines 11,649; every one of the 13 families ran in every lane; every member of the coverage list was called (the least called: `EsaSuffixTreeFactory.Open` 3,948 times). **Harness audit recounted** from `summary_1b8.tsv`: 71 listed = **56 killed by the harness (51 in round 1, 5 after an extension of it) + 2 killed only by the existing unit tests + 11 argued equivalent + 2 second-line defence + 0 open, none unrun**, as stated; I wrote **32 mutants by a fixed-seed random choice of operator flips (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `==`, `!=`, `+ 1`, `- 1`) in nine engine files** and ran the harness (60 s budget each) against each: **21 killed (2 of them by the harness watchdog), 11 survived**; of the 11, **seven change nothing the contract can see** (an array one entry longer, the width choice of the LCP at an exact boundary twice, `<` against `<=` after the equality was already handled in the overflow binary search, an empty-slice edge compare, a one-word-longer bitmap, a count of value 0) and **four are small gaps of the harness: (1) `ChildLayouts` `>=` to `>` in the `Checked` bound of a mapped child cell, i.e. a read of one cell past the section is not noticed because the guard pages sit at the end of the mapping, not of the section; (2) `ChildTable` `ell > LrsDepth` to `>=`, i.e. which of two longest repeats of equal length is reported is not compared between engines; (3) and (4) the boundary of the corrupt-leaf-depth guard (`_n - saA + 1 <= parentDepth`) in `EsaNavigator` and `EsaSuffixIndex.Tree`, which the corruption fuzz does not produce with equality**; so 21 of the 25 non-equivalent mutants are found (84 %). **I did not re-run the implementer's 71 mutants, the 11 equivalence arguments, G5, nor any run above 10^6 symbols or on another platform.** Recorded for 1b.9-1b.10: add the four gaps to the harness (a section-end guard in the corruption lane, a tie-break comparison for the repeat queries, equality cases of the leaf-depth guard) and report the 4-thread count throughput of the file-backed tree at 100 Mbp in 1b.9.

### 1b.9a: why the file-backed tree was 25-35 % slower than the in-memory tree, and the fix (2026-11-20)

The package of the owner's requirement of 2026-11-19 ("find the cause and fix it"; forecast 3-6 hours and 0.9-1.6 M tokens, cap 8 hours or 2.2 M tokens, stated before the launch; the gate was fixed by the orchestrator before the work: `Checked` at most 10 % slower than the in-memory tree on count of present 20-mers, locate of 8-mers and count of absent 24-mers at 10 Mbp, medians of at least 15 processes per variant with the order rotated, also reported at 100 Mbp and on a five-symbol text; the in-memory tree not slower than at `e4a0b2da`; a cold `Header` open of at most 2 ms). **Cause: the code of the mapped arm, in the order of its share, and the layout of the child table; not the memory system.** **Fix, software only and managed only:** the pattern descent over a container is one loop over raw pointers specialised by struct type arguments (`MappedSearch`), and the container has a second CHILD codec, the tagged layout of the in-memory tree, which the builder writes by default (ADR-003 amendment of 2026-11-19, a default taken by the orchestrator under the owner's requirement, reversible). **Result: the gate is met on the text that it names, and not met in two places that are shown below with their isolated experiments.** DNA, 10 Mbp, `Checked` against the in-memory tree, medians of 41 processes per variant with the order rotated: 0.5 / 3.1 / -1.2 % slower on count of present 20-mers / locate of 8-mers / count of absent 24-mers (the in-process A/B: 0.0 / 1.2 / 2.8 % slower); 100 Mbp (22 processes): 2.3 / 3.7 / 4.0 % (in process the file-backed tree is 3.3 / 0.4 / 3.6 % **faster**); a five-symbol text: 5.9 / 3.5 / 6.6 %; the cold `Header` open 0.49 ms (median of 10 processes after a dropped page cache), 0.013 ms warm. **Not met:** (1) the **protein-like text** (5 bits per symbol, 20 symbols): 19.0 / 17.0 / 16.4 % by the medians of 21 processes, 6.2-8.2 % paired by round and 4.1-7.6 % in the in-process A/B, so the gate is **not claimed** for it (the checks are not the cause; one cause is shown, 1-3 points, and the rest is not isolated); (2) **the in-memory tree is 4-5 % slower than at `e4a0b2da`**, a slowdown that is already in `e57cdae0` (1b.7f / 1b.8) and was not made here: the head equals `e57cdae0` within the spread. Production code: **1 new file (`MappedSearch.cs`, 333 lines)** and **16 existing production files edited (+119 / -35)**; ADR-003 amended; no csproj changed; no `P/Invoke`, no `madvise`, no hint of any kind.

**Instruments.** There is no `perf` on this machine (a Firecracker VM, 4 vCPU, 15 GB, kernel 6.18.44, transparent huge pages `madvise`, no PMU), so the diagnosis uses what a managed process can read: a stopwatch harness (`analysis/em/readbench`, one tree per process with a JIT warm-up by time, or several trees in one process with the passes in a rotated order: the **in-process A/B**, which resolves 1-2 % where the spread between processes is up to 2x), `/proc/self/smaps_rollup` (`AnonHugePages`, `FilePmdMapped`), a micro-benchmark of dependent random loads over 128 MB, and a **replay of the descent that counts the reads, the distinct 64-byte lines and the distinct 4 KB pages per query** (`ExperimentsCount`, an instruction-count by experiment). The experiments change one factor of the file-backed path and nothing else (`ExperimentsIx`, `ExperimentsRaw`; they need the container of 1b.7f, codec 1, and the head of the diagnosis `e57cdae0`).

**The gap, measured twice.** In one process with the in-memory and the file-backed tree side by side and the passes in rotated order (3 processes x 9 passes, facade level): `Checked` / in memory 0.759 / 0.745 / 0.763 on count of 20-mers, 0.815 / 0.746 / 0.806 on locate of 8-mers, 0.741 / 0.651 / 0.742 on absent 24-mers: **24-35 % slower, the 29-37 % of the 1b.7f verification**. In separate processes the medians move with the process: 15 processes per variant gave 12 / 3 / 11 % (spread of one variant 700-940 thousand count/s), ten rounds on another hour 23 / 15 / 25 % paired by round (0.56-1.04 between rounds). The order of processes and of the hour matter more than any of the effects below, which is why the table is in-process.

**Isolated effects** (throughput relative to the in-memory tree = 1.000; engine level, in-process, 6 processes x 11 passes, medians of the per-process medians; spread over processes in brackets; one factor changed against the row above or as stated):

| Hypothesis | Experiment (only this changes) | count 20-mers | locate 8-mers | absent 24-mers |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| reference | in-memory tree (tagged child table, byte text, `int[]` arrays) | 1.000 | 1.000 | 1.000 |
| the file as shipped | `Checked`, mapped, packed child table, packed text, the typed views | **0.742** [0.731-0.752] | **0.796** [0.785-0.814] | **0.724** [0.714-0.747] |
| 4 checks | the same file in `Trusted` mode (the checks of the hot loop left out) | 0.757 | 0.810 | 0.736 |
| 2 packed text | the shipped code, the text as a byte per symbol (a copy) | 0.770 | 0.865 | 0.766 |
| 3 views and branches | raw pointers, byte text, no checks, the packed child table of the file (the same mapped arrays) | **1.025** [0.980-1.045] | **1.054** | **1.004** |
| 1 page size / TLB | the same code (packed text, packed child, `Checked`) over the **mapped** arrays | 0.889 | 0.981 | 0.888 |
| 1 page size / TLB | the same code over **resident** copies of the arrays (pinned managed arrays) | 0.895 | 0.978 | 0.876 |
| 6 layout (not on the list) | the in-memory tree with the packed child table instead of the tagged one | 0.857 | 0.952 | 0.847 |
| 6 layout | the in-memory engine over resident copies of the file's arrays, packed / tagged child table | 0.853 / 1.008 | 0.874 / 0.925 | 0.835 / 0.996 |

1. **Page size and TLB: not the cause, and the premise is wrong.** A random dependent load over 128 MB costs 146-169 ns through a read-only file mapping, 151-168 ns in a pinned managed array and 148-169 ns in `NativeMemory`: **the same** (five processes each). Managed arrays are **not** huge-page backed here (`AnonHugePages` 0 kB in every process: the setting is `madvise` and the runtime does not call it); the page cache holds the container in 2 MB folios (`FileHugePages` 114 MB) but the process maps them with 4 KB entries (`FilePmdMapped` 0). The same descent code over the mapped arrays and over resident copies differs by 0.6, 0.3 and 1.2 points (the two middle rows). A mapping that is advised `MADV_HUGEPAGE` is another matter and is measured below as a **throw-away number for the owner**; it is not shipped.
2. **Packed text decode: about 4 points when it is done through the shipped reader (a 64-bit window with a branch on the bit offset, a decode into a buffer and a byte comparison per edge), nothing when it is done inline.** Written as one load and shift per symbol and as a comparison of the pattern packed the same way against 64-bit windows, a packed text costs -1 / -1.4 / 0 points against a byte text (6 processes x 15 passes, `ix-rx-m-b-p-c` against `ix-rx-m-p-p-c`). **No unpacked TEXT section is needed**, and none was added (+0.75 B/char for DNA saved).
3. **View indirection and branches: the largest effect, 25 points.** The shipped descent read the container through `SaView` (an array-or-pointer test and a checked-mode test on every read), `CompactLcp` (the layers of the indexer: the mapped test, a switch on the kind, the escape test), `MappedChild` (a checked-mode test) and `MappedTextReader` (a limit test and the window), through structs that are passed by copy. Raw pointers for every array, no checks and a byte text (the row `raw`) are **as fast as the in-memory tree** (1.025 / 1.054 / 1.004) although the child table is still the packed one.
4. **`Checked` against `Trusted`: 1.2-1.5 points**, below the spread (`Trusted` is not reliably faster in separate processes: 605 against 666 thousand count/s in 15 processes each, the instrument's noise, not an effect). The checks are cheap; what was expensive was the structure that carried them.
5. **Access order and prefetch.** One descent of a 20-mer touches, in the in-memory layout, 127.8 reads, **64.5 distinct cache lines and 49.9 distinct pages**; in the container as written (packed child table, packed text) **181.2 reads, 76.3 lines (+18 %) and 57.7 pages (+15.5 %)**: the extra are the LCP array (20.3 lines against 8.5: the kind of a field of the packed table is told by comparing LCP values, so a step to the next sibling reads the LCP array at `k`, `k + 1` and the target) and 54 more reads of it; the lines of the text are the same (17.9) packed or in bytes; with the tagged layout the counts equal the in-memory ones (127.8 / 64.5 / 49.8). Locate (descent only): 86.8 reads, 52.3 lines against 126.8 and 64.3; absent 24-mers: 121.9 / 63.8 against 174.2 / 75.6. A software prefetch of the next sibling's cell (`Sse.Prefetch0`, a managed intrinsic) was tried on the lean descent: **not resolved** (+3.4 / -0.7 / +1.0 points over 3 processes x 9 passes), not shipped.
6. **The child-table layout (not on the brief's list; found by 5).** The container's CHILD codec 1 is the layout `Packed`; the in-memory tree uses `Tagged` up to 2^30 - 1 symbols. In the in-memory engine the packed layout costs 14 / 5 / 15 points (the row above); in the lean mapped descent the tagged layout is worth **+4.0 % on count, +3.6 % on absent and -2 % on locate** (6 processes x 15 passes, two copies of each variant: the instance noise is 0.5 %).

**The in-memory tree against `e4a0b2da`: slower, and not by this package.** The in-memory code of 1b.7f and 1b.8 is the same engine behind one branch (`IsMapped`), the typed views and one reader count per call. Across processes (15 per build, order rotated, count / locate / absent, `rows_1b9a_inmem_e4a0b2da_vs_e57cdae0.tsv`, `rows_1b9a_gate_inmem_old_new.tsv`): `e57cdae0` against `e4a0b2da` +3.5 / +1.1 / +3.9 % slower paired by round, the head 5.3 / 4.8 / 6.4 % slower by medians (paired 4.5 / 3.8 / 5.1 %, interval -2.9 .. 10.9): **not resolved** (the spread of one build is 0.75-1.29 million per second). **The in-process A/B across builds** (the in-memory tree of `e4a0b2da`, of `e57cdae0` and of the head side by side in one process, each in an `AssemblyLoadContext` of its own, 5 processes x 9 passes, throughput relative to `e4a0b2da`, medians of the processes, count / locate / absent): **`e57cdae0` 0.956 / 0.987 / 0.955, the head 0.945 / 0.961 / 0.952**. So the in-memory tree is **4-5 % slower than at `e4a0b2da`, the slowdown is already in `e57cdae0` (1b.7f and 1b.8) and the head equals `e57cdae0` within the spread** (head against `e57cdae0`: -1.1 / -2.6 / -0.3 points, range of the processes 4-8 points); this package changes nothing on the in-memory arm. **The item of the gate "the in-memory tree not slower than at `e4a0b2da`" is therefore not met, and not by this package.** An isolated experiment on the cause (a copy of the head in which the six search members of an in-memory tree do not take the reader count of 1b.8, `Enter` / `Leave`, and only test the disposed flag): **0.981 / 1.000 / 0.980** (+3.6 / +3.9 / +2.8 points over the head): most of the slowdown is the striped count; the rest (0-2 points) is not isolated. Not shipped: it changes the contract of the 1b.8 count for in-memory trees (a `Dispose` would no longer wait for a running search of an in-memory tree, which is memory safe but is a change of a verified behaviour), and the budget did not allow the re-run of every set and of the audit on it; recorded for 1b.9.

**What was changed, and why.** (1) **`MappedSearch`** (new, `SuffixTree.Esa`): the descent of `DescendMapped`, fetching the pointers once at the construction and running one loop per combination of access mode (`CheckedAccess` / `TrustedAccess`: a compile-time constant, so that the checks are not in the code of a `Trusted` file), LCP width (8, 16, 32 bits) and CHILD codec (12 loops, each instantiated on first use); the text is compared in 64-bit windows against the pattern packed the same way (a stretch of `64 / b` symbols per step, built when the first stretch needs it; `b` from 1 to 8); the window of a symbol has no branch on the bit offset; **the checks of `Checked` mode are those of the reference and no others** (every rank that the walk takes from the table must increase and stay inside its interval, a child must be deeper than its parent, every rank and every value read from a section that is used as an index is validated first; the text positions that come from the suffix array are clamped to the text). **`EsaIndex.DescendMapped` stays as the readable reference**; the tests compare the two. (2) **CHILD codec 2** (ADR-003 amendment): the cell of layout A with the kind in bits 30-31 and the rank in 30 bits, the file form of the in-memory `TaggedChild`; **the builder writes it by default** for a text of at most 2^30 - 1 symbols (`SesaBuildOptions.ChildCodec`, `SesaBuilder.ChildCodecFor`), the stage builder takes either codec, the reader reads both (`SesaHeaderReader.CheckChildCodec`, `MappedChild` with a tagged arm for the navigator and the traversal, `ChildTable.FromMapped`), the `Structure` level compares the table in the layout of its codec, `BUILD_INFO` names the layout, and `e8-verify` knows the codec of a container (`--child-codec` for a prefix run). A file of 1b.7e / 1b.7f (codec 1) is read unchanged. (3) The pointers that `MappedSearch` needs: `MappedChild.Pointer`, `MappedTextReader.Pointer`.

**Not done, and why (the data justify none of them).** *A resident open mode* (`EsaOpenOptions` choice to read the hot sections into managed memory): the same code over resident copies is not faster (row 1), so it would cost 132 MB and the open time for nothing; *an unpacked TEXT section*: the packed text costs nothing when it is decoded inline (point 2); *prefetch*: not resolved (point 5); *`madvise`*: not implemented (the rule of the project), measured outside the shipped code, see below.

**`madvise(MADV_HUGEPAGE)`: measured outside the shipped code, not implemented, and on this kernel it cannot help.** A throw-away copy of the harness outside the repository (`/tmp`, a P/Invoke of `madvise` on the 2 MB aligned part of the mapping, 126 of the 132 MB of the 10 Mbp container, called right after the open) was run once and its number is recorded here for the owner; no P/Invoke, no `madvise` and no hint of any kind is in the repository. The kernel accepts the advice (return value 0, the mapping gets the `hg` flag) but **maps no huge page**: `FilePmdMapped` stays 0 kB, `AnonHugePages` 0 kB and `THPeligible` is 0 for the mapping, because this kernel (6.18.44, transparent huge pages `madvise`, ext4) is built without `CONFIG_READ_ONLY_THP_FOR_FS`: the page cache holds the file in large folios, but a read-only file mapping is not mapped with them. Throughput with the advice against without it, in one process (4 processes x 9 passes, `rows_1b9a_gate_exp_madvise.tsv`): **0.986 / 1.004 / 0.978** (count / locate / absent), that is **no effect**, as the control of the table says about page size in general (resident copies and `NativeMemory` are not faster). Whether another kernel (one with `CONFIG_READ_ONLY_THP_FOR_FS`, or `MADV_COLLAPSE`) would map huge pages for a file is not known and was not measured; the number does not call for a decision about the rule.

**The gate, measured on the head of the code (`c940c097` and the later commits, which change tests, harness and documents only).** One tree per process, JIT warm by time, the same patterns for every variant, the variants in a rotated order from round to round, the page cache warm, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit` unset; "slower" is `1 - median(Checked) / median(in memory)` over all processes, "paired" the median over the rounds of the same quantity inside one round; the interval is a bootstrap over the processes (95 %); the in-process A/B puts the three trees in one process, 11 passes per variant, passes rotated (`rows_1b9a_gate_*.tsv`, `rows_1b9a_gate_summaries.txt`, driver `run_1b9a_gate.sh`).

| Text | Processes per variant | `Checked` slower than in memory, count 20-mers / locate 8-mers / absent 24-mers | paired by round | 95 % interval of the first | in-process A/B (`Checked`, relative to in memory) | `Trusted` (medians) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DNA 10 Mbp | 41 | **0.5 / 3.1 / -1.2 %** | 3.4 / 3.9 / 2.4 % | -13.4 .. 12.5 | 1.000 / 0.988 / 0.972 (6 processes) | 1.0 / -3.8 / -5.9 % | **PASS** (the fixed gate) |
| DNA 100 Mbp (scaled geometry, container 1.33 GB) | 22 | **2.3 / 3.7 / 4.0 %** | -5.9 / -1.1 / -5.8 % | -16.8 .. 10.0 | 1.033 / 1.004 / 1.036 (6 processes x 7 passes) | not run | **PASS** (reported) |
| five symbols (random, 10 Mbp) | 21 | 5.9 / 3.5 / 6.6 % | 6.3 / 5.0 / 4.7 % | -10.2 .. 14.8 | 0.978 / 0.974 / 0.984 (3 processes) | 6.9 / 0.8 / 4.9 % | **PASS** (reported) |
| protein-like (20 symbols, 10 Mbp) | 21 | 19.0 / 17.0 / 16.4 % | 8.2 / 7.4 / 6.2 % | -1.4 .. 24.5 | 0.940 / 0.924 / 0.959 (3 processes) | 14.0 / 14.5 / 17.5 % | **not met, not claimed** |
| the in-memory tree against `e4a0b2da` (DNA 10 Mbp) | 15 per build | head 5.3 / 4.8 / 6.4 % slower | 4.5 / 3.8 / 5.1 % | -2.9 .. 10.9 | head 0.945 / 0.961 / 0.952 of `e4a0b2da`, `e57cdae0` 0.956 / 0.987 / 0.955 (5 processes) | | **not met; already in `e57cdae0`** |

**The spread between processes is 25 % on this machine and bimodal** (a group of processes near 700-730 thousand count/s and a group near 810-930, for the same variant; a pinned run gave 568-841), so a median of 41 processes has a standard error of about 4 % and the interval above is wide (-13 .. +13): **the verdict of the fixed gate by medians is sound as a verdict of "no gap of the size that was found (24-35 %)", not as a measurement of a 1-3 % effect**; the in-process A/B resolves 1-2 % and agrees (0-3 % at 10 Mbp). The protein-like text is the one on which the two instruments disagree most (19 against 6-8 %): the order of the processes matters most there.

**The cold open** (`Header` level, a JIT-warm process: a small container is opened first, then the page cache is dropped and the 10 Mbp container is opened, 10 processes): **0.451-0.582 ms, median 0.49 ms** (gate 2 ms: **PASS**); the warm open (25 opens in one process) takes 13-14 microseconds. **The container at 100 Mbp:** built by the builder in 24.7 s (scaled geometry, peak 103 MB), 1.325 GB (13.25 B/char; 4.0 B/char of it the child table, as before), `e8-verify --container` 11 of 11 checks pass in 4.4 s on 4 threads (the independent checker reads the tagged table through the codec of the header), the `Structure` level of the reader (`Checked`) opens it in 20.9 s; the process that holds the in-memory tree at 100 Mbp had 1.52 GB of resident memory, the one with the file-backed tree 1.41 GB (0.93 GB of it mapped file pages).

**What is left on the protein-like text (5 bits per symbol, up to 20 siblings per node), and what was isolated.** `Trusted` is as slow as `Checked` (0.967 / 0.917 / 0.918 in process): **it is not the checks**. The descent of a text over 20 symbols visits many siblings per node (the root has up to 20 children, and so have the nodes of the next levels), and each visit reads one symbol of the packed text through a 64-bit window with a byte behind it. The isolated experiment (a copy of the head in which that read is one 16-bit load and a shift, run in the same process as the head and the in-memory tree; 4 processes x 11 passes on the protein-like container, 3 on DNA): protein `Checked` relative to in memory **0.937 / 0.927 / 0.943 for the head, 0.950 / 0.953 / 0.956 with the 16-bit read** (+1.3 / +2.6 / +1.3 points), DNA 0.974 / 1.000 / 0.989 against 1.043 / 0.997 / 0.999 (not resolved). **It is a cause of 1-3 points of the 4-8 that are left, not the whole cause; the rest is not isolated** (the candidates that were not run: the packing of the pattern, built for every query that gets past the first levels, against a byte compare of a few symbols that is cheaper at five levels per query than at eleven; the per-sibling `SA[a]` and text reads that both trees do). It was **not shipped** (six lines; the sets and the audit had been run on the head, and a change of the hot loop is a re-run of all of them) and is the first item for 1b.9.

**Tests, in the order they were written.** Tests first, twice. `MappedSearchTests` against a throwing stub of the descent: **30 of 30 red** (`0176ba88`), green with `34ef5a3b`. For the second codec the tests and the stubs (the codec constants, the builder option, `ChildCodecFor` and `CheckChildCodec` that throw, a `MappedChild` that never reports the tagged codec, `IVerifySource.ChildTagged` that is null): **76 of 3,824 Esa default tests and 1 of 19 `ContainerVerifyTests` red** (`2ad34830`), green with `c940c097` except five existing tests that asserted what the builder wrote before (the codec of the CHILD entry, the `BUILD_INFO` text, the counted write calls of two builds, the pinned figures of a text with an overflow list, and the unknown codec of a required section, which was 2): changed to the new default, the reason in the commit message of each. The tests compare `MappedSearch` interval for interval with the in-memory index and with the readable reference (`EsaIndex.DescendMapped`) for every width of the packed text (1-8 bits), every representation of the LCP array, every pattern length up to two windows of the widest and the narrowest text and for a pattern of thousands of symbols, and **on damaged containers between guard pages in `Checked` mode** (random damage of the suffix array, LCP and child sections; every cell of the child table set to ten values; every entry of the LCP array set to eight values at the edges of its range): the outcome must be the one of the reference, an answer or `InvalidDataException`, and no read leaves the mapping. `SesaChildCodecTests` cover the header check, the stage (a text of 2^30 symbols is refused before any file is touched), the builder's choice, the `Structure` level of a tagged table (every damaged cell refused), a table under the other codec's id, and the bound of `NextLIndex`. `MappedSearchTests` has 34 cases and `SesaChildCodecTests` 21; the Esa default set is 3,829 tests (3,819 passed, 10 skipped).

**Harness audit (mutation).** 67 hand-made single-site mutants of the new and the changed code (`analysis/mutation/make_1b9a_mutants.py`: the dispatch, the checks of `Checked` mode, the window decode and the packed pattern, the LCP readers and the two child codecs of `MappedSearch`, the tagged arm of the mapped child table, `ChildTable.FromMapped`, `SesaFile`, `SesaHeaderReader`, `SectionLcpChildBuilder`, the builder, the `Structure` level of `SesaVerifier`, and the `e8-verify` adapter), each built in a separate git worktree and run against the unit tests of the new code and, for a survivor, against the budgeted harness of 1b.8 for 60 s at two workers (`run_1b9a_mutants.py`, `run_1b9a_audit.sh`, watchdog, analyzers off, no `StopOnError`): **67 listed, 67 run, 0 not run: 54 killed by the unit tests in round 1 (six as a hang or a host crash of the test process), 5 killed in round 2 after a test was added or the mutant re-written, 8 argued equivalent (`1b9a_equivalent.py`), 0 open. The budgeted harness killed none of the 13 mutants that survived the unit tests in round 1** (the unit tests of the new code are the net of this audit; the harness, at 60 seconds per mutant, added nothing to it). The survivors of round 1 and what became of them: **`M01`** did not compile (the swap of the access mode changed the type of a cell and of the loop at once): re-written, then killed by the damaged-file tests; **`M16`, `M17`** (a child "deeper than the text" is accepted, the stretch of an edge is not tested against the end of the text): a new test sets each LCP entry to eight edge values and compares with the reference (`AnLcpValueAtTheEdgeOfItsRange_InCheckedMode_EndsAsTheReferenceDoes`); **`M39`** (an escaped 16-bit LCP entry returned as the escape): the existing test covered an 8-bit overflow list and no 16-bit one, so `a^70000` (a 16-bit array with an overflow list, read to depths of 65,535 and more) was added to `EveryRepresentationOfTheLcpArray_EqualsTheMemoryIndex`; **`C04`** (the bound of the tagged `NextLIndex`): a direct test (`ACellOutsideTheChildTable_InCheckedMode_IsInvalidData_ForTheNextLIndexOfBothLayouts`); **eight argued equivalent** (`M02`, `M05`, `M13`, `M20`, `M24`, `M28`, `M30`, `M40`: a speed-only difference, a spare word, a test that the next one repeats, a symbol that is always equal, a zero that is already zero, a stack against a heap buffer, a second line of defence with the same exception).

**Sets that ran on the final code.** The production code of the final head is that of `c940c097`. On the final head: `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default in Debug **3,819 passed + 10 skipped**, Fuzz **39 of 39**, the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) **3,820 passed + 9 skipped**, and the 10-minute budget run of the 1b.8 harness (master seed 4, 600 s, 4 workers, head `39fc4739`: **117904 cases in 905 s (the test process: 907 s), 0 failures** (tiny 4790 cases, small 1394 cases, large 111 cases, xl 5 cases, corruption 111549 cases, concurrency 55 cases); a first run on `39fc4739` stopped at the 300 s watchdog of the xl band on a slow case (`akb`, 478,991 symbols), which finishes at both `e57cdae0` and the head when the xl lane runs alone (2 workers, 20 cases and 0 failures each, `rows_1b9a_fuzz_xl_compare.txt`): a slow case of the harness, not a defect of this package; the limit is now a parameter of `run_1b8_fuzz.sh` (`FUZZ_HANG_S`, 900 s for this run)); on `c940c097`, not repeated because the code and the tests of those sets are unchanged: Exhaustive **82 of 82** and `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **535 of 535**. Skipped: Persistent (its inputs are unchanged), Ukkonen, `Mcp.Core`, Vectors (`SuffixTree.Core` and `SuffixTree` untouched), the full solution.

**Not verified / limits (1b.9a; the full list is in PROGRESS-S1).** Nothing ran on macOS, Windows or arm64. The cross-process medians are noisy (25 % between processes, bimodal; a 95 % interval of about -13 .. +13 points at 41 processes); the in-process A/B is the precise instrument. The 100 Mbp figures use the scaled geometry of the builder. The protein-like text is not within 10 % and its cause is isolated only in part (1-3 points); the in-memory tree is 4-5 % slower than at `e4a0b2da` and the reader count is most of it. The throughput at four threads and the cold-cache query measurement were not measured. The `madvise` number is one experiment on one kernel. The audit mutants are single-site and hand-made, for the code of this package only. A text of 2^30 symbols or more with the tagged codec was tested on the functions, not on a container.

Orchestrator verification (2026-11-20, 1b.9a, not the implementer's numbers): the pushed head `cbc0fabc`, tree clean; the 12 commits since `e57cdae0` all start `ST-S1: 1b.9a ` with a space and have both trailers; the diff is **one new production file, `MappedSearch.cs` (333 lines), 11 edited production files in `SuffixTree.Esa` and 5 edited files of `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` (the CHILD codec of a container; 17 files, +452/-35 with the new file)**, tests, `analysis/**` (including a throw-away project `analysis/em/readbench/ReadBench.csproj`, which no solution or project references; it is not built by `Seqeron.sln`), ADR-003 (amendment: CHILD codec 2, the tagged layout), ADR-004, PROGRESS-S1 and `TEST_HIERARCHY.md`; no csproj of `src` or `apps`, no `wiki/`, ADR-001/002 change; a search of the added lines (`grep -a`) for the owner's name, e-mail, GitHub names, a model identifier and the sandbox account name found nothing. **Tests (policy of 2026-11-16, level 2, run by me on `cbc0fabc`):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default **3,819 passed + 10 skipped** (7 m 10 s Release; 3,763 + 10 before), Exhaustive **82 of 82** (17 m 36 s), Fuzz **39 of 39** (14 m 33 s), the **checked build** **3,820 + 9 skipped**, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 of 672**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **535 of 535** (16 m 33 s; the implementer ran it on an earlier head with identical `src` and `apps`), the Bench build clean. **Own checks at 10^8 on the new default (tagged child table, containers built by the harness variant `container`):** through the public `Open`: Header 48 ms (whole process), Sections 0.59 / 0.57 s, **Structure 21.6 / 20.6 s** (30.5 / 29.0 s with the packed table); **2,300 queries per text against my own oracle** (binary search over the E6 suffix array and the text, 1,000 present, 1,000 absent, 300 locate sets): **0 mismatches in `Checked` and in `Trusted` on `dna_100m` and `genome_100m`**; **`e8-verify --container` 11 of 11** on both and the old `--prefix` mode **11 of 11** (the edited verifier is unchanged for files); hostile inputs on the 10^6 container: **940 header flips refused at `Header`, 300 body flips at `Sections`, 96 sealed damages (CRC recomputed: SA swap, repeat, range, LCP value, CHILD cell, PSI cell, TEXT symbol, COUNTS) at `Structure`**, and a sealed SA swap in the 10^8 container is refused after 7 s; the cold `Open` in a fresh process takes 12.5-16.2 ms (JIT included, as in 1b.7f). **Throughput, my instrument** (10 Mbp, one tree per process, **21 processes per variant** with rotating order, warm, no heap limit; paired by round, file `Checked` divided by in memory): **DNA 1.33 count, 1.22 locate, 1.38 absent (the file-backed tree is the faster one)**, `Trusted` equal to `Checked` within the noise; **a uniform random text of 20 letters (12-mers present, 5-mers locate, 12-mers absent): 2.19 count, 2.08 locate, 1.37 absent**; the spread between processes is large (the in-memory DNA count 376-777 thousand per second). **This does not reproduce the implementer's protein-like result (file slower by 16-19 % across processes and 4-8 % in process) and the two instruments disagree on DNA as well (in process about 1.00); the cause of the disagreement is not explained** (a different text generator and different patterns; in process the two trees share caches; across processes the in-memory tree allocates its arrays differently); the gate of the brief (file within 10 % of memory on DNA) is met by every instrument, but the protein-like item is **not closed**, and the speed of the in-memory tree against `e4a0b2da` (reported 4-5 % slower, caused by the reader count of 1b.8) is **not closed**: both go to 1b.9b. **Mutation audit recounted** from `summary_1b9a.tsv`: 67 listed = **59 killed + 8 argued equivalent + 0 open, none unrun**, as stated; I re-ran **16 of the implementer's kills (10 at random, fixed seed, and the 6 killed by hang or host crash)**: all 16 killed (the hang `M08` and the crashes `M09`, `M11`, `M14`, `M34`, `M36` reproduce) and wrote **24 own mutants of `MappedSearch`** (the three LCP readers, both cell readers, the dispatch, the descent loop and the window arithmetic): **23 killed, 1 survived: `S03`, the bound of a 32-bit LCP value in `Checked` mode five entries wider is not noticed** (a test gap; the exact boundary of that check is not tested). **I did not re-run the 8 argued equivalents, G5, and nothing ran on macOS, Windows or arm64.** Recorded for 1b.9b: reconcile the two instruments on both protein generators, find out why the in-memory tree is slower than the specialised file-backed descent and whether the same descent (pinned arrays, the packed text) speeds the in-memory tree by 10 % or more, restore the in-memory speed (the reader count), and add the boundary test for the 32-bit LCP bound.

### 1b.9b: the two instruments reconciled, the in-memory tree restored, the test gaps closed and the reader count at four threads (2026-11-20)

The package of the owner rule of 2026-11-20 ("no debts before S2"; forecast 3-5 hours and 0.9-1.5 M tokens, cap 7 hours or 2.0 M tokens, stated by the orchestrator before the launch; gates G1-G6 and the abandon rule fixed before the work). It closes what 1b.9a left open: the protein-like text and the two instruments that disagreed, the in-memory tree 4-5 % slower than at `e4a0b2da`, the small test gaps of four verifications, the throughput at four threads, and the hygiene of the analysis files. **Production code: 5 existing files edited (+66 / -19), none added**: the facade takes no reader count for an in-memory tree and its `Dispose` releases nothing (`EsaSuffixTree.cs`), the hot arm of the LCP indexer is small again (`CompactLcp.cs`), the single-symbol read of the mapped descent is one 16-bit load (`MappedSearch.cs`), and two seams for tests (`NativeArena.GuardedCreated`, `SesaBuilder.CheckBuildInfoFits`); no csproj, no `apps/`, no ADR-001 / 002 / 003 change. **Result in one paragraph:** the two instruments do not disagree about the trees, they disagree about their own noise: in the cross-process instrument the throughput of a process falls into one of two groups about 1.4x apart, whatever the variant and the round, so the ratio of two medians of 21 processes takes any value from 0.86 to 1.10 for the same two trees and the same code (the same build against itself: 0.995 / 1.050 / 0.978), and its 95 % interval is +-25-35 %; the in-process instrument (+-3 %) puts the `Checked` tree at **0.91-1.06 of the in-memory tree on every text, count / locate / absent**, none more than 10 % slower (the realistic protein 0.945 / 0.970 / 0.971; at 100 Mbp the file-backed tree is the faster one, 1.09 / 1.00 / 1.07); the in-memory tree is **restored to 0.990-1.010 of `e4a0b2da`** (in process, 12 processes, DNA and realistic protein); the specialised descent is **not adopted** for it (-5 to +3 % against the generic one); the in-memory race is real (the test host crashes against the behaviour of `e4a0b2da`) and is fixed at its cause, not by a count; the reader count of the file-backed tree costs **7.6 % of a count of present 20-mers on DNA at four threads (above the 5 % gate: G3 is not met), 3.6 % on the realistic protein, 0-4 % on locate and absent**, and the in-memory tree no longer has one; all ten test gaps have a test (the audit below says which mutants each kills).

**The benchmark definition** (one, used by every number here; `analysis/em/readbench/README.md`, driver `run_1b9b_bench.sh`, table `summarize_1b9b.py`, rows `analysis/em/rows_1b9b_*.tsv`). Six texts of 10 Mbp: the S0 input `dna_10m` (4 symbols), a uniform random text over 5 symbols, a uniform random protein (20 letters), a **realistic protein** (Swiss-Prot composition, proteins of mean length 360, 35 % of the segments copies of an earlier stretch with 2-50 % substitutions and indels, 4 % low-complexity runs and tandem units), a natural-language-like text of 90 symbols (Zipf vocabulary, capitals, digits, punctuation) and uniform random bytes (256 symbols), made by `ReadBench gen` (SplitMix64, seed 20261120); patterns drawn from the text, the same for every variant: 200,000 present 20-mers (count), 20,000 present 8-mers (locate), 100,000 random 24-mers over the alphabet (absent). **(a) Cross-process:** one tree per process, JIT warm by time, 7 passes, the variants in a rotated order from round to round, warm page cache, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit` unset, 21 processes per variant; reported: the ratio of the medians over the processes, the median of the per-round ratios, a 95 % bootstrap interval of the first, and (exploratory, marked as such) the ratio inside each of the two throughput groups averaged. **(b) In process:** the in-memory, the `Checked` and the `Trusted` tree in one process, the passes in a rotated order, 5 processes of 9 passes; the median over the processes of the per-pass ratio, the range in brackets. Containers: the builder of the head, codec 2, real geometry.

**Reconciliation, text by text** (file-backed `Checked` / in-memory throughput; above 1 the file-backed tree is faster; count / locate / absent):

| Text | (a) cross-process, 21 processes: ratio of medians / paired / stratified (interval of the first) | (b) in process, 5 processes | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| DNA (4) | 0.917 / 0.878 / 0.920 (paired 0.943 / 0.954 / 0.979; stratified 0.947 / 0.965 / 0.931; intervals 0.77-1.11) | 0.964 / 0.961 / 0.932 (`Trusted` 1.023 / 0.993 / 0.972) | in process within 10 %; cross-process medians 8-12 % below 1, intervals contain 1, stratified 0.93-0.97: unresolved by that instrument |
| 5 symbols | 0.842 / 0.863 / 0.869 (0.882 / 0.922 / 0.893; 0.951 / 1.018 / 0.950; 0.77-1.14) | 0.975 / 0.959 / 0.969 (1.038 / 1.012 / 1.026) | in process within 10 %; cross-process medians 13-16 % below 1, intervals contain 1, stratified 0.95-1.02: unresolved |
| protein uniform (20) | 1.014 / 1.124 / 1.093 (0.958 / 0.956 / 0.956; 0.955 / 0.968 / 0.961; 0.78-1.26) | 0.996 / 0.918 / 0.908 (0.968 / 0.941 / 0.928) | within 10 % on both (the smallest in-process figure of the table, 0.908) |
| protein realistic (20) | 1.072 / 1.100 / 1.101 (1.026 / 1.034 / 0.997; 1.053 / 0.959 / 1.112; 0.91-1.15) | 0.945 / 0.970 / 0.971 (1.002 / 0.962 / 0.940) | within 10 % on both (the file-backed tree is the faster one in this cross-process run: the same noise) |
| text (90) | 0.860 / 0.871 / 0.945 (0.978 / 0.961 / 0.962; 0.959 / 0.965 / 0.984; 0.74-1.18) | 0.997 / 0.967 / 0.946 (1.009 / 0.977 / 0.957) | in process within 10 %; cross-process medians 13-14 % below 1 on count and locate, paired 0.96-0.98, interval contains 1: unresolved |
| bytes (256) | 1.008 / 0.936 / 0.953 (1.017 / 1.014 / 1.025; 1.044 / 1.042 / 1.025; 0.80-1.20) | 1.043 / 1.063 / 1.040 (1.009 / 1.013 / 1.013) | within 10 % on both; the file-backed tree is the faster one |

The cross-process rows of DNA and of the realistic protein are those of the final build; the other four are those of the build before this package (`433195b6`, 21 processes each), because the in-memory change of this package (+1-4 % in memory) is far below the resolution of that instrument. **Why they disagree, by isolated experiment.** (1) *Two groups of processes.* In the 21 processes of each variant the count throughput on DNA falls into 845-930 thousand per second (9 processes) and 1045-1290 (12) for the in-memory tree, and the same two groups for the file-backed tree; the group is a property of the process (not of the variant: the null experiment below; not of the time: the per-round ratios of the two variants run one after the other range over 0.66-1.50 on DNA and 0.67-1.45 on the other texts with no trend; not of the CPU: 1b.9a pinned processes and got the same spread); the cause of the groups is not isolated (the page placement of the process, the host). (2) *The noise floor, measured.* The same build run as two variants (the null experiment, DNA, 21 processes each): ratio of medians **0.995 / 1.050 / 0.978**, paired 1.015 / 1.039 / 1.009, bootstrap interval 0.74-1.39: the median of 21 processes lies at the edge of the two groups, so that one or two processes that change group move it by 5-10 %. The effects that 1b.9a and the orchestrator reported and that disagreed (DNA file / memory 0.5-3 % slower in 1b.9a, 1.33 in the orchestrator's runs; protein-like text 16-19 % slower in 1b.9a, 2.19 in the orchestrator's, here 1.014 on the uniform protein) are inside that floor, except the 2.19: the ratio between the two groups is 1.3-1.5, so no mix of the groups gives 2.19 on this machine; with the orchestrator's protocol the medians give 1.17-1.30 on the uniform protein here (next paragraph), the direction of their finding and not its size; their in-memory DNA throughput in that session was 599 thousand per second, 30 % below the lower group seen here (845-930), so that session ran on a slower or busier host; the rest of the difference is not reproduced and not isolated. (3) *The estimator that removes the groups.* Splitting the processes of both variants at the valley between the groups and taking the ratio of medians inside each group gives **0.93-1.11** for every text and metric (the stratified figures; 0.93-1.05 on all but one), the range of the in-process instrument; it is exploratory (it needs both groups in both variants). (4) *What does not explain it.* The in-memory tree searches a byte per symbol (`Prepared.Bytes`, 10 MB), not the 20 MB `string`; a pinned byte copy or a packed copy of the text in front of the specialised descent changes nothing or loses (the ablation below), so the text width is not the cause; the trees sharing one process (cache, heap, page tables) do not bias the in-process figure: it agrees with the stratified cross-process estimate within 3 points on every text. **The in-process verdict** for G2: no text has a tree more than 10 % slower than the other: the smallest `Checked` / memory figure is 0.908 (absent 24-mers of the uniform protein), the largest 1.063 (the 256-symbol text, where the file-backed tree is the faster one); `Trusted` is 0.93-1.04. The remaining 3-8 % of the file-backed tree on 4-90 symbols is the `Checked` checks (`Trusted` minus `Checked`: 2-5 points on the protein texts) and the packed-text decode; **the best software result is stated by the table**. **The protocol of the orchestrator's check of 1b.9a** (2,000 present 12-mers for count, 500 present 5-mers for locate, 2,000 absent 12-mers; uniform protein, 21 processes per variant, order rotated, no heap limit): ratio of medians **1.170 / 1.094 / 1.297** (the file-backed tree "faster", as the orchestrator found: 2.19 / 2.08 / 1.37), but paired by round **0.967 / 0.987 / 0.986**, stratified **0.953 / 0.980 / 0.965**, interval 0.70-1.35; in process, 6 processes: `Checked` / memory **0.984 / 1.019 / 1.018** on the uniform and **1.001 / 1.026 / 1.035** on the realistic protein. So the direction of the orchestrator's finding is reproduced by the medians and removed by pairing, by stratifying and by the in-process instrument; its size (2.19) was not reproduced (the largest median ratio seen here is 1.30).

**The single-symbol read (the protein-like item of 1b.9a).** The descent of 1b.9a read a symbol through the 64-bit window (an unaligned 8-byte load, a byte-order swap, a second load for the carry); a symbol has at most 8 bits and lies in two consecutive bytes, so it is now one 16-bit load and two shifts (the section ends with 64 bytes of padding). In process, `Checked` / memory on the realistic protein, 5 processes: **0.902 / 0.927 / 0.935 before, 0.945 / 0.970 / 0.971 after** (+4 points; the uniform protein, DNA and five symbols move by less than the noise, +-3 points). The packing of the pattern, the other item of 1b.9a, was not done (the pattern is packed only when a stretch has to be compared).

**The in-memory tree.** *The question and the test.* Does `Dispose` of an in-memory tree release anything that a reader in flight uses? The navigation pack pins the suffix array and the LCP base array of the index with GC handles and the suffix-link kernel and the navigator read them through pointers; `Dispose` freed the handles (1b.6: "Dispose releases the GC handle of the pack"), the reader count of 1b.8 only postponed that until no reader was inside. `FacadeDisposeRaceChildTests.ReaderThreads_RacingDispose_OfAnInMemoryTree_...` (in the child process of the 1b.8 race test: four readers walk suffix links, a collector thread forces compacting collections and fills the freed memory, the main thread disposes after a random 0-1.8 ms, 120 rounds) was written first: **against the behaviour of `e4a0b2da` (no count, `Dispose` releases the pack at once) the test host crashed in 3 of 3 runs** (a reader took a garbage value for a position and used it as an index into another pinned array); with the reader count (the head) and with the fix it passed in every run (2 runs at the head, 5 runs with the fix, no failure). A deterministic variant (hold the pointer, dispose, compact, read) was written and dropped: it never failed against the old behaviour (the arrays were not moved in 60 rounds), so it proved nothing. *The fix* is not a cheaper count: **`Dispose` of an in-memory tree sets the flag and releases nothing**; the pins go with the finalizer of the pack's `PinnedArray` (tested at its level), i.e. when no reader can reach the tree; a call checks the flag (one volatile read) and takes no count, and its scope still holds the tree for the length of the call. A call in flight completes with its answer, a call that starts later throws `ObjectDisposedException`, as before. One verified behaviour of 1b.6 changed: two tests that asserted the immediate release (`FacadeEsaDisposeTests.Dispose_AfterAPackWasBuilt_...`, `..._AfterTheNavigationSurfaceBuiltThePack_...`) now assert that it does not happen. The abandon rule did not fire. A tree over a container keeps the reader count unchanged. *The speed.* In process, in-memory trees side by side, throughput relative to `e4a0b2da` (count / locate / absent, medians of the processes):

| Text | Build | n | count / locate / absent |
|---|---|---|---|
| DNA (4) | `e4a0b2da` | 12 | 1.000 / 1.000 / 1.000 |
| DNA (4) | the head before this package (reader count for every tree) | 12 | 0.975 / 0.961 / 0.973 |
| DNA (4) | **this package** | 12 | **0.994 / 0.992 / 1.009** |
| realistic protein (20) | `e4a0b2da` | 12 | 1.000 / 1.000 / 1.000 |
| realistic protein (20) | the head before this package | 12 | 0.990 / 0.988 / 0.980 |
| realistic protein (20) | **this package** | 12 | **0.997 / 0.990 / 1.010** |

The reader count was most of the gap (the head before: 0.975 / 0.961 / 0.973 on DNA, 12 processes); the rest was the LCP indexer: its slow arm (the overflow list, the error) had grown with the mapped form of 1b.7f and the indexer was no longer inlined into the descent, so the slow arm is now a function of its own (`CompactLcp.EscapedValue`, `ThrowRankOutside`): +1.5 / +1 / 0 points on DNA in a paired run of 8 processes (0.981 / 0.982 / 0.998 before, 0.996 / 0.993 / 0.995 after, against `e4a0b2da`). **The gate (median at least 0.99 on count, locate and absent, DNA and realistic protein) is met by the 12-process run** (the lowest figure is 0.990, realistic protein locate); two earlier runs of 8 processes gave 0.994 / 0.994 / 0.980 (DNA) and 1.008 / 0.987 / 1.000 (realistic protein): the per-process spread of one ratio is +-3 points, so the median of 8-12 processes resolves about 1-1.5 points, and the gate figure is at the resolution of the instrument.

**Item 2(b): the specialised descent for the in-memory tree, measured and not adopted.** A prototype (harness only, `-p:Experiments=true`: `ix-mem-b`, `ix-mem-p`) runs the lean descent of 1b.9a over the pinned arrays of an in-memory index (suffix array, tagged child table, LCP base array; nothing checked): with a byte text (a pinned padded copy, n bytes) and with a packed text copy (n x bits / 8 bytes). Engine level, in process, 3 processes of 9 passes, throughput relative to the generic descent of the in-memory index (count / locate / absent):

| Text | byte text | packed text copy | the generic descent relative to the facade |
|---|---|---|---|
| DNA (4) | 1.004 / 0.978 / 0.952 | 0.947 / 0.944 / 0.918 | 1.008 / 1.023 / 1.006 |
| 5 symbols | 1.010 / 1.047 / 1.010 | 0.966 / 0.991 / 0.950 | 1.019 / 1.021 / 1.030 |
| protein uniform (20) | 0.998 / 0.983 / 1.015 | 0.946 / 0.949 / 0.933 | 1.001 / 1.023 / 1.015 |
| protein realistic (20) | 1.006 / 1.004 / 1.030 | 0.956 / 0.961 / 0.948 | 1.003 / 1.024 / 1.000 |
| text (90) | 1.032 / 1.028 / 1.000 | 0.941 / 0.961 / 0.870 | 1.013 / 1.018 / 1.017 |
| bytes (256) | 1.014 / 1.001 / 1.054 | 0.978 / 0.961 / 0.990 | 0.998 / 0.998 / 0.986 |

The rule (adopt for a gain of 10 % or more on count and absent for every text) is **not met on any text**: the byte-text descent is -0.2 to +3.2 points on count and -4.8 to +5.4 on absent (the best case, the 256-symbol text, is +1.4 / +5.4), and the packed copy loses 2-13 points almost everywhere. The reason is the opposite of the file's: the generic descent is already compiled per child layout and symbol width, reads a byte text and a tagged table, and the indexer is small again; what 1b.9a removed from the file-backed path (views, branches) does not exist in the in-memory path. No production code was written for it; the memory cost that would have been paid (n x b / 8 bytes, 2.5 MB at 10 Mbp for DNA, 10 MB for bytes) is not paid.

**Test gaps** (each closed by a test that fails against the named mutant and passes on the head; the audit below ran them):

| Item | Test | Mutants it kills (`make_1b9b_mutants.py`) |
|---|---|---|
| 1b.7e `B102`: the guard option reaches the BWT pass | `SesaGap1b9bTests.TheGuardOption_ReachesThePassOfTheSuffixArray_TheLcpAndChildPass_TheBwtPass_AndThePsiPass`: a build with a hook at every boundary reads the new counter `NativeArena.GuardedCreated`, which must move in each of the four passes (control: no option, no move) | `1b9b_B01` (the BWT pass gets no guards: the original `B102`), `B02` (PSI), `B07` (SA), `B08` (LCP and child), `A01`-`A03` (the counter) |
