**Reading (the numbers are the cells above).**

* **PH1 is unaffected, as in E7:** 1.7 s (`dna`) and 2.2-2.6 s (`genome`) at every effective cache from 0.4 to 1 (unlimited 1.66 s and 2.20 s); **the floor is PH2 / PH3**, the part that reads the rank file and the text at random: 12.9 s unlimited, 27.8 s [21.6-37.9] at an effective 0.71, 114 s [103-117] at 0.50 (`dna`); 13.5 s, 90 s, 288 s, 542 s at 1.0, 0.80, 0.69, 0.55 (`genome`). PH4 and later (the limit is raised there) take 10-15 s in every cell (10.5 s unlimited).
* **dna_100m, default parameters** (unlimited **25.7 s** [25.3-26.9], three runs): effective **0.84 and 0.79: 1.2x; 0.74: 1.5x; 0.71 [0.59-0.72]: 1.73x [1.40-2.12]** (three runs); 0.60: 3.8x; **0.50 [0.47-0.56]: 5.05x [4.51-5.23]** (130 s, three runs); 0.40-0.42: 5.4-5.7x (the plateau of E7: the device reads the 38 MB in pieces of 4 KiB). The 3x line is at about **0.6** (0.59 gave 2.1x and 0.60 gave 3.8x: the cliff is steep and noisy). **Lean** (the E7 settings): unlimited 30.7 s; 0.69: 1.37x; 0.60: 2.5x; **0.50: 4.6x**; the prototype's cells of E7 for the same settings: 0.72: 1.23x, 0.59: 2.2x, **0.49: 3.6x** (three runs), so **the production chain is 1.3-1.45x slower than the prototype under the same cache pressure** (142 s against 98 s at 0.50) of which 12 % is there unlimited (30.7 s against 27.3 s, the same settings) and the rest appears under pressure; not investigated (the effective cache is a measure of the text and the rank file only; the production chain also keeps the SA of the container, the position files of PH3 and the BWT in the same group).
* **genome_100m, default parameters** (unlimited **28.7 s** [28.2-29.3]): **0.80: 3.7x; 0.69: 10.7x; 0.55: 19.4x** (557 s, the longest cell, just under its 600 s watchdog). The prototype's E7 cells: 0.81: 2.8x, 0.65-0.67: 5.2-5.3x, 0.51: 8.5x. The text with ties needs an effective cache of about **0.85** for 3x, as in E7 (0.8-0.85), and the production chain takes 557 s where the prototype took 356 s (0.55 against 0.51) and 306 s against about 220 s at 0.69 against 0.65-0.67. Block-layer reads: **340 GB for a 50 MB working set at 0.55** (453 GB at 0.51 in E7).
* **The output side does not thrash:** the pages that the process dirties are **1.02-1.05 times the bytes that it writes in every cell** (a page that is flushed and written again would count twice; the limit makes the builder write its scratch back before it deletes it, so the device bytes rise from 0.55 to 0.73-0.94 of the logical bytes, which is the cost of the limit and not a re-dirtying), and the E5 rule for layout A (write amplification below 2) holds in every cell that finished.
* **The constant limit (variant C), the literal "the whole pipeline in one limit":** **130 MB (effective 0.84): 56.7 s, 2.2x** (PH4 and later 38 s against 10.5: the 12 B/char of scratch of PH4 has to be written back inside the group); **95 MB (0.68 over PH3) and 84 MB (0.49): killed by the cgroup's OOM killer after PH3** (95 MB at 227 s: the group holds 86 MB of anonymous memory and 5 MB of cache; the unlimited build's RssAnon peak in PH4 / PH5 is 146 MB). E7's C65 did not finish in 15 minutes; here the default parameters need a group of about 150 MB plus the cache for PH4 and later, so **the E7 finding stands for the production chain: a constant limit below about 130 MB (the anonymous memory of PH4-PH6 plus a cache for the scratch) does not work, whatever the effective cache of PH3**; with the lean settings (48 MB anonymous) the same limit would be 70-80 MB (not run).
* **Whether the rules of E7 fire.** The gate C (slowdown at most 3x at an effective 0.5x on `dna_100m`): **not met, 5.05x** [4.51-5.23] (default) and 4.6x (lean); E7's prototype 3.6x (also not met). **The abandon rule of 4.6 / 5 ("the build needs a page cache larger than 2x text + rank to stay within 3x") does not fire**: the 3x line is at about 0.6 (`dna`) and above 0.8 (genome-like: 3.7x at 0.80; E7 put it at 0.8-0.85) of text + rank. **The rule of E3 ("above 5x the design needs a minimum page cache: document it or drop to F1") is reached below an effective 0.55 (`dna`: 5.05x at 0.50) and below 0.7 (genome-like: 10.7x at 0.69)**; the owner's decision of 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-16 documented the floor (about 1x text + rank, F1 stays the fallback) and **these numbers are its evidence for the production chain: above an effective 0.8 the chain runs at 1.2x (`dna`) and 3.7x (genome-like), above 0.95 at the unlimited speed; between 0.6 and 0.8 it is 1.5-3.8x on `dna` and 4-11x on genome-like text; below 0.55 it is 5x or more**. **Thrash:** the read side thrashes below those lines (35-340 GB of device reads), the write side does not (amplification 1.03), and a constant limit collapses (OOM) as E7's C65 did.
* **RssAnon** in the limited cells is 50-110 MB (default: 83-110 MB against 146 MB unlimited; the difference is not explained here), 44-56 MB lean, **never above the gate of 320 MB**; the single-process builds at the real geometry above (262-303 MB) are the gate's figures.

**Re-run on the pushed head `116e015c`** (the production code is that of `a7207055`; the Bench application was built from the head into a directory of its own; `rows_1b9d_verify_*.tsv`, the same scripts, one run or round of each): the full volume: **both sparse builds exit with 135 and leave two entries behind; the reserved build without PSI exits with 0 and its container has the SHA-256 of the one built on the ordinary disk (`373fc2a3741e44a8`); the reserved build with PSI exits with 0** (the competitor lost the race for the freed blocks this time), nothing left in any cell; the growth alone: `zero` costs +0.094 s (cold) and +0.096 s (warm) at 100 MiB and +0.303 s and +0.308 s at 300 MiB (first run: +0.101, +0.097, +0.315, +0.283), `worst` -0.016 to +0.011 s, the device bytes equal; the in-process A/B at 10^7, six warm pairs: `dna10` (the null) **1.008 [0.942-1.212]**, `u32_10` **0.981 [0.938-1.169]**, 0.0052 s of the build in the reservation (first run: 0.0050 s); the cache cells: `dna100` unlimited **25.8 s** (25.7 s), RssAnon 146 MB, and the limit that gave an effective 0.47-0.56 in three runs (84 MB) gave **0.62 and 103.9 s, 4.0x** (the same knee: 0.60 gave 3.8x), the container identical; `u32_100` single process at the real geometry **18.4 s and 295 MB RssAnon** (19.5 s and 302 MB). The numbers above are the first runs; the re-run does not move any conclusion.

**Defects found.** *In the code of the build, closed here:* (1) the sparse growth of the container (the package's subject); (2) the target of `Dist.Scatter` was created sparse when `Files` was null (a signal on a full volume; `BuildIsa` and `BuildPhi` have no caller in `src`, `RankInverter` passed `Files`, so the build itself was not exposed; found by the enumeration; a test of 1b.9c, `Y02`, had pinned the sparse default and is changed to pin the reservation and the sparse mode on request). *Outside the package's scope, reported, not touched:* (3) `MappedFileStorageProvider` of `SuffixTree.Persistent` creates and grows its file with `SetLength` and maps it for writing (the same signal on a full volume, the persistent form of the in-memory tree); (4) `FileStreamOptions.PreallocationSize` is **silently ignored** by the BCL on a file system without `fallocate` (so `ReservationOutcome.Preallocated` means "asked", as 1b.7b said, and there the container is sparse; the E8 runs on the owner's Mac were Linux containers; which file system their work directory was on is not recorded here, and a bind mount from the host is another file system that was not tried). *In the tests and tooling (mine):* (5) the first adaptation of the E7 runner raised the limit at once, because the production build creates the directories `ph1` and `ph3` before PH1 while the prototype created `ph3` at the start of PH3: those four cells (`rows_1b9d_cache_discarded_limit_raised_at_start.tsv`) are discarded and re-run with the raise tied to the life of `ranks.bin`; (6) the first experiment of the full volume (a ballast written once) did not crash the sparse build and proved nothing: B3 frees 8n bytes of scratch while it writes the tail; a thread that takes what is freed is what shows the window; (7) an existing test, `Grow_ToMoreThan2To32Bytes_IsSparse_AndTheTailIsWritable`, would have written 5 GB of zeros with the new default (it passed, slowly, on the first run of the External tests): it now asks for the sparse mode; (8) `summarize_1b9d_cache.py` first computed the slowdown against the last unlimited cell of an input, whatever its configuration; it now pairs a cell with the unlimited cell of the same configuration.

**Test sets run (policy of PROGRESS-S1, level 2: the affected set of `src/SuffixTree.Esa` and the Bench application), on the head `116e015c`, one after the other with nothing else running, logs in the scratch directory:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default in Debug (`dotnet test` in the project directory): **3,989 passed + 10 skipped** (3,963 + 10 at 1b.9c: +26 new tests), 12 m 16 s; Exhaustive (`dotnet test -c Release --filter "Category=Exhaustive"`): **82 of 82**, 17 m 26 s; Fuzz (`dotnet test -c Release --filter "Category=Fuzz"`): **39 of 39**, 14 m 50 s (both because `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed); the checked build (`ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true dotnet test -p:EsaBounds=true`, Debug; run although the new lines carry no `ESA_BOUNDS` conditional, because `Dist.cs` holds code that the checked build compiles differently): **3,990 passed + 9 skipped** (3,964 + 9 at 1b.9c), 12 m 3 s; `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (`dotnet test`): **672 of 672**, 30 s; `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (`dotnet test -c Release`): **535 of 535**, 16 m 14 s; the Bench application builds clean (`dotnet build apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench.csproj -c Release --no-incremental`: 0 warnings, 0 errors). **Not run, with the rule:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the MCP tests (their projects and what they test did not change). The tests that mount a tmpfs need root (`mount`); without it they are ignored, the injected ones need nothing.

**Not verified / limits (1b.9d).** (a) Anything on macOS, Windows or arm64: nothing ran there; `SystemReservationSource.CanPreallocate` is true on Linux only, so elsewhere the creation of every output is still a free-space check and a sparse file (the window of the whole build, not only of the tail), while the zero fill of `Grow` is portable code that ran on Linux only. (b) File systems: ext4 (the sandbox's volume) and tmpfs. The BCL ignores `fallocate` silently on a file system without it (the container is then sparse and `Preallocated` means "asked"); a copy-on-write file system (btrfs, ZFS, APFS) can need new blocks at writeback even for a reserved range; a bind mount of the host's file system under Docker Desktop and network or FUSE volumes were not tried. (c) The competitor for the disk is a thread of the same process and a fill at a hook or a trace point; no second process and no user quota (a quota is the same `ENOSPC` / `EDQUOT` of the `pwrite` and of the `fallocate`, not tried). (d) The window at a real size was shown on a tmpfs (RAM-backed) with one text of 10^8 symbols; a device that fills during a build of 3x10^9 was not run. (e) Everything at 3x10^9 is an ESTIMATE by size: the zeros of a 9 GB growth (an LCP of four bytes at 3x10^9) about 9-10 s, plus a similar time on the mapped stores that follow (about 1 % of a 15-minute build), and W's extra disk of 9 GB. (f) The A/B at 10^8 does not resolve 1-2 % (single pairs vary by 5-7 %, also in the null cell): the cost is bounded from the inside by `reserve_s` and by the micro measurement, and the whole-build ratios are consistent with it (0.99 [0.93-1.11] with six balanced cold pairs for the worst case), not a proof of a difference of 1 %. (g) The cache runs: one machine, cgroup v1, one run per cell except where the table says three, the effective cache measures the text and the rank file only, and the production chain's extra slowness against the prototype under the same cache pressure is measured, not explained. (h) `FullDisk1b9dTests` and the real-volume tests of `Reservation1b9dTests` take 40-60 seconds together in the default set (a mount per build). (i) Single-site mutants, hand-made, by the implementer who wrote the code.

**Open points handed to the owner.** (1) **Z (the zero fill of the added range, implemented) or W (the worst case reserved at the creation and cut when the width is known)?** W costs no time and no device bytes (+0.000-0.039 s at 300 MiB, inside the spread) and 3 (n + 1) bytes of disk in every build (0.3 GB at 10^8, 9 GB at 3x10^9: 12 % of the pre-flight); Z costs 1 s per GiB of growth (0, 0.011, 0.18 and 0.3 s at 10^8 for `dna`, `genome`, `u16`, `u32`) and no disk. Z is in the code because the common case pays nothing; for a deployment that builds very repetitive text on a tight disk W is the better trade, and the two do not exclude each other (W could be chosen by an option). (2) **A platform without `fallocate` (native macOS and Windows) or a file system that ignores it:** reserve every output by writing zeros (about 1 s per GiB; at 3x10^9 roughly 40-60 s on this machine's disk for the container and the scratch outputs, an ESTIMATE) or keep the window and document it. Nothing ran there, so it is not built. (3) **`MappedFileStorageProvider` of `SuffixTree.Persistent`** has the same window (its own package; a full disk while the persistent tree grows is a signal). (4) **The production chain is 1.3-1.45x slower than the E7 prototype under the same cache pressure on `dna_100m` and 1.4-1.6x on the genome-like text (557 s against 356 s at about 0.5), and the 3x line is at about 0.6 (`dna`) and above 0.8 (genome-like)**; the documented floor (about 1x text + rank) stands and the abandon rule of 4.6 does not fire; whether to look for the cause (the text sits in the container next to the SA that is written through windows, the position files of PH3, the larger anonymous memory) is the owner's decision.

**Cost of 1b.9d (wall clock by the machine clock; tokens are an estimate).** about **4.1 hours** of wall clock by the machine clock (00:50 UTC to about 04:55 UTC on 2026-11-21), against a forecast of 3-5 hours and a cap of 7 hours (80 % = 5.6 hours, not reached); **no context compaction and no container restart**; every result, test and run was committed and pushed as it was made (six commits before the documents); tokens: **about 1.2 M by my own estimate** (I cannot read the counter; the forecast was 0.9-1.5 M, the cap 2.0 M, the 80 % mark 1.6 M was not reached). Where the time went: reading the code, the ADR and the E7 scripts, the enumeration of the write sites and the design (00:50 to 01:20); tests first, the code and the first red / green runs (01:20 to 01:40); the measurements (01:40 to 02:50: the growth alone, the A/B at 10^7 and 10^8, the cache cells, the full volume; the cache cells took about 50 minutes of machine time, inside the cap of one hour that the brief gave); the mutation audit with three worktrees (02:50 to 03:23, two passes); the affected set (03:24 to 04:40, 76 minutes); the re-run of the figures on the pushed head and the documents (04:40 to 04:55).

## 6. Honest limitations and open questions

What I could **not** verify:
* The primary evidence for pSAscan (CPM 2015) and fSAIS (ALENEX 2017) could not be opened (author-site PDFs returned 404); their per-dataset tables are unread, numbers come from the README, the ESA 2016 Table 4 and the thesis summary. B2ST, DiGeST, TDD, Trellis, WaveFront primary papers, Flick-Aluru, Fischer-Kurpicz 2019, the JEA 2008 pDC3 journal version (I read the ALENEX 2005 version), the ALENEX 2013 eSAIS paper (I read the thesis chapter), BCR experiments, nSAIS and the 2020 EM paper were not read; PFP only as a search record.
* Nothing was run (the machine was busy); every number about B-DCS is an estimate (E). The literature numbers come from 2005-2017 hardware, 8-bit symbols, mostly one input family each.
* **B-DCS in external memory is an extrapolation.** Karkkainen's paper measured 1 GB on one core with the DCS in RAM; the EM bucketing is only a sketch in Haag et al. section 5; the recursion that builds the DCS and the file-resident rank lookups are untested. The cost of the DCS comparator on satellite-rich genomes (T2T has about 60 Mbp of alpha satellites) is unknown; E1/E2 exist to measure it.
* The difference-cover table for X = 512..4096 (size, offsets `l(a,b)`) is not verified; known constructions give |D| about sqrt(1.5 X).
* The effect of the page cache is the main hardware dependence: the text and the rank file are read randomly (about 0.45-0.55 B/char for 2-bit DNA, 1.2-1.3 B/char for bytes).
* Whether sliding mapped windows keep dirty pages low on this kernel, and whether the cgroup memory controller can be used by the prototype user, are untested.
* A WaveFront patent exists (US8914415B2, U); not relevant to the recommendation but relevant if the ERa/WaveFront family is ever considered.
* The ESA 2016 numbers are for reads (1000 Genomes), not an assembled genome, and for 512 GiB with 120 GiB RAM (n/M = 4.3); my scaling to n/M = 12 is linear in n/M (E).

Open questions for the owner:
* **Q1** Are scratch files allowed (about 5-12 B/char peak, bounded by `S_max`, on the output volume) and is the DCS rank file (0.04-0.14 entries per char, i.e. 0.2-0.6 B/char) acceptable as "index-derived scratch"? A strictly scratch-free build is only possible with a non-robust algorithm.
* **Q2** Is the 256 MB cap on anonymous memory including the .NET runtime baseline and thread stacks (assumed yes)?
* **Q3** DNA with N and IUPAC symbols: sigma is 5-16, not 4. Pack as b = 3-4 bits (key = 16-21 symbols), or 2 bits plus an exception list for N runs? Are both strands (n = 6.2x10^9, `POS_WIDTH` 8 or 5) in scope?
* **Q4** Is a design that reads the text and the rank file randomly through the OS page cache acceptable, and what is the minimum RAM of the target machine?
* **Q5** Must the output be byte-identical for any thread count and M (the design provides it)?
* **Q6** For n <= N_base (16 M symbols) is "in-RAM SA-IS, then stream the sections" acceptable (working memory is bounded by N_base)?
* **Q7** Priority: SA+LCP+child only (Core), or the pack (Psi + RMQ) in the same build? ISA for `RankOf`/`ExtendLeft`: needed in Core?
* **Q8** Disk for the 1-3 Gbp gates: 40 GB free allows 1 Gbp unbounded, 60 GB allows 3 Gbp; otherwise the largest local run is 500 Mbp and 3 Gbp stays an extrapolation.
* **Q9** Licence policy: GPL sources (eSAIS, Bowtie 2) are read as documentation only, MIT sources (pSAscan, fSAIS) are used as read-only references and nothing is copied (PROCESS.md rule 1 extended to all third-party code).

## 6a. Owner answers to Q1-Q4 (2026-11-04) and the constraints that follow

* **Q1 scratch files: allowed, "without fanaticism"** - the build must not be slowed down badly and must not fill the disk with temporary files. Constraints: scratch lives on the output volume, is bounded by `S_max`, is released stage by stage (a scratch file is deleted as soon as its consumer finished), and the prototype gates E1-E3 report **peak scratch (B/char) and total I/O (B/char)** next to wall time. Proposed acceptance (owner to confirm): peak scratch <= 8 B/char and total I/O <= 100 B/char; if a design needs more, report it instead of silently exceeding.
* **Q2: the 256 MB cap includes the .NET runtime baseline and thread stacks.** Measured as peak anonymous memory (`RssAnon`, plus a cgroup limit when the prototype user may create one). Mapped-file pages are not counted.
* **Q3: text codec = b bits per symbol, 4 bits for DNA with N/IUPAC, with a 2-bit fast path - and it must be extensible without rewriting.** Requirements: the TEXT section header records `codec` and `bits_per_symbol` (the ADR-003 codec field is used, not replaced); every stage takes `b` as a parameter (the 64-bit bucket key holds `64/b` symbols; splitter, bucket and merge logic never assume sigma = 4); no file layout depends on `b`; unit tests are parameterised over b in {2, 3, 4, 8} from the first prototype (small inputs); widening to 8-bit bytes or 16-bit UTF-16 is a **new codec value, not a format change**. The 2-bit path is chosen when the alphabet scan finds sigma <= 4, b = 4 when sigma <= 16 (A, C, G, T, N and IUPAC). An exception-list codec for long N runs is a possible later codec id, not built now. Both strands are out of scope here (S3, query side). **Amended on 2026-11-18 (section 6c): the width is `b = ceil(log2 sigma)`, i.e. 3 bits for sigma 5-8 (the rule of the prototype and of every verified file); 4 bits only from sigma 9.**
* **Q4: dependence on the OS page cache for the random reads is acceptable.** No minimum RAM for the target machine is fixed; gate E3 states how throughput degrades with the available page cache.
* Accepted by default (the owner did not object): **Q5** byte-identical output for any thread count and `M`; **Q6** in-RAM SA-IS as the base case for n <= N_base (16 M symbols, memory bounded by N_base, independent of n); **Q7** Core first (SA + LCP + child table), the navigation pack (Psi + RMQ) afterwards; **Q8** local runs up to what the free disk allows (about 22 GB free now, so roughly 100-200 Mbp with scratch), 3 Gbp as a hand-off package for a machine with about 60 GB; **Q9** GPL sources (eSAIS, Bowtie 2) are read as documentation only, MIT sources (pSAscan, fSAIS) as read-only references, nothing is copied.

## 6b. Owner decision 2026-11-09 (after the measured prototypes E0-E3, section 5a)

* **B-DCS is the construction algorithm of the persistent SESA builder; F1 (SAscan family, section 4.6) stays the fallback.** The recommendation text of section 4 stands unchanged.
* **Page-cache floor fixed as a documented requirement:** the build needs an effective page cache of about **1x (text + DCS rank file)** for the file pages it reads at random. Above it the build degrades smoothly (measured: at most 2.4x on random DNA down to 1.1x; 1.4-1.7x at the nominal 0.5x). Below it the output is still correct and identical, but tie-rich genomic text can run **10x or more slower** (measured on 100 Mbp genome-like text: 11-14x at an effective cache of 0.74-0.84x, one cache-limited run reproduced by the orchestrator: 161 s against 14.4 s, 166 GB of block-layer reads). Size estimate for 3x10^9 symbols (D: 0.25-0.375 B/char text + 0.129 B/char rank file = 0.38-0.50 B/char): about **1.1-1.5 GB** of cache. This is an estimate, not a measurement above 100 Mbp. Consistent with Q4 (page-cache dependence is acceptable; no minimum RAM of the target machine is fixed - this floor is the documented figure for a good build time, not a refusal rule).
* **Switch to F1** if E4 (PH1, the DCS rank file built in external memory) fails its gate, or if the floor turns out to be unacceptable on the real target machine.
* **Not authorised by this decision:** E4-E9 (external-memory PH1, PH4-PH7, 500 Mbp, the true 0.5x cache test) need a separate yes; the container writer 1b.7 and anything that assumes the persistent build stay blocked until then (**the yes was given on 2026-11-11, see section 6c**). S1b packages that do not depend on the build (1b.3-1b.6, the in-memory ESA) are unaffected.
* Open suggestion (not decided): the builder prints the floor (text bytes + rank bytes) before it starts so that it can be compared with the memory available.

## 6c. Owner decision 2026-11-11 (the separate yes that section 6b asked for)

* **Authorised:** E4-E9 as listed in 6b (external-memory PH1, PH4-PH7, 500 Mbp, the true 0.5x cache test), **and then** the container writer 1b.7. Order: E4-E9 first, 1b.7 after them (answer to "1b.7 after 1b.6": "Yes: E4-E9, then 1b.7"). 1b.6 (facade) is done and verified.
* **How it runs:** one agent at a time, no parallel sessions; before each launch the orchestrator states cost and time to the owner ("Decide before spending"); each prototype is judged against the gate of section 5, and a failed gate ends in the switch to F1 of 6b, not in silent tuning. 1b.7 is split as in the PROGRESS-S1 row of this ADR's table of consequences for other documents (1b.7a-1b.7e); the prototype gates precede 1b.7c-d.
* **Label slip in 6b, resolved:** 6b calls the external-memory PH1 gate "E4", but the table of section 5 defines E4 as PH4 (chunked Phi/PLCP). The table stands (E4 = PH4, E5 = PH5, E6 = end to end, E7 = scaling, E8 = 1-3 Gbp, E9 = PH6 Psi); the external-memory PH1 prototype (the DCS rank file built with bounded RAM, where E3 built it in RAM and mapped it) has no row in the table. Its name and gate are put to the owner in the launch statement before it runs; it is the first launch because 6b makes F1 the consequence of its failure.
* **Not covered by the 6b list:** E8 (1 Gbp bounded-scratch, 3 Gbp). The largest run in the list is 500 Mbp, which is also the largest that fits the local disk (about 22 GB free). E8 stays a hand-off package for a machine with about 60 GB (Q8), not a blocked item.
* **Owner decisions 2026-11-13, after E3b-E5 (the orchestrator put four design choices; the owner answered):** (1) **Child table layout A** (CHILD 4 B/char, one pass over the LCP); layout F (PSV + NSV, 8 B/char, a second pass) stays built and tested as a fallback, not as the plan. (2) **Child codec Packed** (4 B/char, texts below 2^32 - 2 symbols, so the 3x10^9 goal fits; the Tagged codec holds ranks in 30 bits and ends at 2^30 - 1). The cost of reading through Packed (LCP comparisons per child step, which Tagged avoids by its kind bits) is **not yet measured**; it is measured in 1b.7 with the reader, and if it is large the choice is reopened with the numbers (Tagged below 1 Gbp, Packed above). In E5, Packed built within +-10 % of Tagged in time, with equal windows, dirty pages and amplification. (3) **PH4 in base mode** (12.0 B/char of scratch, no dependence on a page cache; the bounded mode, 5-8 B/char with a 4 B/char PLCP file that must stay cached, is not used). (4) **No run-length encoding of arithmetic progressions on the stack.** Recorded limitation: a text with a single symbol repeated for hundreds of millions of positions needs 16 B x (max LCP + 1) of spill on disk (about 48 GB for a^n at 3x10^9, ESTIMATE); genome-like and random texts spill nothing, a 20 M N run 315 MB. (5) **E6 authorised** with these settings (layout A, Packed, PH4 base, fused through the B3 hook), forecast 2.5-4 h and 0.7-1.0 M tokens (+-50 %, stated before the launch). The time clause of G3 of E5 (PH5 <= PH4) stays recorded as not met on chains and for layout F; it is not re-argued here.
* **Owner decisions 2026-11-16, after E7 and E9 (the orchestrator put the questions, the owner answered):** (1) **No switch to F1** (SAscan / pSAscan): the series-A abandon rule of E7 (slope 34-36 MB per doubling at fixed parameters; in the real regime, series B, 9.5-10.4 MB) and the cache gate (3.6-3.9x against 3x at an effective 0.5x) do not lead to F1; B-DCS stays the plan. (2) **PH4 base mode only in 1b.7c** (as decided on 2026-11-13); the bounded mode may become a separate package after E8, only if E8 shows it is needed (E4: the bounded mode needs its 4 B/char PLCP file in the page cache and has a cliff). (3) **Minimum machine, written down as a requirement** (ESTIMATES for 3x10^9, not measured above 500 Mbp): page cache of about 1x (text + rank file) = 1.1-1.5 GB; disk 60-70 GB with fast sequential write (scratch about 36 GB, outputs about 28-31 GB); **how much page cache PH4's 12 B/char of scratch needs beyond the 1x is not known until E8** (E7: the whole pipeline in one constant limit did not finish below an effective 0.7 of text + rank). (4) **Test policy for 1b.7a-e** (three levels, the text is in PROGRESS-S1 'Owner decisions 2026-11-16'). (5) **E8 package:** a Linux container image (x86-64 and arm64) plus scripts for the owner's Mac (Docker Desktop), written after E9; the orchestrator's session has no Docker daemon, so the image cannot be built or tested there (Dockerfile and scripts are delivered unverified on Mac / arm64; timings of arm64 and x86 are not comparable); cost and time are stated before the launch. (6) **E9 authorised** (done, section 5a). Still open: N_base of the in-RAM base case (16 Mi symbols, about 181 MB, or 4 Mi, about 64 MB) before 1b.7.
* **Owner decisions 2026-11-18, after the E8 run on the owner's Mac (section 5a):** (1) **A1 is relaxed for the reference parameters: the anonymous working set of the persistent builder is bounded by about 320 MB** (measured plateau at 3x10^9 symbols with four threads: 309.7 and 309.2 MB, set by the arena caps 160 + 64 MiB, the managed heap limit 96 MiB and the runtime; 172 MB at 1 Gbp and 226 MB at 2.2 Gbp, where the scaled parameters are smaller). The gate G2 of the builder (1b.7c onward) is **RssAnon at most 320 MB at every n with the default parameters and four threads**; the caps of E6 stay as they are (no tuning package); above 3x10^9 the arenas stay at their caps, so the bound is expected to hold (ESTIMATE, not measured above 3 Gbp). (2) **N_base = 16 Mi symbols** (16,777,216; the in-RAM base case of the DCS recursion and of n <= N_base, about 181 MB; the value E6-E8 ran with). (3) **1b.7a authorised** (the first of 1b.7a-e of section 4.5; forecast 3-5 hours and 0.8-1.2 M tokens, +-50 %, stated before the launch); each of 1b.7b-e needs its own stated cost and yes.
* **Owner decisions 2026-11-18, the container format (the four gaps found by 1b.7b, section 5a; the owner chose the orchestrator's recommendation in each case):** (1) **TEXT codec 4 layout as the verified prototype**: `PARAM0` = b, `ELEM_WIDTH` = 0, one big-endian bit stream, 64 zero padding bytes inside the payload, `LENGTH = ceil(n*b/8) + 64`, `ALPHABET` required. (2) **`TEXT_SHA256` = SHA-256 of the code units as bytes for codecs 1, 2 and 4, UTF-16LE for codec 3.** (3) **The symbol counts get an own optional section, kind 13 `COUNTS`** (`uint64` per symbol, sum = n; kind 12 stays reserved for `PSI`). (4) **`b = ceil(log2 sigma)`** (3 bits for sigma 5-8; the text of Q3 in section 6a is corrected). ADR-003 is amended in the same commit (its sections 3, 4 and 6); the container writer of 1b.7e implements the amended text.

Local: PROGRESS-S1.md (top), ADR-001, ADR-002, ADR-003, BENCHMARK.md (grep), PROCESS.md rules, `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa` (file list, `Buf`, `NativeArena`, `LcpKernel` signatures), `analysis/bench/gen/gen_inputs.py`.
